41.1 Implementation sequence and release dependency
Start by approving data purposes, the manual-deletion invariant, role matrix and measurement definitions. Next implement restricted context storage, trusted IP handling and durable audit; then campaign capture and form/chat context references. Deliver content trash/restore/purge controls before installing cleanup jobs or importing production blogs. Add controlled experiments only after conversion and assignment evidence are reliable.
The chatbot follows shared identity/consent/lead contracts; its generative adapter is not allowed to invent a parallel data pipeline. Complete the owner interfaces, integration diagnostics and independent audit export before production acceptance. Chapter 31's illustrative core schedule must be re-estimated for these additions; they are required scope in the v1.1 profile, not invisible work assumed to fit the previous estimate.
41.2 One end-to-end demonstration
Use synthetic data and a test campaign. A visitor reaches a tagged landing page, makes a consent choice, receives an eligible variant, views the pricing marker, clicks a resource, opens chat, optionally shares location for a stated service task, explicitly submits contact details, and receives confirmation after the lead transaction commits. Staff qualifies the lead and observes eligible conversion delivery with consistent event identities.
Show the permitted journey in the owner console with acquisition provenance, actual exposure, submission source, service-area result and CRM state. Demonstrate the alternative path with analytics/ads refused: the page, manual location choice, chat and lead capture still work, but no prohibited tracking or reconstructed earlier journey appears. Show that the model and generic analytics never receive raw IP, exact position or transcript simply because staff can access them. Any permitted advertising network identifier must pass the separate destination-specific purpose, policy and final-payload checks in Chapter 69; exact coordinates and transcripts remain excluded.
Next, an authorized editor manually trashes a test blog with a reason. Show the actor/timestamp, public/search/bot invalidation and owner notification. Run every scheduled cleanup job at a future date and prove the trashed source remains. Restore it to draft and republish deliberately. Demonstrate a denied purge, then an independently approved synthetic purge, its audit evidence, and post-restore deletion-ledger reconciliation.
41.3 Owner operating runbooks
41.4 Completion and cost additions
Budget for experiment design/statistical review, context and bot testing, local geo-database maintenance or API use, conversation storage and model tokens, human support, audit ingestion/export/verification, protected storage, restore exercises and ongoing policy reviews. Model-based answers, map/geocoding, replay and third-party SDKs must have independent limits and failure modes. No current vendor price is implied by this master specification.
A module is complete only when frontend, API, database, authorization, logging, failure behavior, owner operation, tests and documentation are delivered together. Use Appendix G's acceptance IDs alongside Appendix B. Required capabilities cannot be marked Not Applicable simply because implementation was skipped; public data collection may remain intentionally off until the site's processing basis and configuration are approved.
Version 1.2 integration
The v1.2 expansion continues in Chapters 42–65. Its additional owner, marketing, SEO, paid-media, security and technical requirements are integrated through Chapters 63–64 and Appendices H–L. Original v1.1 demonstrations remain required.
42 | Completeness, applicability and gap-closure contract
42.1 What version 1.2 adds
This consolidated edition retains Chapters 01–41 and Appendices A–G. It extends them with owner-operated marketing automation, scoring and segmentation, audience synchronization, safer CMS editing and custom collections, deliverability, domain continuity, channel attribution, deeper SEO and paid-media operations, and additional technical and industry controls. The additions are proposed implementation requirements, not claims that a website or advertising account has already passed them. References distinguish inherited guidance, newly consulted official material, and account-level verification links.
“Complete” means every applicable requirement has an accountable owner, an implemented workflow, security/privacy controls, failure handling, operational documentation, and acceptance evidence. It does not mean installing every business module on every site. A medical booking site, a school website and a retail store need different activated workflows. None should silently omit a requirement because it is difficult, or display an unimplemented feature as if it works.
42.2 Three delivery classifications
For an individual website, a module may be out of scope without disappearing from the master catalogue. Record activation separately from implementation status: a fully implemented audience connector can remain disabled because the business is ineligible. “Provider unavailable,” “not implemented,” “not applicable,” and “implemented but not activated” are different states.
42.3 Closure of the previously identified gaps
These are specification-gap closures. They become implementation-gap closures only when the corresponding tests pass against the deployed system. Appendices H and I extend the requirement register; do not mark their rows Passed merely because this document was delivered.
42.4 Requirement traceability and conflict precedence
Each project keeps a machine-readable register with requirement_id, edition, classification, applicable, rationale, owner, dependencies, UI route, API use case, data entities, event IDs, privacy classification, tests, evidence, status and review date. Each activated capability must resolve all these fields. Legal obligations and provider restrictions cannot be waived by a convenience exception.
The current site-specific legal decision controls lawful processing; the protected-content rules in Chapter 38 control source-blog preservation; verified provider contracts control external payloads; the current edition's explicit expansion controls additional implementation requirements. An apparent conflict is a recorded architecture decision, not permission to choose the least restrictive paragraph. Retained references to v1.1 describe the inherited minimum; they do not limit the v1.2 scope.
42.5 No-omission review and change process
Review the complete catalogue at discovery, architecture approval, prelaunch and significant business change. Ask whether a new feature changes customer obligations, public URLs, editorial data, personal-data destinations, advertising goals, cost or recovery. Introduce a new requirement when the answer exposes a gap. Keep a dated decision log so the standard evolves instead of claiming to anticipate every future browser, law, industry or platform feature.
The delivery team must maintain one source of truth for requirements and one for business facts. No spreadsheet, tag container, AI prompt or website builder may override authoritative payment, pricing, consent, publication or permission rules. The owner signs the selected capability profile; engineering and marketing sign their evidence; unresolved release blockers remain visible.
43 | Marketing strategy, offers and growth planning
43.1 Discovery before acquisition spending
Create a marketing brief defining the audience's real problem, purchase journey, buying roles, objections, alternatives, service area, languages, evidence, sales cycle and desired outcome. Use approved interviews, sales/support questions and first-party research. A persona is a planning aid, not an excuse to infer sensitive attributes from an IP address or transcript. Record which findings are observed and which are hypotheses.
Build an offer register shared by the CMS, forms, chatbot, campaigns and sales team. Each offer has offer_id, owner, eligible products/services, audience description, value proposition, inclusions, exclusions, truthful proof, terms revision, start/end instants, service capacity, contact route, price source and review date. Campaign copy references approved offer revisions. An expired promotion stops being advertised or displayed under its approved rule without automatically deleting its source article.
43.2 Funnel and content planning
Each priority audience gets a journey map showing entrance, question, proof, next action, fallback and staff responsibility. Do not force every reader into a gated resource or make consent acceptance a conversion goal. Use user research to identify friction before adding more tracking or popups.
43.3 Budget and economics model
Define value and cost consistently before showing ROI. Keep ad spend, agency fees, creative production, communication, incentives, platform charges and internal labor separately visible. Revenue is not profit. Record whether reported revenue excludes taxes, refunds, discounts and cancellations. Report realized value separately from forecast lifetime value.
Use explicit formulas: cost per qualified lead = attributable acquisition cost / qualified leads in the chosen matured cohort; customer acquisition cost = declared acquisition costs / new customers under the chosen definition; ROAS = attributed revenue / ad spend. A zero denominator produces not_available, not infinity or a false zero. Explain attribution windows and lag. A high ROAS on a small remarketing group is not proof that acquisition spending can scale proportionally.
Approve campaign budgets, maximum acceptable exposure, support capacity, forecast assumptions and a stop/review threshold. Forecast low/base/high scenarios; do not promise traffic, ranking or revenue. Spending authority is separate from permission to edit a landing page. Forecasts should show uncertainty and the date/source of inputs.
43.4 Campaign and creative operating workflow
The owner creates a campaign brief, selects an approved offer and destination, names a conversion goal, assigns source/medium/campaign IDs, prepares creatives, previews mobile placements, requests review and schedules launch. Content, legal/claim, tracking and budget approval are distinct checks. Store campaign status, owner, dates, creative rights, source files, copy versions, target regions, channels and measurement settings.
Maintain a shared calendar for articles, webinars, offers, newsletters, paid campaigns and important operational dates. Calendar changes must not publish drafts or send campaigns accidentally. A campaign stop action must describe which external accounts are paused, which messages remain queued and what still requires manual action. A local pause is not proof an external ad platform stopped delivery.
43.5 Brand, reputation and distribution
Provide a brand kit with approved logos, typography, colors, tone, claim library, legal entity names, licensed assets and social-preview templates. Use genuine author/team records and real customer permissions. Reviews and case studies must not be invented, selectively manipulated to misrepresent feedback, or published under fabricated identities. Review invitations, responses and removal requests need a documented moderation policy and the relevant platform/legal review. FTC guidance distinguishes consumer reviews from promotional testimonials and describes restrictions on deceptive practices; apply the actual jurisdiction and platform rules. [R98]
Support selected organic distribution channels through approved adapters or documented external workflows: email, social publishing, partnerships, directories, events, press and community. Track distribution effort and earned outcomes without purchasing manipulative links or scraped contact lists. Social platform publishing, influencer disclosure, affiliate rewards and prize promotions each require their own approval and current policy check.
43.6 Implementation and acceptance
Implement offer, campaign, creative and editorial-calendar entities with revisions and references to CMS resources. The owner dashboard links each campaign to destination health, approval state, lead quality, cost and operational alerts. The API prevents publication of an unapproved or unavailable offer and audits changes to claims, dates and budgets.
Acceptance demonstrates a campaign from brief to a real synthetic lead and staff response; a promotion expiry that retains source content; a conflicting price rejected before launch; and a report whose totals reconcile to the defined business records. The operating handbook assigns a reviewer and backup for every active campaign.
44 | Advanced owner CMS, collections and editing safety
44.1 Autosave, recovery and editing states
Deliver an editor that distinguishes local unsaved changes, saving, server-acknowledged draft, conflict, offline state, failed save, approved revision and live content. Autosave uses a documented bounded debounce and a manual Save action. A visible save indicator changes to saved only after the server commits the draft version. Network failure must preserve the editor's recoverable local state under an approved storage policy rather than falsely confirm a save.
Provide undo/redo for the editor session, unsaved-change protection and recoverable drafts after an interrupted session. Do not store secrets, private lead records or sensitive unpublished content indefinitely in browser storage. Local draft persistence requires a defined expiry, logout clearing, device-sharing warning where appropriate, and server-side authorization on recovery. A restored draft never auto-publishes.
44.2 Concurrency, comments and approvals
Use optimistic concurrency with an expected version or If-Match contract. When two editors modify the same draft, reject the stale write and show a readable comparison. Offer reload, duplicate-as-draft or a reviewed merge; never silently overwrite the newer acknowledged revision. Presence indicators and edit leases may improve usability but are not sufficient integrity controls.
Support editorial comments, reviewer assignments, approval reasons, task status and notifications. Comments are private by default and do not leak through the public content API or chatbot. Real-time coauthoring is an optional implementation choice requiring tested conflict resolution, offline reconciliation and author attribution. Basic lost-update protection is mandatory even without coauthoring.
44.3 Controlled custom collections and relationships
The comprehensive owner platform includes a content-model builder for approved informational collections such as people, locations, events, partners and case studies. Owners may add fields from an allowlist: plain/rich text, number, boolean, dates, media, controlled select, reference and bounded repeating groups. Each field has a stable key, type, validation, localized flag, sensitivity and permissions. Use metadata-defined schemas over controlled storage; do not allow arbitrary production SQL or executable expressions.
A collection requires a list/detail template binding, route policy, preview, publication workflow, search/index policy, permissions and export contract. Relationships enforce allowed target types and same-site scope. A public reference cannot expose a private record. A new collection is not equivalent to implementing payment, inventory or eligibility business rules; those remain domain modules.
Schema changes create versions. Adding an optional field can be compatible; changing type, deleting a field, making it required or altering a relationship needs a migration preview and explicit approval. Retain historical published revisions and their renderability. A field deletion must not silently discard data in existing entries. Prevent circular references, unbounded nesting and expensive public joins.
44.4 Import/export and mass operations
Imports support approved CSV/JSON or an explicit source adapter, field mapping, dry-run validation, duplicate detection, conflict policy, asset mapping and row-level results. Use source IDs and import manifests; do not match solely on display titles. Default behavior is create/update, not replacement of the complete collection. Existing blogs and revisions cannot disappear because an import file omits them.
A reviewed import has a bounded manifest, owner, expected versions, audit ID and resumable execution. Handle malformed files, formula-like spreadsheet values, oversized objects, unsafe remote media URLs and partial failures. Report created, updated, skipped, conflicted and failed counts separately. Rollback reverses only eligible changes from that import and must not erase later edits. Exports preserve IDs, references, revisions, locale links and asset manifests, with permissions and private-field redaction.
44.5 Coordinated releases and reusable design
Allow a release bundle to pin approved page, menu, banner, collection and resource revisions. Preview the bundle as a coherent snapshot. Validate routes, claims, prices, dependencies, accessibility and affected campaigns. Publish by changing a site release pointer or another explicit consistency mechanism; record which resources became live. CDN/search/feed invalidation is asynchronous, so show pending/partial/complete status instead of pretending every external cache switches atomically.
Provide rollback to a compatible release manifest without reversing customer transactions. Scheduled releases retain UTC instants and the owner's timezone. A release cannot bypass blog-trash/purge approval. The page builder supports reusable sections, global components, approved responsive presets, accessible drag alternatives and design tokens; owners can inspect the impact of shared changes before publication.
The authoring interface should itself be accessible and help authors produce accessible output; ATAG addresses both concerns. Use that guidance alongside the existing WCAG target, not as a claim of automatic conformance. [R67]
44.6 Data, APIs and acceptance
Add collection_definitions, collection_schema_versions, content_entries, entry_revisions, draft_sessions, editorial_comments, release_manifests, release_items, import_runs and import_row_results. Public queries read only approved published snapshots; admin writes require membership, field permissions and expected versions. Appendix J gives representative contracts.
Tests cover acknowledged-save recovery, stale edits, shared-device logout, schema migration, mixed private/public references, malicious imports, partial release invalidation and rollback. The owner independently creates an Events collection using an approved template, imports synthetic entries, corrects one error, previews a release and publishes it without developer intervention.
Final edition integration: Chapters 66.4 and 73 strengthen derivative metadata, editor recovery and accessible owner workflows.
45 | Visual marketing automation and durable workflow execution
45.1 Owner workflow and node catalogue
Provide a visual builder with trigger, condition, delay, branch, action and exit nodes. Each workflow has a stable ID, immutable published version, owner, purpose, eligibility, enrollment/re-enrollment policy, exclusion conditions, timezone, expiry/review date, execution budget and stop conditions. Owners create drafts, simulate with synthetic cases, request approval, publish, pause, inspect runs and retire versions.
45.2 Enrollment and state machine
Use draft → reviewed → published → paused → retired for definitions, and enrolled → running → waiting → completed/cancelled/failed for runs. Pin the workflow version at enrollment. A definition update affects new runs unless a reviewed migration explicitly moves existing runs. A pause policy must say whether it prevents enrollment, dispatch, timers or all three; an emergency stop prevents new external effects immediately at the dispatch boundary.
Deduplicate enrollment by site, workflow version, subject, trigger event and declared re-entry scope. A customer can have multiple legitimate enquiries, so choose lead/case/order/contact as the subject deliberately. Never merge all cases because email matches. Bound loops, total actions, duration, graph size, fan-out and nested branches. Detect unreachable nodes and missing exits before publication.
45.3 Durable scheduling and safe external effects
Persist workflow runs, node state, timer due_at, input references and operation IDs in PostgreSQL or an equivalently durable workflow system. Workers use leases, bounded batches, deadlines and retry backoff. Commit domain transitions, audit and outbox atomically. The queue is transport, not the only record of a scheduled customer obligation.
Every action has a stable execution key. Before sending, re-read consent/suppression, current case status, recipient binding, quiet hours, frequency caps, template approval, provider capability and remaining budget. A stale segment snapshot must not override a later opt-out. Record policy/version and the eligibility result. Where a provider's request result is uncertain, reconcile by supported identifiers before blindly resending. Do not claim exactly-once delivery across external services.
Apply rate and cost budgets per tenant, channel and recipient. Reserve quotas transactionally where concurrent workers might exceed them. A safety pause must not discard pending acknowledged leads. Keep failed-action review and restricted retry tools with original identities and current eligibility checks.
45.4 Reference workflows
Lead follow-up: accepted enquiry → route to qualified team → transactional acknowledgment → wait for response deadline → staff reminder/escalation → stop when contacted or closed. Marketing nurture is a separate branch only for an eligible subscription; an enquiry does not automatically enter it.
Webinar: registration committed → confirmation → approved reminders → attendance status from a trusted source → permitted resource delivery → optional follow-up under the applicable permission. Do not classify opening a reminder as actual attendance.
Commerce: verified order → fulfillment updates; abandoned-checkout follow-up requires a separately approved contact/permission basis and a declared eligibility window. Do not capture abandoned form keystrokes or invent an email address. A completed purchase, opt-out or suppressed contact cancels further recovery messages.
45.5 Simulation, operator controls and evidence
Simulation uses fixtures and a virtual clock, shows selected branches, scheduled times, message previews, exclusions and estimated cost, and cannot reach production destinations. Staff can inspect a safe execution timeline showing who published a workflow, why enrollment occurred, what was sent, what remains and why a node failed. Record edits and manual intervention without exposing message bodies in generic logs.
Test restart during a delay, repeated trigger, concurrent worker claims, opt-out immediately before dispatch, provider timeout with uncertain acceptance, definition changes during a run, daylight-saving changes and emergency stopping. Acceptance requires no duplicate business action from retries and no prohibited send after the dispatch gate observes a disqualifying state.
45.6 Implementation contract
Add automation_definitions, automation_versions, automation_runs, node_executions, timers, enrollment_keys, action_reservations and workflow_events. NestJS owns policy and business commands; workers execute bounded actions. A third-party orchestration engine is replaceable behind the workflow contract. Owner export includes definitions and safe execution metadata, not reusable provider secrets.
46 | Lead scoring, segments and responsible personalization
46.1 Separate business concepts
A behavioral engagement score, a declared customer fit, a verified qualification and an advertising conversion are different concepts. Store and display them separately. The system must explain a score rather than present a mysterious AI number as business truth. No score may deny essential services, determine protected eligibility or infer sensitive characteristics by default.
The owner defines the business question, eligible inputs, rules, thresholds, review frequency and staff actions. Start with simple deterministic rules before introducing predictive models. Examples of approved evidence include an explicitly selected program, requested callback or recorded staff qualification. Raw IP, precise coordinates, transcript-derived health information and guessed wealth are not default scoring features.
46.2 Rule and score model
A scoring version contains rule IDs, input schema, positive/negative weights, per-event caps, repeated-action handling, recency/decay, missing-value behavior, exclusion rules and maximum/minimum bounds. Store the score as a derived snapshot with calculated_at, source window, rule_version and safe explanation contributions. Keep immutable business events and recompute derived scores after legitimate correction.
Do not give unlimited points for repeated clicks or messages. Exclude synthetic tests, recognized abuse and duplicate events under documented rules. A lack of telemetry after tracking refusal is unknown, not proof of low interest. Keep score decay separate from deletion of business records. Manual override requires a reason, actor and expiry; the UI shows both computed and overridden values.
46.3 Segment builder and membership lifecycle
Provide typed AND/OR conditions, inclusion/exclusion groups, time windows and a preview of eligible counts with data freshness. Support static reviewed lists and dynamic segments. Each segment has owner, purpose, version, permitted destinations, sensitive-field restrictions, membership window and refresh schedule. Preview counts are not guaranteed provider-match counts.
Maintain membership_added, membership_removed, expired and suppressed states with reason/version. Deletion, consent withdrawal, account changes and corrected CRM state must propagate to eligible derived membership. Record calculated population and uncollected/unknown categories. Bound query cost and prevent segments from reading unauthorized fields or crossing sites.
46.4 Personalization rules and cache isolation
Personalization may choose an approved language, visitor-selected topic, relevant CTA or service-area information. Use explicit business goals, control experience, precedence, compatibility with experiments and a kill switch. An essential location selector is not an advertising profile. Avoid sensitive profiling, covert price discrimination and manipulative pressure.
Server-rendered personalized content must use bounded safe cache keys or bypass shared caching. Never cache personal names, contact details, authenticated recommendations or Set-Cookie responses into public pages. Keep the crawler/no-state fallback available to ordinary users; do not cloak. Distinguish personalization assignment from actual exposure. During A/B tests, freeze or record relevant personalization rules to avoid confounding the result.
46.5 Predictive scoring, only after a separate approval
A model-based scorer needs training-data lineage, outcome labels, consent/purpose review, temporal train/test separation, leakage checks, calibration, false-positive/negative evaluation, drift monitoring, explanation policy and rollback. A high historical accuracy result does not justify automatic consequential customer decisions. Humans must be able to review and correct the outcome.
Keep model features and outputs out of advertising destinations unless explicitly reviewed and supported. A prompt-generated interpretation of a chat is not verified qualification. Do not secretly enrich a contact using purchased identity data. Predictive scoring is a conditional enhancement; deterministic scoring and human review remain operational without a model.
46.6 Data and acceptance
Add score_definitions, score_versions, score_snapshots, score_contributions, score_overrides, segment_definitions, segment_versions and segment_memberships. Expose explain, preview, simulate, publish and recalculate use cases with permissions, costs and audit. Segment exports require destination authorization and field minimization.
Acceptance proves repeat-click caps, missing telemetry, rule-version changes, withdrawn eligibility, correction/recalculation, manual overrides, site isolation and personalization cache isolation. A high score must not emit lead_qualified until the separately defined qualification transition occurs.
47 | Email authentication, deliverability and subscription operations
47.1 Sender readiness and ownership
The business owns its sending domains, DNS records, provider account, billing and recovery. Maintain a sender register with purpose, From identity, Reply-To, return-path domain, provider, permitted templates, volume assumptions, DNS records, verification date and operational owner. Separate marketing and transactional streams where useful, with an intentional reputation and recovery strategy.
Gmail documents different requirements for all senders and bulk senders. Its bulk-sender requirements include SPF, DKIM, DMARC and one-click unsubscribe for marketing/subscribed messages; sender alignment and delivery quality also matter. Validate the current provider requirements for the actual volume and message category, rather than treating a successful SMTP/API response as proof of deliverability. [R62]
47.2 Authentication implementation and safe DNS change
Configure SPF for the actual permitted senders; inventory existing records before changing DNS. Enable DKIM and verify the received signature and domain alignment. Deploy DMARC with an approved monitoring-to-enforcement plan after reviewing legitimate mail sources; process reports without exposing customer information. Record key rotation, removed providers and rollback. Do not blindly copy a sample TXT record or publish competing SPF policies.
Use verified sender addresses and monitored replies. Set the website visitor's email as an appropriate Reply-To only after validation; do not forge it as the sending domain's From identity. Protect inbound mail/webhooks and attachment handling. Avoid loading remote tracking content merely because staff inspect a received message.
47.3 Subscription and preference lifecycle
Use explicit subscription states: pending_confirmation where applicable, subscribed, unsubscribed, suppressed, bounced and complained. Record channel/purpose, notice version, source, confirmation evidence and state changes. Optional double opt-in is a project choice or applicable requirement, not a universal legal claim. A user can request a service acknowledgment without accepting promotional mail.
Provide an accessible preference center and a direct unsubscribe path without forcing account creation. Unsubscribe should suppress eligible future marketing promptly under the approved legal/provider schedule, including actions already queued. A later import or CRM sync must not resubscribe a suppressed contact without valid new authorization. Transactional messages require their own purpose test and must not disguise promotional content as necessary service communication.
47.4 One-click unsubscribe and link handling
Where required, implement the List-Unsubscribe and List-Unsubscribe-Post headers using the RFC 8058 HTTPS POST mechanism. The endpoint uses an opaque, hard-to-forge reference, needs no login or extra cookies, and performs an idempotent subscription action. DKIM must cover the required headers. A link scanner's GET request must not accidentally unsubscribe a recipient; the visible email link may lead to a simple human-facing preference page. [R63]
Keep unsubscribe tokens out of analytics, application logs and referrers. Confirmation pages reveal no contact identity. Expiry and token rotation must not make legally required unsubscribing impossible; provide a fallback route and suitable durable suppression identity. Do not add tracking redirects that break the provider's one-click contract.
47.5 Template, sending and feedback workflows
Owners edit approved templates with typed variables, localization, HTML/plain-text alternatives, accessible structure, working links and test-send controls. Escape inserted data, reject missing critical variables and prevent arbitrary template code. Keep legal footer and required identity/contact fields under controlled editing. Preview dark-mode/image-blocked/mobile behavior where used and never make a message understandable only through an image.
Send through a durable outbox with stable message IDs and provider receipts. Track queued, attempted, provider_accepted, delivered where confirmed, delayed, bounced, complained and suppressed separately. Opens and clicks are imperfect engagement observations, not proof of reading or a human response. Apply opt-out, bounce/complaint suppression, quiet hours, contact-frequency caps and per-provider budgets before dispatch.
Alert on authentication failures, delivery degradation, abnormal complaint/bounce rates, queue age and provider quota issues. Define a review-based ramp-up plan for legitimate sending; never buy lists or rotate domains to evade restrictions. Suppress hard failures appropriately while permitting reviewed correction of an incorrectly entered address.
47.6 Acceptance and recovery
Test actual received headers, DMARC/DKIM/SPF outcomes, replies, one-click POST, harmless scanner GET, visible unsubscribe, reimport suppression, provider callback authenticity, duplicate sends and interrupted delivery. Show the owner a failed-delivery queue and a safe correction workflow. Test provider replacement without losing templates, suppression history or queued obligations; do not automatically resend messages whose prior acceptance is uncertain.
48 | Omnichannel inbox, calls and offline acquisition
48.1 One business case, multiple controlled channels
Deliver the unified-inbox capability in the comprehensive reusable platform. Activate only selected web chat, email, WhatsApp, SMS, Telegram and telephony providers for an individual site. Every channel uses the same case/lead model but retains its own provider identifiers, permissions, delivery states, rate limits and identity evidence. An email address, phone number, chat ID and website session are not automatically interchangeable authenticated identities.
The owner configures teams, business hours, queues, routing, escalation, assignment, canned replies, approved templates, tags, private notes and closure reasons. Prevent two agents from sending conflicting replies without seeing one another's activity. Distinguish assignment, first human reply, resolution and customer satisfaction; bot greetings do not satisfy a human-response service level.
48.2 Channel adapter contract
Telegram's Bot API provides explicit messaging/update mechanisms; implement the selected webhook/polling contract, protect credentials and collect contacts only through supported deliberate interaction. WhatsApp's policy requires appropriate notices/permissions and respecting opt-outs; the actual messaging and calling configuration must be checked before activation. [R103] [R104]
48.3 Call attribution and recording boundaries
For call-led businesses, decide whether attribution uses a fixed campaign number, approved dynamic number insertion or staff-declared source. Dynamic insertion needs a licensed number pool, lease/expiry policy, fallback number, source binding, concurrency protection and rules for a call arriving after the visitor session ends. Keep permanent business contact details accurate and prevent number swapping from producing misleading public identity or inaccessible links.
Link a connected call to a lead only through verified provider evidence and the approved matching policy. Shared numbers, forwarded calls and blocked caller ID produce uncertainty. Call duration is not qualification; staff or an approved business rule records the outcome separately. Record callback requests distinctly from completed calls.
Recording, transcription and AI scoring are off until their purpose, notices/consent, jurisdiction, retention and staff access have been approved. Redact sensitive content, protect recordings in private storage and prevent transcripts from entering generic analytics or ad payloads. A browser cannot silently capture ordinary telephone calls. Provider capabilities and device permissions must be tested, not assumed.
48.4 Cross-channel handoff and offline entry
Website-to-messaging handoff uses a short-lived scoped reference or a visitor-confirmed link, not raw contact details, precise location, transcript text or a reusable login token in a public URL. Verify the receiving channel's user before disclosing private account data. Carry eligible campaign/case context by server reference and keep declared source separate from observed source.
Support QR links, event codes, partner referrals, physical brochures and manually entered campaign codes. A QR code identifies a link or placement, not a named person. Record campaign IDs through the same sanitized acquisition service; avoid creating permanent tracking identifiers for people who decline optional collection. Staff can record “How did you hear about us?” as declared attribution without overwriting observed evidence.
48.5 Reliability, support and data model
Add channel_accounts, channel_identities, inbox_cases, conversations, agent_assignments, messages, provider_receipts, delivery_events, call_sessions, number_leases and case_links. Webhooks verify signatures or provider-specific secrets, persist receipts, deduplicate and handle out-of-order states. A late delivered status must not overwrite a later failed/cancelled business decision without a defined rule.
Provider outage shows delayed/unavailable states and fallback contact options. Disconnect stops future sends, revokes credentials where possible and preserves required case history under its retention policy. Explicitly test account revocation, reconnect, pagination/backfill, duplicate inbound messages, cross-tenant mapping and a case involving more than one channel.
48.6 Owner acceptance
Demonstrate a tagged website lead, human handoff, real supported channel reply and CRM outcome using synthetic recipients. Also demonstrate a missed call, invalid callback signature, unknown caller/source, opted-out recipient, duplicated delivery event and unauthorized transcript access. The final report must show what was observed, declared, inferred or unavailable rather than manufacture a complete person-level omnichannel journey.
49 | SEO strategy, editorial quality, local discovery and reputation
49.1 Search demand and intent architecture
Create a versioned search-intent map connecting customer questions to page purpose, target audience, offer, language, evidence and next action. Research real search results and first-party Search Console data; record the date, geography and uncertainty of third-party estimates. Keywords are inputs to content planning, not a mandate to repeat phrases unnaturally. Assign one clear primary purpose to each important page while supporting useful related questions.
Map topic relationships and internal links before generating content. Distinguish navigational, informational, comparison, transactional and local intents. Investigate overlapping pages before consolidation; low traffic alone is not deletion authority. Maintain an editorial inventory with ownership, review dates, quality issues, related offers, index policy and update decisions.
49.2 Content production and evidence workflow
A brief specifies the reader's task, unique contribution, factual sources, original experience or expertise, necessary illustrations, accessibility requirements and the next useful action. Use real authors/reviewers and accurate qualifications. Avoid fixed word-count or keyword-density targets masquerading as ranking requirements. A well-supported short answer can be more useful than padded text.
Draft → factual/subject review → rights/accessibility review → SEO/UX review → approved publication → performance review. Record sources, permission for quotes/images, changed claims and substantive correction dates. AI may assist drafting or research but does not establish facts, author experience or publication approval. Google emphasizes helpful, people-first content and prohibits manipulative practices including scaled content abuse. [R06] [R07]
49.3 On-page and trust requirements
Every indexable template needs a meaningful title, useful description, coherent heading structure, clear main content, crawlable internal links, descriptive media, appropriate canonical/index settings and supported structured data derived from visible facts. Titles and descriptions may be rewritten by search engines; do not promise exact search-result appearance. Avoid duplicating identical text across city/product/program pages merely to occupy more queries.
Provide accurate business identity, contact routes, terms, relevant pricing/fees, authorship, service limitations and substantiated proof. Keep comparison pages fair and dated. A review or accreditation badge needs a source and current permission; no fabricated awards, customer counts, pass rates or guarantees. Maintain a correction/takedown workflow with source preservation and audit according to Chapter 38.
49.4 Local and international programmes
For genuine eligible locations, manage business profiles, categories, opening/special hours, contact details, service areas and destination links under business-owned accounts. Ensure the website and public profiles describe the same real operations. Google Business Profile guidelines govern eligibility and representation; a set of artificial addresses is not a legitimate location strategy. [R87]
Location pages should contain meaningful local details: actual services, directions/accessibility, staff or accountable contacts where appropriate, coverage limitations and useful local questions. Support user-selected location and locale corrections. For multiple languages, use reviewed translations, localized metadata, reciprocal locale relationships and appropriate hreflang; do not force a redirect only because IP geography suggests a country. Measure each language's task success and support readiness.
49.5 Earned discovery, reputation and AI search
Plan ethical distribution: original research, useful tools, relevant partnerships, genuine press, resource citations and community participation. Maintain a backlink/referral issue register and investigate suspicious activity without buying links or automating indiscriminate disavowal. Link qualification for paid or user-generated links belongs in the publishing policy. Social sharing metadata and consistent entity information help presentation but are not guaranteed ranking factors.
AI search visibility should rely on accessible, accurate, well-organized published information and evidence. Google's guidance does not require a special AI-only schema or text file for its AI search features. Treat any optional machine-readable resource as a documented interface, not a ranking promise. Distinguish AI-answer observations, referral traffic and business outcomes; do not claim person-level AI impression attribution from ordinary web analytics. [R11]
49.6 Measurement and ongoing improvement
Build separate views for brand/non-brand queries where defensibly classified, landing pages, device, locale, geography, index state and downstream outcomes. Report query-data omissions and limits: Search Console extraction does not guarantee every detailed query row, and privacy/aggregation rules can affect totals. Preserve source query dimensions and extraction metadata rather than forcing arithmetic equality with GA4. [R105]
Review content decay, cannibalization, internal-search failures, broken references and customer objections. Use a documented refresh/consolidation decision, with redirect and dependency impact analysis. SEO work produces reviewed backlog items and measured experiments; no script may automatically delete articles because rankings fall. Test template-level changes against a representative crawl and actual rendered content before release.
Final edition integration: Chapter 66 adds current search-surface eligibility and aggregate generative reporting; Chapter 71 completes distribution/lifecycle operations.
50 | Technical SEO, crawl control and search-surface engineering
50.1 Route-level indexability contract
Maintain a route policy registry covering canonical public pages, campaign variants, locale pages, category/tag archives, filtered lists, internal search, pagination, downloads, media, previews, accounts and error states. Each policy specifies status, canonical, robots directives, sitemap inclusion, authentication, crawlable links, rendering method and structured-data eligibility. CMS publication cannot bypass this registry.
Indexable pages must expose the intended main content, links and metadata in stable HTML/rendered output. Validate server response and rendered DOM, not only the browser screenshot. Use real redirects and meaningful 404/410/5xx states; a universal 200 response is not a valid error strategy. Keep private/draft content behind authorization. robots.txt controls crawler behavior, not confidentiality, and blocking a URL can prevent a crawler from seeing its noindex instruction.
50.2 URL normalization, faceting and pagination
Define canonical hostname, HTTPS, trailing-slash policy, case/encoding behavior and approved query parameters. Do not normalize case-sensitive resource IDs or signed tokens blindly. Test redirects with eligible UTMs/click IDs, locale routing and A/B assignment. Canonicals and sitemaps use clean intended URLs rather than campaign identifiers. Reject open redirects and unsafe destination parameters.
For filters, identify a finite useful set of indexable landing pages; constrain irrelevant parameter combinations and crawl traps. Decide separately whether URLs should be crawlable and indexable. Google documents the crawl cost of faceted navigation; canonical signals alone should not be treated as a complete crawl-volume control. Malformed or meaningless filter combinations need an intentional response and useful user recovery. [R83]
Provide crawlable links to paginated results. Infinite scroll or Load more must have an accessible URL/link strategy and preserve browser back/forward behavior. Paginated pages normally identify their own content; do not point every page's canonical to page one simply to hide pagination. Google does not generally click buttons to discover more results, so essential discovery cannot depend only on interaction. [R84]
50.3 Sitemaps, resources and HTTP behavior
Generate sitemaps from approved canonical publication state. Partition large collections and use sitemap indexes under current limits; keep modification dates truthful and exclude private, redirected, trashed or intentionally nonindexable URLs. Validate XML escaping, absolute URLs, host ownership and incremental publication/removal. A submitted sitemap is a discovery signal, not guaranteed indexing. [R97] RSS/Atom feeds may distribute public content where useful; retain stable item identifiers and do not expose drafts.
Downloads have an explicit indexing/access policy. Public PDFs can provide searchable content with an accessible HTML summary; protected resources require real authorization. Use appropriate content type, safe Content-Disposition, and headers such as X-Robots-Tag where suitable. Test range requests, expired links, conditional requests and cache headers without implying that a delivery response proves reading. The HTTP contract should follow the semantics of the selected status codes and validators. [R99]
50.4 Structured data and specialized search surfaces
Use a controlled schema registry: Organization, LocalBusiness, BreadcrumbList, Article/BlogPosting, Product/Offer, Event, JobPosting, VideoObject and other actually applicable types. Schema.org's vocabulary is broader than Google's supported rich-result features. Validate both syntax and current search-feature policies; valid markup does not guarantee a rich result. Generated values must match visible, authoritative content, especially ratings, prices, availability, dates and business identity. [R08] [R102]
Do not promise FAQ rich results or a special AI-search result. The inherited baseline notes Google's 2026 FAQ change; recheck the current search-feature register at release. Event/job pages require real dates and lifecycle handling; a closed job should not remain falsely available in a feed or markup. Removal from public discovery must preserve the source under its approved content policy.
Images need descriptive context, meaningful alt text, usable resolution, stable public URLs where intended and discoverable image elements. CSS backgrounds are not a substitute for indexable image markup. Video pages need an accessible player, truthful title/description, stable thumbnail and clear main-video context where targeting video search. Captions/transcripts and video-sitemap/markup decisions belong in the media workflow. [R85] [R86]
Google's Indexing API is restricted to eligible JobPosting pages and BroadcastEvent embedded in VideoObject; it is not a general “instant index all blogs” API. Use ordinary discovery/sitemap processes for other content and never sell unsupported indexing guarantees. [R88]
50.5 Technical search monitoring and migration
Implement scheduled route crawls with bounded rates, representative rendered checks, redirect-loop/broken-link detection, canonical conflicts, missing metadata, sitemap discrepancies and accidental noindex alerts. Track server errors, crawl anomalies and publication lag. Review robots changes as high-impact configuration. Verify a crawler before granting special operational treatment; do not trust a user-agent string as authentication or show reviewers a different offer.
A redesign requires URL and asset inventory, old/new mapping, traffic and conversion baseline, backlink-critical routes, staged redirect tests, locale/structured-data checks, domain/Search Console ownership and a rollback plan. Preserve redirects for the approved migration period and monitor unresolved old URLs. A traffic drop needs investigation by affected templates/queries; no automatic mass rollback or article deletion based on a noisy daily chart.
50.6 Acceptance evidence
Deliver a route-policy export, crawl report, rendered HTML fixtures, sitemap validation, structured-data evidence, download-header tests and migration map. Test JavaScript disabled/degraded, long Unicode slugs, case/encoding differences, empty filters, large pagination, localized alternates, asset replacements and manual blog removal. Search Console and provider diagnostics are evidence inputs, not a substitute for the actual deployed route tests.
Final edition integration: Use Chapter 70 for edge-response, robots, routing, resource and migration edge cases.
51 | Google Ads operating specification and website integration
51.1 Separate website readiness, measurement and campaign management
Treat three workstreams independently: the website delivers a relevant working destination; the measurement system records eligible authoritative outcomes; the campaign operator configures targeting, bidding, creative and spend. A website can support excellent campaigns without controlling the auction. Google's displayed Quality Score is diagnostic, not an auction input or a guarantee of cheaper clicks. [R01] [R02]
The business owns the ad accounts, manager relationships, billing, recovery, Google Analytics, Tag Manager, Search Console and any Merchant Center assets. Document access by role, approved regions/business categories, advertiser verification status, conversion goals, timezone/currency and integration permissions. Separate read-only reporting from conversion delivery and campaign-write credentials. No content editor automatically receives spending authority.
51.2 Campaign planning and supported campaign families
Maintain a provider capability register and select campaign types according to the actual business and account. The catalogue should accommodate Search, Shopping/retail, Performance Max, Display, video/Demand Gen and other supported types without assuming identical controls. Legacy or newly introduced features require a dated support/migration decision. Website A/B tests and ad-platform campaign experiments remain separate experiments.
Do not force all campaign families into one generic UI that silently drops unsupported settings. A native lead-form submission can occur without a website visit: preserve provider lead/campaign evidence, but do not fabricate a page-view journey or a website cookie ID.
51.3 Search keywords, assets and destination relevance
Organize search campaigns by coherent intent and business economics, not an arbitrary number of keywords. Maintain keyword and negative-keyword lists with owner, reason, language and change history. Review available search-term reports, unsuitable intent, geographic mismatch and duplicate/overlapping coverage. Search-term visibility is not a complete record of every person's query.
Google's match types are not simple literal matching: exact match can include the same meaning or intent, and negative matching has distinct rules. Validate the actual selected campaign behavior and monitor qualified outcomes instead of assuming that a keyword label perfectly restricts traffic. [R72]
Prepare truthful responsive search assets and applicable sitelinks, callouts, structured snippets, calls, images, locations, promotions or prices. Verify current format limits in the account instead of freezing every changing character limit in this master. Every asset destination must work, match its promise and disclose material conditions. Keep creative revisions, rights and approvals. Avoid fabricated scarcity, unsupported superiority claims and false affiliations.
Landing pages must clearly deliver the advertised offer, work on mobile/in-app browsers, present contact/legal information and accept the promised action. Test redirects, query preservation, speed, form validation, consent states and the thank-you response. A location or keyword-personalized headline must be based on approved copy and cannot invent a branch or entitlement.
51.4 Bidding, budgets and automated features
Choose bidding around validated business goals and sufficient reliable feedback. Separate primary bidding conversions from diagnostic micro-events. Document counting, value, currency, acquisition vs retention objectives, lag and the impact of refunds/disqualification. More chat messages or brochure clicks must not silently replace qualified leads as the optimization target.
Smart Bidding and campaign controls vary by campaign type and current account support. Record the selected strategy, target semantics, value policy and observation window; avoid universal minimum-volume folklore or fixed learning-period promises. Average daily budgets are not necessarily hard per-day spending caps. Confirm current overdelivery/billing behavior and use explicit review/alert thresholds. [R73] [R74]
Where AI Max or other automated text/search/URL features are available, explicitly approve their activation. Define eligible destination routes, URL exclusions, brand/claim constraints, approved feed/content inputs and a review process for generated assets. AI Max documentation describes expanded matching and asset/URL automation; those features do not remove the advertiser's responsibility for accurate destinations and offers. Recheck actual API and account availability before building management controls. [R71]
Automated changes may propose budget or asset adjustments, but consequential account writes require a clear authority boundary, bounded limits and audit. A local budget alert cannot guarantee a remote platform stops before the next charge; do not label it an absolute spend cap unless the provider contract truly supplies one.
51.5 Conversion and offline-feedback implementation
The accepted lead/order/booking remains authoritative in the business database. Map each conversion action to a source, primary/secondary status, deduplication/counting rule, value policy and original occurrence time. Avoid double-primary counting through both a native tag and GA4 import. Keep test conversions away from production optimization wherever the provider supports it.
For new offline/enhanced-lead integrations, Google's current documentation directs the integration to Data Manager API. Its older UploadClickConversions guidance includes restrictions beginning in June 2026 for previously unused upload access. Select the currently supported credentials and ingestion contract rather than copying an old tutorial. Reporting still uses its separate supported reporting interface. [R14] [R16] [R45]
Apply destination-specific normalization/hashing only to eligible matching fields, with versioned fixtures; hashing is not anonymization or permission. Preserve genuine click identifiers, transaction IDs and original event times. Recheck consent and policy at dispatch. Handle rejected, accepted, unmatched/unknown and attributed states separately. Later lead qualification, sale, cancellation or correction needs a supported lifecycle mapping, not an invented negative conversion or repeated new purchase.
51.6 Experiments, reporting and governance
Use predeclared campaign experiments where supported, with a business hypothesis, valid comparison, outcome window, sample-quality checks and stable measurement. Do not combine changing audiences, creatives and landing pages and claim a single isolated page effect. Google's experiment tooling provides supported test mechanisms; the website's Chapter 34 still controls site-level experiments. [R75]
Reporting includes spend, impressions, clicks, eligible outcomes, qualified leads, realized value, search/placement information where available, account settings and extraction freshness. Document currency/timezone and attribution settings. Do not add platform-attributed conversions to internal totals as if they were extra sales. Review disapprovals, limited eligibility, billing problems, permissions, feed failures and destination errors daily during active spend.
51.7 Google Ads release gate
Before live activation, verify account ownership/permissions, current policy eligibility, destination and offer consistency, primary goal configuration, a synthetic form/chat/transaction, CRM receipt, eligible browser/server delivery and reporting extraction. Test denied consent, ad blockers, malformed click IDs, redirects, duplicate retries, delayed qualification, account revocation and provider outages. A campaign launch is blocked when leads are lost, prohibited data is transmitted, essential destinations fail or conversion goals are known to be misleading.
Final edition integration: Chapter 67 supplies the visibility/economics playbook and effective-goal checks; Chapter 69 updates ingestion/credential contracts.
52 | Meta Ads operating specification and conversion quality
52.1 Business assets, policy and current-account contract
The business owns the relevant business portfolio/assets, ad accounts, Facebook/Instagram identities, dataset/pixel, domains, billing and recovery. Maintain a secure asset map with delegated staff/agency roles. Page/content rights, reporting access, event delivery, native-lead access and ad spending are separate capabilities. Disable departing staff and audit asset/permission changes.
Before implementation, verify current objectives, performance goals, attribution settings, permitted audiences, special/restricted advertising categories, data-source restrictions and API versions in the actual account. Some detailed Meta references were login-gated or could not be retrieved during this edition's public research. R76–R80 are therefore implementation verification links, not claims that every current field, limit or entitlement was independently confirmed. These checks are explicit launch dependencies. [R76] [R77] [R78] [R79] [R80]
52.2 Campaign, ad-set and creative workflow
The owner prepares a campaign brief with approved offer, outcome, spend authority, target markets, audience assumptions and destination. The operator chooses the currently supported objective and performance goal that match the business outcome. Keep campaign/ad-set/ad IDs and human labels separate so renaming does not break attribution or reporting.
Configure budgets, schedules, placements, optimization, audience controls and exclusions as supported. Review any automatic audience expansion or creative enhancement explicitly; an input labeled an audience suggestion may not be a hard restriction. Do not promise precise reach or exclusion behavior beyond the current platform contract. Website personalization must not infer sensitive characteristics or claim private knowledge of the person seeing an ad.
Creative records include source files, copy, format/aspect variants, subtitles, safe placement areas, thumbnail, destination, rights, expiry, approval and experiment tags. Preview actual mobile placements and the Facebook/Instagram in-app browser path. Avoid text clipped by interface overlays, unreadable disclaimers, deceptive buttons, fake testimonials and exaggerated outcomes. Review automated creative transformations before or during controlled activation under the provider's available workflow.
52.3 Website, native-lead and messaging destinations
Website campaigns use fast, accurate landing pages and the same authoritative form/lead service as other sources. Preserve eligible campaign identifiers through redirects and the complete website journey. Do not copy raw ad macros into public content or treat query parameters as permission to change pricing.
Native lead forms require their own approved field map, privacy notice, lead-access permissions, verified retrieval/webhook process, retention policy, duplicate handling and CRM assignment. A retrieved platform lead is not proof the person visited the website. Record source_channel=provider_lead_form, provider lead/form/campaign identifiers, capture time, disclosed purpose and verification state without fabricating website telemetry.
Click-to-message campaigns require an actual channel integration, staffing and permitted response workflow. A messaging click, a first inbound message, a conversation, a captured enquiry and a qualified lead are separate milestones. Chapter 48 controls cross-channel identity and suppression; a click-to-chat destination must not silently enroll the contact into unrelated promotional messaging.
52.4 Pixel and CAPI event architecture
Maintain one approved owner for each browser event. Use Chapter 14's safe event catalogue, Chapter 16's authoritative business events and Chapter 37's chatbot capture transaction. Page views, content views, lead acceptance, registration and purchase have distinct meanings. Event names and content identifiers must not disclose prohibited or sensitive context.
For the same permitted business action represented by browser and server, reuse the stored event identity and agreed event name under the current Pixel/CAPI deduplication contract. The server returns that identity only after commit. Retries preserve it; independent later lifecycle events get independent identities. The exact browser/server parameter names and supported windows must be validated in current official documentation and account diagnostics, not guessed from this internal schema. [R19]
The server adapter validates action source, original time, safe source URL, eligible matching data and relevant value/currency. Use authentic click/cookie-derived identifiers only where allowed. Never invent a missing fbclid or treat a backend/provider IP as the visitor's address. Raw IP/user-agent, where a destination permits and a lawful purpose justifies matching, require a restricted adapter; they do not belong in generic analytics or model context. Hash only fields for which the provider contract requires it.
52.5 Data minimization, matching quality and diagnostics
A matching-quality indicator is a diagnostic signal, not an instruction to collect unlimited customer details. Do not sacrifice consent, data minimization or sensitive-business restrictions to maximize a score. Field eligibility is evaluated at collection and again at dispatch. Full form answers, transcript text, exact coordinates, health/financial details and private portal URLs must not leak through custom data, event names or source URLs. [R20]
Build a reconciliation view: committed business outcomes → policy-eligible events → queued → attempted → accepted/rejected → diagnostic match status where available → platform-attributed results. Explain differences rather than force equality. Store safe error codes and original identities for replay; retries cannot bypass later withdrawal, expiry or correction.
Use test modes/Events Manager and network inspection for browser-only, server-only, duplicated, delayed, consent-refused and blocker-affected scenarios. Event Match Quality, deduplication feedback and attributed reporting may not appear instantly or identically for every source. Show pending/unknown where evidence is not yet available; do not manufacture success badges.
52.6 Optimization, experiments and operating controls
Select optimization events that reflect genuine value and adequate available feedback. Keep micro-engagement separate from leads and sales. Define qualified-lead feedback using actual CRM evidence and a currently supported destination/optimization mapping. Do not assume that sending a custom event makes it immediately eligible for every bidding feature.
Review creative fatigue, audience overlap, conversion quality, lead response time, placement/destination issues and refund/cancellation outcomes. Use supported campaign experiments or lift studies where eligible, with appropriate analysis. Website A/B assignment must not be confounded with separate campaign audiences. Avoid declaring success from a small apparent uplift or automatically escalating spending without an approved budget decision.
Maintain account-quality/disapproval escalation, billing alerts, credential rotation, API deprecation tracking and an emergency stop procedure. Automated campaign writes are a separately authorized module with validation, audit and confirmation of remote state. Do not evade policy enforcement by cycling accounts, domains or identities.
52.7 Meta release evidence
Require a current asset/permission register, approved policy category, tested website/native-form/messaging destination, reviewed event field map, authenticated deduplication evidence, consent and sensitive-data tests, CRM outcome reconciliation and a reporting backfill/incremental-sync test. Account-level uncertainty remains Blocked until checked; it must not be labeled Passed because the general plan is complete.
Final edition integration: Chapter 68 supplies creative/delivery operations and Chapter 69 enforces final serialized payload boundaries.
53 | Audience lifecycle, remarketing and paid-media control boundaries
53.1 Audience purpose and eligibility
Implement an owner-operated audience catalogue with owner, purpose, internal segment version, provider/account, audience type, entry/exit conditions, membership duration, exclusions, refresh frequency and retention. Website-visitor audiences, first-party customer lists and provider-generated audience products have different eligibility and control models. Google documents website-segment creation and Customer Match policies separately; respect the actual supported product and personalized-ad restrictions. [R70] [R68] [R69]
Do not upload every lead by default. A contact record is not universal permission for advertising. Sensitive categories, minors, restricted industries and unsuitable destinations require explicit exclusion or a specific reviewed legal/provider basis. Use the minimum allowed fields and restrict raw matching identifiers to the destination adapter. Hashing does not make a customer list anonymous.
53.2 Membership and exclusion state machine
Compute candidate membership from a versioned approved segment, then evaluate purpose/consent, region/business restrictions, retention, suppression, account capability and destination policy. Keep internal candidates, eligible records, submitted records, accepted records and provider match/size information separate. A platform may not reveal person-level matching or exact real-time membership; show the evidence actually available.
Maintain pending_add, active_or_submitted, pending_remove, removed_or_confirmed, suppressed, expired and failed states as appropriate to the provider. Use idempotent batch manifests and retain minimal receipt metadata. Opt-out, deletion, purchase, loss of entitlement or a changed segment may remove eligibility; prioritize removal and exclusion operations. Recheck eligibility before sending each batch so delayed jobs cannot recreate revoked membership.
The owner can pause updates, retire an audience and inspect safe membership explanations. Audience deletion, record removal and campaign exclusion are different operations. Confirm which operation is supported and whether it stops future targeting; do not promise that an API request instantly reverses earlier ad delivery.
53.3 Exclusions and measurement
Use declared campaign rules to exclude existing customers from acquisition campaigns, remove recent converters from a relevant follow-up sequence or avoid repeated contact where appropriate. Exclusions must be defined against real business state, not just a browser purchase button. Record the limits of account-level exclusion behavior and any automatic expansion settings.
Evaluate remarketing separately from prospecting and retention. A person who returns through a remarketing ad might have converted without it; attributed revenue is not proof of incremental impact. Where volume and eligibility permit, use holdouts or platform lift studies with reviewed methodology. Do not add audience-level reach estimates to website unique-person totals.
53.4 Paid-media console: three independent permission levels
Building campaign management is not a prerequisite for website readiness. The comprehensive catalogue includes its requirements, but the site brief must explicitly activate external writes. Providers can approve, reject or modify settings asynchronously; maintain proposed, submitted, remote_pending, applied, rejected and uncertain states. A green local Save button must not imply a remote campaign changed.
53.5 Backend and operational contract
Use provider-neutral internal IDs with external mappings. Add audience_definitions, audience_membership_actions, sync_batches, provider_receipts, campaign_change_requests and remote_state_snapshots. Store credentials only by reference. Apply tenant/account scoping, permission review, retry budgets and dead-letter handling. Never run arbitrary API calls assembled from a CMS text field.
Disconnect revokes future access where supported, stops queued writes and initiates an approved data-removal workflow. Export definitions and mappings without secrets. Test unsupported audience types, under-threshold/unknown sizes, permission loss, rate limits, partial batches, duplicate retries and withdrawal during synchronization. Unconfirmed removals create an operational task, not a false completion claim.
54 | Product catalogues, feeds and transaction growth
54.1 One catalogue with channel-specific projections
An activated commerce site uses authoritative product/variant, price, tax, stock and fulfillment records. Marketing pages, structured data, Google Merchant feeds, Meta catalogues and the chatbot read approved projections of the same business state. Do not maintain conflicting manual prices in several systems. Record channel-specific eligibility, labels and excluded items without changing the source product's identity.
The catalogue includes stable product/variant IDs, titles, descriptions, brand/identifiers where legitimately available, attributes, images, accessibility text, categories, prices/currency, availability, destination URLs, shipping/returns references, locale and revision. Never invent GTINs, ratings or availability to satisfy a feed. Google Merchant Center's product-data specification defines field requirements and supported attributes; validate against the actual destination and product type. [R81]
54.2 Feed generation and diagnostics
Generate versioned feed snapshots or supported API updates through adapters. Validate required fields, image fetchability, URL status, price/availability consistency, language/currency and destinations before dispatch. Record submitted, accepted, warning, disapproved and unavailable states by item. Preserve error details safely and show actionable owner guidance.
Use Google's current Merchant API migration guidance when selecting a new implementation rather than assuming an old Content API example remains appropriate. Confirm current versions, authentication and destination capabilities during development. Meta catalogue field requirements and account eligibility require current authenticated verification. [R82]
Use incremental changes plus periodic full reconciliation. A missing item in a partial update must not accidentally erase the full catalogue. Deactivated products require deliberate destination removal/availability state and customer-facing handling. Track freshness; pause or suppress unsafe stale items according to approved rules. A feed upload accepted by an API is not proof an item is approved for advertising.
54.3 Transaction lifecycle and merchandising
Upsells, bundles, coupons, gift cards, store credit, loyalty and referrals are separately activated features with accounting and fraud rules. Do not add preselected paid extras, deceptive scarcity or cancellation obstacles. Guest checkout and account creation are business decisions; payment success must not depend on optional marketing consent.
54.4 Payment security and consumer obligations
Use supported hosted/tokenized payment flows and determine the real compliance scope with the acquirer/provider and qualified reviewer. PCI guidance addresses authorization, integrity and change monitoring of payment-page scripts and security-impacting headers; outsourcing collection does not justify ignoring the website's applicable responsibilities. [R95]
Never store card security codes or raw payment credentials. Verify signed provider events and trusted server status, amount, currency and order association. Handle duplicate/out-of-order webhooks, disputed payments, partial captures, refunds and abandoned redirects. A browser thank-you URL is not payment proof. Keep a ledger of immutable financial facts and subsequent adjustments.
Display material prices, fees, tax treatment, shipping/service restrictions, return/cancellation terms and customer support before commitment. Regional taxes, invoices, electronic receipts, recurring-payment disclosures and consumer rules require an activated jurisdictional checklist. The blueprint is not a universal tax/legal opinion.
54.5 Measurement, recovery and acceptance
Emit trusted commerce events only at defined lifecycle transitions. Separate gross revenue, net revenue, tax, shipping, refunds and realized margin. Product-content IDs must map correctly to destination catalogue IDs. Abandoned-cart recovery uses Chapter 45's eligible contact workflow, not hidden keystroke capture; stock changes and completed purchases cancel inappropriate reminders.
Test price manipulation, concurrent stock purchases, delayed payment after hold expiry, duplicate webhooks, partial refunds, feed mismatch, unavailable products and canceled subscriptions. Reconcile order/payment/settlement records and ad corrections under supported APIs. The owner must be able to update a product, inspect a disapproved feed item, fulfill an order and process a permitted refund with complete audit history.
Final edition integration: Preserve provider-required generated-media provenance and labeled data through feed transformations under Chapter 66.4.
55 | Reporting, warehouse, attribution and incrementality
55.1 Metric dictionary and source-of-truth model
Define every reported metric with owner, grain, numerator/denominator, filters, timezone, currency, attribution model, window, latency, source and correction policy. Keep events, sessions, permitted browser identities, leads, customers and orders distinct. “Unique visitors” is not a census of people. Consent-refused or blocked activity is not silently classified as direct traffic.
Authoritative operational facts come from PostgreSQL/CRM/payment records. GA4 provides its supported behavioral/reporting view; ad accounts provide attributed performance and spend; Search Console provides search visibility data. Use reconciliation categories, not invented events, to explain differences. A count of accepted API requests does not establish matched or attributed conversions.
55.2 Read connectors and analytical storage
Implement read-only connectors with actual account permissions, backfill cursors, incremental refresh, lookback restatement for late data, pagination, rate budgets, schema/version validation and freshness. Keep extraction time, source settings and original currency/timezone. Do not double count totals by summing overlapping breakdowns or multiple attribution reports.
Use a separate analytical workload when reporting threatens transactional performance. Maintain raw approved source snapshots, validated normalized tables and curated metric views with lineage. BigQuery export is an optional GA4 integration; export data and UI reports can differ, and enabling export must not be represented as a guaranteed complete historical backfill. Validate the selected property's actual behavior and limits. [R89]
Warehouse imports follow the same privacy schedules, site isolation and deletion/hold rules as source data. Use restricted analyst roles, query budgets and export controls. Pseudonymous event data is not automatically anonymous. Do not join aggregate ad reach to named visitors as if the provider supplied individual impressions.
55.3 Attribution views and conversion corrections
Offer clearly named first-eligible-touch, last-touch, last-non-direct or other approved internal views, with a frozen conversion-time snapshot and versioned logic. Keep platform attribution alongside, not blended into an unexplained single number. Respect lookback and eligibility; do not replay unconsented earlier visits when a person later supplies an email.
Maintain observed source, user-declared source and inferred attribution as separate fields. A person returning from a payment processor should not become a new acquisition source under an approved referral policy. Cross-domain journeys require the controlled transfer/linker design, not PII in URLs.
Refunds, cancellation, disqualification and duplicate-case corrections produce auditable adjustments. Recompute affected internal metrics through a versioned process and use supported external adjustments where available. Preserve a report revision or freshness note when late data changes a past period. Avoid silently rewriting exported board or financial reports without a correction trail.
55.4 Dashboards and reporting delivery
Provide saved views, role-based filters and approved scheduled reports. Exports need permission, expiry and CSV/formula injection safety.
