Email/report notifications contain safe summaries and authenticated links, not full lead lists or precise locations. A missing source displays unavailable/stale, never an unexplained zero.
55.5 Incrementality and advanced analysis
Attribution allocates credit; incrementality asks what changed because of an intervention. Use randomized holdouts, credible campaign experiments or appropriately designed geographic studies when feasible. Predeclare outcomes, sample requirements, comparison groups, duration, guardrails and contamination risks. Do not call an observational before/after chart a causal result.
Marketing-mix modeling and lifetime-value prediction are conditional analytical modules, not prerequisites for a small website. They need adequate historical variation, trustworthy inputs, seasonality/lag assumptions, uncertainty evaluation and specialist review. Model estimates must not overwrite actual invoices or be presented as guaranteed future revenue. Low-volume sites should prioritize customer research and reliable core measurement.
55.6 Data-quality acceptance
Test duplicate facts, late-arriving events, daylight-saving boundaries, multiple currencies, empty cohorts, refunded orders, connector outages, deleted identities and permission-filtered exports. Maintain reconciliation assertions at each stage and alert on structural breaks. The owner can inspect a reported metric's definition, source freshness and drill-down to authorized evidence without exposing unrelated personal data.
56 | Conversion optimization, research and interactive growth tools
56.1 Beyond installing an A/B testing tool
Operate a conversion-improvement backlog based on customer tasks, usability research, form errors, performance, permitted journey data and actual lead quality. Prioritize by impact hypothesis, confidence, effort, risk and required evidence. A/B testing in Chapter 34 is one method; it is not appropriate to force statistical winner claims where traffic is insufficient.
Each proposed change identifies the audience, obstacle, intended mechanism, affected pages, primary outcome, guardrails, privacy effect, measurement and rollback. Observe whether the complete journey improves, not only button clicks. Keep price/terms and staff capacity consistent with the offer being tested.
56.2 Research, surveys and feedback
Support approved onsite feedback, exit-intent questions, post-conversion surveys and customer interviews with clear purpose, optional participation and bounded frequency. Do not request sensitive free text unnecessarily. Protect responses as business/research data, not generic analytics dimensions. Allow staff to code themes without publishing customer identities.
Use accessible tasks and representative devices, including assistive technology and slow networks. Record recruitment constraints and the limits of a small sample. Heatmaps/replay remain separately consented and masked; excluding private and sensitive pages is more reliable than assuming a recording tool masks everything correctly. Do not expose transcripts, form values or account pages in replay.
56.3 Forms and lead magnets
Provide concise forms, clear labels, inline validation, accessible errors, appropriate autofill, progress and useful confirmation. Conditional/multi-step forms need versioned branching and server validation at final submission. An optional save-and-resume flow uses explicit customer intent, protected draft storage, expiry and a secure resumption mechanism; it is not permission to silently collect abandoned keystrokes.
Resources can be public, explicitly form-gated, account-protected or paid. State what is received and whether follow-up is optional. A download click, authorized delivery and completed reading are different. Do not gate required pricing, legal terms or safety information merely to increase lead count. Expired resource links need a useful renewal/contact path without exposing private access tokens.
56.4 Calculators, quizzes, configurators and offer widgets
For activated interactive tools, specify input units/ranges, business formula, result meaning, version, assumptions, rounding, error states and accessible alternatives. Compute authoritative prices or eligibility on the server. Label estimates as estimates and avoid invented financial, medical or academic guarantees. Test extreme values, missing inputs, localization and stale configuration.
Quizzes need an approved question/branch/result model, optional contact capture and a clear distinction between a recommendation and verified eligibility. Do not infer protected traits or silently enroll respondents into advertising audiences. Personalization and chatbot suggestions reference the same approved offer/knowledge sources. A generated summary remains a draft where a staff decision is required.
Popups, announcement bars, sticky CTAs, coupons and referral invitations use frequency limits, keyboard access, unobstructed content, honest close controls and appropriate mobile behavior. No deceptive countdowns, fake activity notifications, forced permission prompts or back-button traps. Define conflicts among multiple widgets so the consent panel, chat, form and promotion do not cover one another.
56.5 Experiment interaction and deployment
Maintain an experiment/feature-conflict registry. A change to the same form, offer or CTA may invalidate a running experiment; owners see the conflict before publication. Preserve stable action IDs and pinned revisions. Accessibility, performance, spam rates, complaints and qualification are guardrails, not optional afterthoughts.
A winning variant is promoted through the normal reviewed CMS release, not copied into production outside version history. A harmful or invalid test can be paused immediately. Store the analysis and decision even when results are inconclusive. Customer obligations created during a test remain valid after rollback.
56.6 Acceptance
The owner creates a feedback form, previews a resource offer, tests a calculator's bounds and runs an approved synthetic experiment. Verify optional tracking refusal, denied location, accessible widget dismissal, duplicate lead prevention, accurate result labels and no data leakage. Report actual task completion and business outcomes; do not present a tool's “optimization score” as proof of improved sales.
57 | Domain, DNS, TLS and account continuity
57.1 Business ownership and recovery independence
Maintain an asset register for domains, registrar, DNS/CDN, hosting, email, source repositories, CMS, certificates, payment, advertising, analytics and communication providers. Record business owner, delegated admins, billing contact, renewal dates, recovery channels, MFA methods, support path and last access review. Store secret references, never passwords or recovery codes in the blueprint.
Use business-owned accounts, individual staff identities and at least one tested recovery path independent of the affected domain's mailbox. A developer's personal email or laptop must not be the sole authority for recovering the business. Staff departure triggers access revocation, ownership verification and rotation of shared service credentials where required.
57.2 Registrar and renewal controls
Configure renewal responsibility, payment validity, reminders, escalation and actual renewal confirmation. Automatic renewal is a convenience, not proof it succeeded. Alert on failed payments, expired cards, transfer attempts, unauthorized contact changes and approaching domain expiry. Keep an approved policy for domain/registry locks and transfer authorization.
A domain transfer and a DNS/hosting change are different procedures. Review the registrar's actual eligibility, locks, authorization process and timing; ICANN's transfer guidance is a reference, not a promise of a universal instant transfer. Preserve service and mail continuity throughout the chosen procedure. [R64]
57.3 DNS and certificate configuration
Inventory A/AAAA/CNAME/MX/TXT/CAA and other required records with purpose, owner, provider, TTL and change history. Use least-privilege DNS credentials. Validate new records in staging or a controlled change window, include propagation expectations and monitor both IPv4/IPv6 where published. Do not leave dangling records pointing to abandoned services.
DNSSEC activation/rotation requires coordinated signing and parent DS records, provider support and tested rollback; an incorrect chain can make the domain unreachable. Certificate issuance/renewal requires verified domain control, an automated challenge path, renewal monitoring and expiry alerts. Follow the selected DNS/certificate provider's supported process instead of treating a toggle as sufficient evidence. [R65] [R66]
TLS redirects, HSTS and certificate coverage must be tested for canonical hostnames, subdomains, legacy routes and integrations. Apply HSTS scope/preload only after reviewing affected subdomains and recovery impact. Keep an intentional CAA policy where supported. A certificate renewal test must include the challenge after WAF/proxy changes.
57.4 Controlled changes and migration
A DNS or domain change request records requested records, business reason, before/after values, approver, affected services, execution window, verification and rollback. Export the current zone and preserve recovery credentials. Test web, email authentication, inbound mail, API/webhooks, private downloads, analytics ownership and ad destination verification after cutover.
Plan TTL changes before a migration, but do not assume every resolver immediately respects the desired timing. Keep the old service available for the approved overlap period when possible. Use synthetic requests from relevant regions and confirm mail delivery, not only the homepage. Domain redirects must preserve intended customer journeys and eligible acquisition parameters without leaking secrets.
57.5 Incident and shutdown runbooks
Document unauthorized domain transfer, DNS hijack, certificate expiry, registrar lockout, email outage, CDN/origin outage and accidental zone deletion. Define emergency contacts, proof of ownership, containment and safe restoration. Do not disable security controls globally as a routine troubleshooting shortcut.
Site retirement has a plan for customer obligations, final notices, URL redirection or intentional removal, records retention, cancellation of paid services, webhook shutdown, account revocation and lawful disposal. Keep domain ownership long enough to prevent harmful reassignment under the approved business/risk policy; do not specify an arbitrary universal period.
57.6 Acceptance evidence
Deliver verified ownership/access, renewal and certificate alerts, a reviewed DNS inventory, independent account recovery, a test zone/certificate exercise and a cutover/rollback rehearsal. The owner demonstrates recovery without contacting the original developer. Production changes remain approved external actions, not automatic consequences of editing this document.
58 | Security, identity, supply chain and abuse resistance
58.1 Threat model and assurance scope
Threat-model public pages, forms, CMS, APIs, integrations, chatbot tools, analytics collectors, exports, databases, queues, storage and deployment pipelines. Identify assets, trust boundaries, attacker capabilities, abuse cases and recovery requirements. Include malicious content editors, compromised integration keys, forged callbacks, cross-site access, prompt injection, unauthorized exports and deletion attempts. Select a risk-appropriate verification standard and record its version.
Use the existing ASVS plan together with API-specific review and secure-development practices. OWASP's API risks include object/property authorization, resource consumption and unsafe third-party consumption; NIST SSDF provides a secure-development framework. These references support the assurance programme, not a claim of certification. [R34] [R93] [R92]
58.2 Identity and privileged access
Use individual accounts, least privilege, MFA for staff and stronger reauthentication for destructive operations, credentials, exports and spending changes. Prefer phishing-resistant authentication where the chosen identity system and recovery process support it. Customer authentication must not share staff permissions. Support session review/revocation, account invitation expiry, safe password reset, rate limiting and account-enumeration resistance.
SSO, enterprise federation and automated provisioning are conditional modules with tested joiner/mover/leaver flows, emergency access and tenant boundaries. Social login must use supported authorization flows and cannot trust an email string supplied by a browser. Account linking requires proof of control. Preserve minimal attributable actor history after staff deactivation without retaining unnecessary personal data forever.
Use OAuth security best practices for connectors: appropriate authorization-code flow, proof-key protection where applicable, state/redirect validation, scoped tokens and secure refresh/revocation handling. Do not put client secrets in public apps or accept arbitrary redirect URIs. Confirm the actual provider contract rather than inventing a universal login flow. [R94]
58.3 Application and API hardening
Validate all external input at runtime; use parameterized database access, safe rich-text/HTML sanitization, context-aware output encoding and controlled uploads. Enforce object/field/tenant authorization in application use cases, not only UI hiding. Public collectors cannot create authoritative purchases, set qualification or choose arbitrary destinations. CORS is not authentication, and a hidden URL is not access control.
Protect cookie-authenticated writes from CSRF. Apply safe content/security headers, CSP, clickjacking controls, restrictive referrer behavior, TLS and correct MIME handling. Review SSRF in content importers, link previews, webhook testing, AI URL tools and remote image processing. Validate final destinations after redirects, restrict internal-network access, bound decompression and prevent active uploaded content from executing on the main origin.
For GraphQL, enforce object/field authorization, depth/complexity limits, bounded pagination and controlled operations. SOAP/XML adapters disable unsafe entities and constrain size/depth. WebSocket/SSE chat connections require scoped authentication, origin checks where applicable, message validation, expiry and reconnect handling. API keys are scoped, rotated and stored by reference; public embedded keys are never treated as secrets.
58.4 Third-party script and tag governance
Keep a script/widget inventory with owner, purpose, route scope, vendor, loaded resources, collected fields, consent category, performance budget, access risk, version/change policy and removal plan. Only approved templates and destinations can be published through GTM/CMS. Separate content editing, container publication and security administration. Require review for new tags, custom HTML and remote embeds.
Monitor unexpected scripts/network destinations, consent violations and performance regressions. Use integrity controls where technically appropriate and supported; dynamically changing vendor tags need a different reviewed control than a static asset hash. Sandbox untrusted embeds and limit frame permissions. OWASP's third-party JavaScript guidance highlights that outsourced scripts can affect the site's data and security boundary. [R91]
Provide independent kill switches for optional tags, replay, chat, automation and external writes. Test that turning off marketing code leaves navigation, lead acceptance and payments working. Keep critical audit and content preservation always enforced. No marketing user may disable redaction or grant a model unrestricted tools.
58.5 Supply chain, secrets and deployment
Pin dependencies and container images appropriately, maintain SBOM/license/provenance records, scan dependencies/secrets/code/images and triage findings with owners and deadlines. Distinguish exploited/critical issues from low-risk informational findings; record accepted exceptions and expiry. Do not silently ignore a vulnerable transitive package because it was not added directly.
Use protected branches, reviewed releases, isolated CI jobs and minimal credentials. Prefer short-lived workload identity where supported; restrict production secrets from pull-request builds. Sign or attest artifacts where the assurance level requires it, deploy immutable artifacts and keep a release manifest. Test upgrades against content revisions, API clients, migrations and rollback compatibility. Remove unused plugins and endpoints.
58.6 Abuse, fraud and bot handling
Layer request budgets, payload limits, honeypots/timing signals, reputation and accessible challenges where justified. Include email/SMS/OTP abuse, fake leads, mass downloads, coupon/referral abuse, credential stuffing, event inflation and expensive AI/search requests. Rate limits combine service/tenant/session signals with approved network evidence; shared IP addresses must not automatically imply one person or fraud.
Keep suspected abuse separate from verified legitimate conversions. Staff can review false positives through a safe queue. Ad-click anomalies can trigger investigation and supported provider processes, not a guarantee that all invalid traffic is identifiable or refundable. Never manufacture conversion events to compensate for suspected fraud or browser blocking.
58.7 Security acceptance and disclosure
Test cross-tenant/object access, privilege escalation, forged proxy headers, malicious uploads/imports, CSRF, XSS, SSRF, webhook replay, token leakage, prompt injection, log injection and resource exhaustion. Use authorized test environments and synthetic data. Provide a vulnerability-reporting route, triage process, incident contacts and patch verification. Independent testing is required where the project risk/contract specifies it; a scanner report alone is not a penetration test.
59 | Privacy policy engine, rights and jurisdictional controls
59.1 Data inventory and legal applicability
Maintain a field-and-purpose inventory spanning content, leads, accounts, IP/location, chat, recordings, analytics, advertising, automation, audiences, exports and backups. Each entry identifies source, collection trigger, lawful-processing assessment, notice, sensitivity, access roles, destinations/processors, region, retention, deletion/hold and evidence. Distinguish truly necessary service state from optional analytics or advertising.
A qualified reviewer determines the actual applicable jurisdictions and sector rules. Geography from an IP is only an uncertain hint, not a complete legal analysis. The inherited India reference describes phased commencement of the 2025 rules; current notifications and business applicability need confirmation. Do not equate a website consent banner with a statutory registered Consent Manager or universal compliance. [R41]
59.2 Purpose-aware policy service
Centralize decisions in a versioned policy service used by web SDKs, forms, chatbot, location, workflow dispatch, reporting exports and ad adapters. Inputs include task/purpose, data class, authenticated role, current user choices, account/site configuration, legal applicability and destination eligibility. Outputs are allow/deny/minimize, reason, field projection, expiry and policy version. No widget invents its own independent permission model.
Default optional tracking remains off under this standard's conservative blocking policy until the applicable permission is satisfied. Distinguish analytics_storage, advertising storage/use/personalization and service communication controls where relevant; map supported provider consent signals correctly. Advanced cookieless measurement is a separate approved decision, not an automatic workaround. [R21]
59.3 User controls, withdrawal and browser signals
Provide accessible accept/reject/manage choices, a persistent preference entry point, clear purpose descriptions, versioned records and a tested withdrawal path. Do not use preselected optional choices, repeated coercive prompts or make a service enquiry conditional on unrelated marketing consent. A browser's geolocation grant is separate from advertising consent and from the stated purpose for saving coordinates.
Where applicable, implement recognized opt-out preference signals and account-level rights. California's Attorney General describes GPC as a valid opt-out signal that covered businesses must honor for sale/sharing. Applicability and the permitted scope of processing must be reviewed; it is not a universal instruction to erase necessary service records. [R90]
Resolve conflicting choices through the approved policy, favoring applicable withdrawal/opt-out restrictions rather than silently re-enabling tracking. Propagate changes to queued work, projections, segments and destination eligibility. Do not reconstruct earlier unconsented activity after later consent. Record limited suppression evidence so delayed imports or restored backups do not revive prohibited processing.
59.4 Rights-request workflow and processor propagation
Provide request intake, proportionate requester verification where required, scope selection, deadline tracking, exceptions/legal holds, approval, execution, processor notifications and completion evidence. Access/deletion requests may need identity checks; a simple unsubscribe or legally recognized opt-out must not be obstructed by unnecessary account creation or identity demands. Define the distinctions in the jurisdictional matrix.
Map rights actions across operational tables, event stores, chat summaries/attachments, search/vector indexes, warehouse extracts, exports, audience destinations and providers. Separate deleted, anonymized, suppressed, legally retained and backup-expiry-pending states. A deletion request is not complete merely because one database row disappeared. Reapply valid deletion/suppression records after restore before exposing the system.
Preserved blogs may contain personal or licensed content. The manual-only editorial policy cannot override lawful removal, security containment or legal hold. Preserve safe deletion evidence, not full sensitive text in immutable audit diffs. Retain raw IP, precise coordinates and transcripts only for their independently approved schedules, not indefinitely because “everything is logged.”
59.5 Sensitive audiences and sector decisions
The table is a review gate, not legal advice or a declaration that one worldwide rule applies. Store the actual legal decision, reviewer, effective date and next review trigger. Do not expose private application or patient data to general-purpose marketing tags even when the public site uses them.
59.6 Retention, contracts and evidence
Maintain processor/subprocessor records, contracts, permitted training/use of AI data, region choices, breach responsibilities, deletion/export capabilities and exit procedures. Verify actual vendor settings; do not claim zero retention from an unverified marketing statement. Privacy/security impact assessments are required when the chosen risk or law calls for them.
Test data minimization before storage and at every outbound adapter, not only after a leak appears in a dashboard. Use synthetic secret/contact/location markers and inspect payloads, logs, recordings, exports and model/tool context. Audit preference changes, policy publication, sensitive reveals, rights execution and legal exceptions. A privacy release gate must fail on prohibited collection even if conversions increase.
60 | Reliability, database operations, DevOps and cost control
60.1 Service objectives and workload model
Approve measurable service objectives separately for public rendering, lead acceptance, CMS publication, chat, payments, automation and reporting freshness. Define workload assumptions, traffic peaks, message bursts, asset size, database growth and external quotas. Availability, latency and recovery targets are engineering commitments only when the deployed design and staffing can support them.
Load-test realistic route mixes and business transactions, including consent choices, warmed/cold caches, mobile networks and concurrent workers. A homepage benchmark is not a test of lead durability, inbox concurrency or feed generation. Track field and laboratory performance separately; field Core Web Vitals depend on real eligible measurements and cannot be proven by one local score. [R04]
60.2 Database integrity and operational maintenance
Use migrations, constraints, restricted roles, connection pools, bounded queries, indexes justified by plans and optimistic concurrency. Monitor slow queries, lock contention, deadlocks, replication lag where used, disk growth and maintenance. Set statement/connection budgets and test cancellation so reports do not starve form submission. Analyze tenant-specific load and isolate noisy workloads.
Backups and point-in-time recovery must match the approved RPO/RTO. PostgreSQL continuous archiving combines base backups with the necessary WAL history for recovery; successful backup creation alone does not prove recoverability. Exercise restoration, required keys, object assets, configuration and independent deletion/audit ledgers. [R96]
Define upgrade/maintenance windows, storage expansion, vacuum/maintenance ownership, credential rotation and failover testing. A replica is not a substitute for an independent recoverable backup. High availability does not prevent logical deletion or corruption. Keep the source-blog deletion restrictions active during migrations, cleanup and database-owner operations.
60.3 Failure isolation and graceful degradation
Use timeouts, circuit breakers where appropriate, bounded retry/backoff, dead-letter queues and operator remediation. Avoid cascading retries across layers. Keep critical business transactions independent of optional remote services. A fallback must not bypass authentication, consent, pricing or audit invariants.
60.4 Deployment profiles and infrastructure change
Maintain native and Docker profiles with equivalent business behavior, environment validation, supervised processes, logs, health checks and persistent storage. Managed containers and Kubernetes are selected by scale/operations needs, not required for every site. Terraform/infrastructure code must preserve secrets, remote state, review and destruction safeguards. No bootstrap command may overwrite secrets or reset production data.
Deploy immutable artifacts through reviewed CI/CD. Test unit/integration/contract/security/accessibility/performance behavior and production smoke tests. Use expand-and-contract changes where versions coexist. Application, database and content rollback have different constraints; document forward-fix and restore tradeoffs. Canary or blue/green deployment is useful only with health criteria, data compatibility and an actual rollback procedure.
Protect the origin, private database, object storage and administrative endpoints. Test forwarded headers, DNS/TLS, cache keys, multi-instance invalidation and authenticated responses. Shared public caches must never contain visitor context, draft content, personal prices or session cookies. Limit autoscaling so it does not overwhelm database connections or external quotas.
60.5 Observability, incident response and cost
Provide correlated structured logs, metrics and traces with redaction and distinct audit/business streams. Monitor domain/TLS, route and form synthetics, errors, latency, queues, publication freshness, lead assignment, conversion rejection, consent violations, feed health, workflow backlog and unexpected content loss. Alert thresholds require owners, response windows and escalation; dashboards alone are not response procedures.
Maintain incident severity, on-call contacts, containment, customer communication, evidence preservation, recovery and post-incident review. Test a database restore, compromised integration, unavailable form, broken tag and accidental content removal. Public status/maintenance communication must remain truthful and avoid exposing sensitive topology or customer data.
Budget infrastructure, database/storage/egress, backups, licenses, messages, calls, geo lookups, AI tokens, analytics, staff support, content and ad spend separately. Use per-tenant/provider quotas, spend alerts, idle-environment cleanup and rate controls. Do not auto-delete preserved blogs to save space; expand storage or raise a human-reviewed retention decision. A cost cap for AI must degrade to a useful non-AI path.
60.6 Reliability evidence
Deliver measured load results, restore times, observed data loss boundaries, failure-mode tests, monitoring/alert proof and an owner-readable runbook. Re-run after significant framework, database, provider or infrastructure changes. Record residual single points of failure and accepted tradeoffs honestly; a Dockerfile or multi-node diagram is not evidence of resilience.
61 | Conditional business and industry capability catalogue
61.1 Activation is a full lifecycle commitment
This catalogue prevents common business-site capabilities from being overlooked. It does not assert that every industry-specific law, device or future feature can be implemented by one generic platform. Each selected module receives its own business rules, data model, permissions, customer journey, integration contracts, failure states, operating owner and acceptance pack. An unselected module remains documented but does not add public routes, scripts or cost.
For every module, specify create/read/update/archive behavior, legitimate deletion exceptions, customer obligations, notifications, billing where applicable, accessibility, consent, logging, import/export, migration and recovery. Administrative audit and source-blog preservation are inherited, not reimplemented inconsistently.
61.2 Commerce, booking and education
Live meetings/WebRTC, examinations, proctoring and extensive learning-management functions are separate systems with their own scale, safety and privacy requirements. A webinar landing page does not imply a meeting platform exists. Embedded providers require real access/recording and deletion contracts.
61.3 Portals, B2B, directories and marketplaces
Payment splitting, identity verification, tax reporting, commission accounting and credit facilities require specialized provider/legal decisions. Do not implement them as unreviewed arithmetic in the page builder. A marketplace is not merely a product grid with multiple authors.
61.4 Publishing, community, media and donations
Publisher ad monetization is distinct from buying Google/Meta ads. Where an ad network requires authorized-seller declarations, consent integrations or supply-chain files, implement its current specification and verification. For applicable web publisher inventory, IAB Tech Lab documents ads.txt as an authorized-seller declaration; it is not a requirement for simply buying Google/Meta ads. [R106] Do not insert an ad-network script globally and call the publisher module complete.
61.5 Specialized sectors and international delivery
Health/home-care, finance/insurance, legal services, real estate, travel/hospitality, public services and regulated products require a sector activation pack. It should define verified claims/licenses, safe intake, eligibility, availability, professional review, consumer obligations, records access, approved advertising and escalation. The generic marketing tracker remains excluded from protected applications and sensitive portals unless specifically reviewed and permitted.
Localization is more than translating text: routes, metadata, currencies, units, timezones, legal notices, content approvals, customer support and accessibility need local ownership. Right-to-left, pluralization, Unicode names and address/phone formats require tests. Geography must not silently substitute for language, nationality or tax residency.
PWA/offline reading, push notifications, install prompts, maps, voice input, device integrations and live location are conditional enhancements. Offline caches must not expose private content to another user or pretend an unsent form reached the server. Push and device permissions are requested in context, with unsubscribe/revocation and unsupported-device fallbacks. Continuous location, microphone or camera use requires a distinct visible purpose and explicit controls.
61.6 Module completion gate
For each activated row, deliver a module brief, state diagram expressed as a precise transition contract, entities/constraints, APIs, owner screens, public states, event map, permission/retention rules, provider capability matrix, tests and operating guide. Test a happy path, refusal/error path, duplicate/retry, unauthorized access, correction/cancellation and recovery. An inactive module is Not Applicable with rationale; an activated but unfinished module is Not Implemented or Blocked, never Passed.
62 | Extensibility, multisite, interoperability and portability
62.1 Extension boundary and shared platform design
Use a shared core with explicitly registered modules. Each extension declares version, compatible core/schema versions, public/admin routes, components, permissions, entities/migrations, jobs, events, providers, secrets and operating costs. Enablement validates dependencies; disablement stops eligible jobs/tags/routes while preserving existing customer obligations and legally retained records.
The owner may configure approved behavior but cannot install arbitrary executable code into production through a CMS text field. Review extension licenses, maintenance, supply-chain risk and export support. Keep product-specific branding/content separate from platform code. Custom CSS/HTML, when explicitly permitted, is sandboxed/restricted and cannot bypass accessibility or security controls.
62.2 API and integration standards
REST/OpenAPI remains the default. Document schemas, authentication, object scope, idempotency, concurrency, pagination, errors, rate limits and compatibility. OpenAPI is the interface description; generated types do not remove the need for runtime validation and deployed contract tests. Pin an implementation-compatible specification version instead of assuming every tool supports the latest release. [R101]
GraphQL, SOAP, webhooks, streaming and file exchange are optional adapters that call the same domain rules. Require an actual WSDL/XSD for SOAP. Inbound/outbound webhooks use provider-specific authentication, durable receipt, retries, event identity and secret rotation. Customer-configured webhook URLs need SSRF protection, ownership/authorization checks, bounded payloads and a delivery log.
Use provider adapters for CMS, storage, email, CRM, geo, analytics, experiments, AI, payments and ads. An adapter's capability matrix distinguishes login, read, write, backfill, incremental sync, webhook, deletion, export and production evidence. A provider logo or OAuth connection proves none of the remaining capabilities.
62.3 Multisite and tenant isolation
Decide isolated deployments/databases versus shared tenants explicitly. Shared tenancy requires trusted site resolution, tenant-scoped keys/constraints, non-owner database roles, object authorization, cache isolation, separate provider mappings, per-tenant queues/quotas and restricted exports. A browser-supplied site_id is never sufficient authorization.
Domain onboarding verifies control, uniqueness, certificate setup and route binding before serving content. Prevent a tenant from claiming another organization's domain. Test shared assets, global templates, local overrides, account recovery, background jobs, reports and restored backups for cross-site leakage. Platform administrators use time-limited audited support access rather than invisible impersonation.
62.4 Feature flags and configuration governance
Version feature capability, rollout rules and permissions separately. A flag's default, owner, intended lifetime, fallback, required data, dependencies and cleanup date are documented. Standards such as OpenFeature can reduce coupling to one feature-flag provider, but a flag service is not an authorization or consent system. [R100]
A disabled module must stop its outbound data and recurring cost where appropriate, not merely hide a menu. Keep schema migrations and retained records compatible. Test emergency deactivation, missing configuration, old clients, multiple instances and a provider outage. Configuration changes affecting tags, targeting, prices or privacy require the same review/audit discipline as code.
62.5 Search, AI and content interoperability
Export structured content with schema/revision IDs, locale relationships, asset manifests, rights, routes and redirect history. Search/AI indexes are derived and rebuildable from approved sources; they do not become the only copy of content. Include deletion/unpublication invalidation and permission filters at retrieval time. A stale index must not expose a newly private or removed resource.
AI gateways specify models by approved capability/version, purpose, data projection, region/retention, cost, latency, tools and evaluation results. Keep deterministic forms/FAQ fallback. Export bot flows, approved knowledge manifests and safe conversation data under policy. Avoid hardcoded model brands as a permanent architectural dependency and never give the model raw database or purge authority.
62.6 Portability and migration proof
The exit package includes code, dependency locks, migrations, content/media exports, form revisions, consent/suppression, campaign/experiment/workflow definitions, provider mappings, operational runbooks and required keys/recovery references transferred securely. Identify nonportable entitlements and external assets before choosing a provider.
A second qualified team must install the documented native/Docker profile, import data, publish content, accept a lead, restore a backup and rotate a provider without the original developer's machine. Test provider replacement using adapters and reconciliation; preserve identifiers and pending obligations. A migration must not seed demo content over production or reintroduce legally deleted data. Ownership and portability are demonstrated tasks, not marketing labels.
63 | Integrated frontend, backend, database and delivery work packages
63.1 Build the owner workflows, not disconnected screens
The public site, owner console, API, worker, analytical layer and integration adapters share versioned content/event/policy contracts. A visible control must map to an authorized use case and durable state. Do not create empty folders or mock success endpoints merely to match this document. Code, migrations, tests, diagnostics, failure states and runbooks are delivered together.
63.2 Repository expansion
Extend Chapter 27 and Chapter 40 with the following responsibilities. Names can be refined by an architecture decision; boundaries and tests cannot disappear.
apps/admin/src/features/
  marketing-calendar/ offers/ creative-library/
  cms-drafts/ content-models/ import-center/ release-bundles/
  automations/ scoring/ segments/ audiences/
  sender-domains/ message-templates/ unified-inbox/
  feeds/ reporting/ asset-register/ release-evidence/
apps/api/src/modules/
  content-models/ draft-recovery/ content-releases/ imports/
  automation/ scoring/ segmentation/ communication/
  audience-sync/ reporting/ catalog-feeds/ domain-registry/
apps/worker/src/jobs/
  workflow-timers/ message-dispatch/ audience-sync/
  report-extraction/ feed-reconciliation/ policy-propagation/
packages/
  workflow-schema/ segment-schema/ policy-contracts/
  provider-contracts/ metric-definitions/ content-migrations/
tests/
  cms-recovery/ workflow-retries/ audience-suppression/
  sender-auth/ channel-provenance/ feed-consistency/
  tenant-isolation/ restore-reconciliation/ owner-journeys/
docs/
  capability-register/ provider-verification/ legal-decisions/
  campaign-runbooks/ data-dictionary/ release-evidence/
63.3 Database integrity and entity ownership
Keep content schemas and workflow definitions as validated versioned structures; store critical identity, amount, lifecycle and unique keys relationally. Enforce site-scoped foreign keys, idempotency keys, immutable published definitions and restricted source-content deletion roles. Use timestamps for occurrence, collection, dispatch and processing separately. Preserve exact money/currency and source timezone.
Additional tables include offer_versions, creative_assets, content_schema_versions, draft_sessions, import_manifests, content_releases, workflow_versions, workflow_runs, workflow_timers, action_executions, score_snapshots, segment_memberships, message_intents, subscription_preferences, audience_actions, feed_runs, report_sync_runs and asset_register_entries. Every table has an owner, access class, retention/hold policy, indexes and migration fixtures. Do not place raw IP, coordinates or transcripts into every join table for convenience.
Use transactional outbox for state changes with external effects. Maintain delivery ledgers and stable operation IDs. Separate operational data from analytics and keep sensitive fields encrypted/restricted where justified. A deleted actor or source object must not cascade-delete the minimal audit/tombstone evidence required to explain the action.
63.4 API and command additions
These routes are illustrative; implementation must generate/test OpenAPI against real handlers. All transport styles share domain authorization. Appendix J supplies representative envelopes and state examples, not a complete application codebase.
63.5 Events and service boundaries
Use explicit events such as draft_saved, content_release_published, workflow_enrolled, workflow_action_suppressed, score_recalculated, segment_membership_changed, message_delivery_updated, audience_sync_completed and feed_item_rejected. Define authority, schema, fields, privacy, duplicate scope and consumers. Administrative events do not automatically become GA4 or ad conversions.
The workflow engine calls approved business commands; the chatbot calls scoped tools; reporting reads governed views. None bypass the policy service through unrestricted SQL. A provider webhook is validated before it becomes trusted input. Public behavior collectors remain unable to assert sales, qualification or successful delivery.
63.6 Ordered implementation and evidence
Build policy/identity/audit and source-content integrity first; then CMS draft/collection/release operations; then attribution/form/chat transactions; then workflow and communication dispatch; then scoring/segments/audiences; then feed/reporting/campaign integrations and conditional business modules. Finish owner operations, load/security/privacy tests, backup/portability proof and launch preflight.
Every work package includes a dependency list, completed owner task, tested API/database behavior, synthetic fixtures, negative tests, provider evidence, operational alerts and documentation. Code review and passing mocks are not enough to mark an external integration complete. The release register states Passed, Failed, Blocked, Not Implemented or Not Applicable with reasons.
64 | Master testing, acceptance and release evidence
64.1 Trace every activated requirement to a test
Retain Chapter 30, Appendix B and Appendix G. Execute the additional tests in Appendix I for the new capabilities. Each case specifies setup, action, expected result, environment, evidence, owner and status. Default status is Not executed. Include both code and configuration changes: a CMS edit, tag publication, new audience or DNS change can break production without a Git commit.
Use unit, integration, contract, end-to-end, visual/accessibility, security/privacy, performance, recovery and real-provider tests. Mocks isolate logic but do not prove permissions, webhook authenticity, platform acceptance or provider policy eligibility. Test the selected deployment profile and actual supported browsers, including in-app browsers used by paid campaigns.
64.2 Synthetic data and environment controls
Use nonreal identities, dedicated test recipients/accounts/campaigns and clearly marked fixtures. Prevent staging from sending real customer messages or polluting ad optimization. Test modes must be enforced at dispatcher/account configuration, not just a UI badge. Protect staging access and prevent accidental indexing while still testing production crawlability at launch.
Prepare fixtures for Unicode names, international phone/address formats, long translated copy, narrow screens, missing media, stale content, old schema revisions, opt-outs, sensitive markers, duplicate events, invalid payloads and out-of-order callbacks. Use a virtual clock for expiry/retention and workflow tests, including a date beyond one year to prove no automatic source-blog or trash purge.
64.3 Golden end-to-end journey
Demonstrate the user's original journey: tagged visit → approved consent choice → eligible A/B variant → CTA click → qualifying pricing visibility → resource click → form or chat capture → committed lead → owner assignment → eligible follow-up → trusted qualification → sale/booking where activated → permitted ad feedback → reporting reconciliation. Optional precise location is a separate deliberate service task with refusal/manual fallback.
Repeat with optional tracking refused: the site, form, chatbot and core service still work; no prohibited earlier history, audience membership or ad sends appear. Repeat with malformed UTMs, disabled storage, blocked tags, provider outage and duplicate submission. The report must show unknown/not collected where appropriate, not fabricate a complete journey.
64.4 Owner independence and preservation demonstration
The owner creates a custom collection from approved fields, edits a page, recovers a draft, resolves a conflict, imports synthetic content, publishes a release and rolls back safely. They build a campaign URL, simulate/publish/pause a workflow, inspect a score, review a segment, manage an inbox case and resolve a feed or delivery error where activated.
Then an editor manually trashes a blog with a reason. Verify actor/time, route/search/chat invalidation, no automatic expiry, safe restore and separate independent purge approval. Run cleanup jobs after advancing the clock, attempt unauthorized deletion, deactivate the deleting actor and restore a backup. Source preservation and attributable audit must remain correct; lawfully purged data must not be resurrected.
64.5 Release gates and evidence packet
Store the evidence packet with release commit/build, migrations, configuration versions, content manifest, provider/API versions, test reports, accessibility/performance results, risk exceptions, backup/restore evidence, owner training and approval. Sensitive traces remain restricted. A checklist tick without a linked observation is not evidence.
64.6 Production activation and aftercare
Use approved change windows, backups, migration checks, cache/domain validation and production smoke tests. Activate optional tags, messaging, audiences and spend in controlled stages only after their prerequisites pass. Monitor live destination errors, rejected conversions, lead routing, consent, billing and support load. Confirm both success and fallback paths with synthetic data.
Define rollback/stop thresholds and who can act. A critical privacy/security issue stops affected processing even when campaign performance appears positive. After stabilization, review defects and update the master requirements through change control. Completion means the approved scope passed with disclosed residual risks, not that no future maintenance will ever be needed.
Final edition integration: Execute Appendix N in addition to inherited tests; use Chapter 78 and Appendix P for final status and sign-off.
65 | Delivery governance, operating calendar and living standard
65.1 Scope, procurement and accountable ownership
The owner approves business objectives, content, activated modules, budgets, data purposes and service expectations. Product/design owns journeys; engineering owns implementation and integrity; marketing owns campaigns and metric interpretation; operations owns deployment/recovery; a qualified privacy/security/legal reviewer owns applicable decisions. Name a backup for critical roles. Small teams may combine roles but must preserve independent approval where required.
Evaluate build-versus-buy against owner workflow, accessibility, security support, integration/export quality, licensing, price, limits, region and operational skill. A self-hosted product still has dependencies and maintenance. A managed product may be appropriate when it passes ownership/exit requirements. No paid tool is mandatory solely because it appears as an example in a source reference.
65.2 Phased delivery and revised estimation
The earlier core schedule is an illustrative historical baseline, not a commitment for the expanded profile. Estimate the selected v1.2 work packages, content readiness, provider approvals, statistical design, legal review, migration, owner training and operating support separately. Do not pretend a complete custom CMS/workflow/ads platform fits a brochure-site budget.
No phase is complete because a menu exists. Dependencies, blocked provider permissions and unfinished business inputs remain explicit. A reduced first release is allowed only through an approved scope profile, not an undocumented omission.
65.3 Cost and capacity worksheet
Budget one-time discovery/design/build/migration/training separately from recurring infrastructure, maintenance, content, ad spend, messaging/calls, tools, analytics, AI, geo and transaction charges. Include logs, backups, export/egress, retries, staging, security review, support and taxes. Recheck current vendor prices when estimating a real project rather than putting transient prices into an evergreen standard.
Model traffic and business volume, not only registered users. Estimate page transfer, eligible events, messages, peak concurrency, database growth, storage retention, index overhead and restored-data size. Use low/base/high scenarios and an explicit margin for uncertainty. Cost controls must degrade optional services safely and never discard acknowledged leads or preserved source content.
65.4 Operating calendar
The exact cadence is approved per risk. Time-critical security or legal action must not wait for the next scheduled meeting. Appendices K and L provide operating and verification templates.
65.5 Handover and independence
Deliver business-owned accounts, source, deployment and rollback instructions, migrations, content/media and configuration exports, event/metric dictionaries, provider maps, privacy/retention decisions, tests, monitoring, recovery and support contacts. Transfer credentials securely, revoke temporary access and validate recovery. A second developer deploys from repository instructions; a second operator completes core owner tasks.
The owner can manage approved content, templates, forms, campaigns, workflows and settings without routine code edits. New executable components, sensitive processing, infrastructure changes and major upgrades remain qualified maintenance work. This boundary protects independence without granting unsafe arbitrary production access.
65.6 Standard maintenance and final implementation instruction
Use this edition with a completed site brief and the activated requirement register. Preserve the original Chapters 01–41 and the v1.2 additions. Complete every applicable requirement across frontend, API, database, provider, owner workflow, security/privacy, logs, tests and runbook. Report statuses honestly and attach evidence. Never fabricate business facts, tracking identities, precise coordinates, provider acceptance, test results or rankings.
Maintain a version history and deprecation register. For each external rule/API, record official source, last checked date, applicable account/version, owner, next review trigger and tests. New platform features or industry requirements are evaluated through the same completeness process. A reusable website bible is a maintained standard with accountable evidence, not a promise that one document can freeze the entire future web.
Final edition integration: Use the v1.3 source-merge and clarification register in Appendix M. Freeze this edition per project and maintain reviewed provider/business changes.
66 | Current search surfaces, AI discovery and platform-change controls
66.1 The search-surface register
This chapter extends Chapters 10, 11, 49 and 50. Deliver an owner-visible Search Readiness register with a separate row for ordinary web search, local discovery, product results, image/video search, news/Discover, eligible jobs/events, generative search and any selected regional search feature. Record the intended audience, canonical routes, required business facts, public/private boundary, provider account, supported markup/feed, evidence, status and review owner. A page may be eligible for several surfaces without obtaining exposure on any of them. Do not aggregate eligibility flags into a fabricated Google ranking score.
For each surface, distinguish not applicable, configured, technically validated, submitted where applicable, observed in provider reporting, restricted and awaiting evidence. The public site must remain usable while a verification or reporting feature is unavailable. Search Console property ownership, domain variants and staff recovery are business assets, not agency-only access.
66.2 Generative search: eligibility and observable reporting
The official AI optimization guidance consulted for this edition requires ordinary indexing/snippet eligibility and inclusion in Search generative AI features through Search Console. It does not prescribe special AI markup or llms.txt as a ranking mechanism. Treat that account setting as a specific launch check rather than assuming technical SEO alone confirms activation. [R107]
Google now documents a Generative AI performance report for Search, including AI Overviews and AI Mode impressions. The report may be absent when there are insufficient impressions. Its dimensions and timezone must be preserved; absence is not evidence of a broken website, and impressions are not a visitor identity or a sale. [R108]
Implementation: add search_surface_settings and search_report_snapshots with property, surface, source period, timezone, canonical URL, metric definition, ingestion time and provenance. Check supported API/export access independently from UI availability. When an API does not expose a report, provide a documented manual evidence upload instead of scraping authenticated interfaces or fabricating a connector. Label unavailable dimensions and never join aggregate AI impressions to named visitors.
66.3 Additional discovery features and other search engines
For an eligible publication, record whether readers can select it as a preferred source. Google's current guidance supports domain/subdomain-level sources and gives button/deep-link options; a blog subdirectory is not itself an eligible source identity. This is a reader-controlled discovery feature, not a purchased or universal ranking boost. [R109]
The regional search-feature register must evaluate the company's actual location, query category and published eligibility. Google's documented regional experiences include supplier/aggregator and other specialized units; they are not available to every business in every country. [R110] Keep eligibility evidence and a meaningful fallback; never create a false address to qualify.
Include Bing Webmaster Tools and other search/discovery providers selected by the audience in the account register. For participating search engines, IndexNow can notify changed URLs after ownership-key validation; acceptance does not mean indexing, and it is not a general Google indexing mechanism. [R111] Use one publication event to enqueue eligible sitemap/feed/index-notification updates. Keep provider-specific adapters, rate limits, duplicate handling, last-known state and disabled-provider behavior.
66.4 AI-generated media: preserve provenance while removing private metadata
The updated Google guidance explicitly extends human review to generated titles, descriptions, structured data and alt text. It also describes Merchant Center requirements for AI-generated image metadata and separately labeled generated product data. [R112] The media pipeline must therefore remove unnecessary private EXIF data without blindly stripping mandatory provenance, rights or AI-generation metadata.
Classify metadata fields as private/remove, rights/preserve, required-by-destination/preserve, or unknown/review. Record whether the original is human-created, generated, materially edited or uncertain. Save source permission, approved transformations and a derivative manifest. Validate the actual exported image/feed, not merely the original upload. A loss of required metadata blocks the affected feed, not deletion of the source asset. Avoid representing an AI-generated person as a real employee or customer testimonial.
66.5 Agent-compatible sites without weakening authorization
Semantic labels, stable controls, explicit prices, accessible states and durable confirmations also help browser agents interact with sites. Current web.dev guidance discusses screenshots, DOM and accessibility-tree interaction and identifies WebMCP as an evolving proposal. [R113] Agent compatibility is a conditional interface capability, not a Google ranking requirement.
An agent action must pass the same account, object, payment, consent and confirmation controls as a human action. Reading a page does not grant permission to submit an order or export records. Any WebMCP, MCP, commerce protocol or machine-action adapter needs a version-pinned schema, supported-client decision, delegated authority, short-lived credentials, replay protection, transaction preview and cancellation policy. Do not expose an unauthenticated tool that bypasses checkout or allows content purge. Unsupported agents retain the ordinary accessible human workflow.
66.6 Completion and source freshness
Deliver the surface register, approved Search Console settings record, source-appropriate schema/feed fixtures, AI-media metadata tests and a reporting capability matrix. Check the official update register before implementation and launch; changes affecting an active surface create a reviewed migration task. [R114] The exact UI labels and account availability are recorded in the project's evidence, not assumed from a permanent document screenshot.
67 | Google Ads visibility, bidding economics and operating playbook
67.1 Translate “rank at the top” into a business objective
Separate visibility, qualified demand and profitable revenue. Google's Ad Rank depends on auction-time factors, and the displayed Quality Score is diagnostic rather than an auction input. Top and absolute-top impression rates describe served impressions; impression-share metrics use eligible opportunities and are different denominators. [R115] The owner must select which outcome matters before choosing a bid strategy.
Target impression share can seek top, absolute-top or other Search placement, with a maximum CPC bid limit. It is a visibility strategy, not a guarantee that every auction will be won or that leads will be profitable. [R116] For a conversion-led business, test economic outcomes before choosing maximum visibility as the default. Do not click the business's own live ads to inspect position; use available preview/diagnostic tools and account evidence.
67.2 Campaign architecture and exclusions
Create an account/campaign/ad-group/asset/destination map. Separate objectives, markets, budget owners, conversion definitions and business economics when those differences justify separate campaigns. Record brand/non-brand treatment, competitor-term policy, search partners, language, geography, location-option semantics, device behavior, schedule, landing locale and existing-customer policy. Do not fragment campaigns merely to increase the number of rows in the console.
Each keyword and negative-keyword change needs the match type, scope, language, reason and reviewer. Search-term review must distinguish unsuitable intent, actual out-of-area demand, policy risk, ambiguous queries and potentially useful discovery. A negative at account level has a different impact from an ad-group negative. Test conflicts that block intended services. Keep available search-term coverage limitations visible; an omitted row is not proof no query occurred.
Automated URL expansion, generated copy, brand controls and final-URL exclusions require a pinned approved configuration. Never allow a campaign to route people into login, staff, draft, expired-offer or irrelevant blog routes. Restrict source feeds and public content used to generate claims. A crawler must see the same substantive offer, prices and eligibility as ordinary users under equivalent state.
67.3 Goals: inspect effective bidding, not just labels
Maintain one business conversion catalogue across website, CRM and the advertising account. Resolve effective goals at campaign level: account-default goals, campaign-specific standard goals, custom goals and included actions. Google's current documentation states that an action included in a custom goal can be used for bidding even when labeled secondary. Therefore, checking only the primary/secondary column is insufficient. [R117]
Create a goal-resolution test that exports the campaign's effective actions and compares them with the approved list. Flag pricing views, downloads, chat opens and repeated lifecycle updates when accidentally biddable. A website event name, GA4 key event and Google Ads conversion action are different configuration objects; maintain their exact mapping. A changed goal requires marketing approval, impact review and a dated reporting annotation.
67.4 Value, budget and maturation decisions
Store the conversion value policy, currency, tax/shipping treatment, refund adjustment and whether value is observed revenue, expected value or a nonmonetary proxy. Do not submit full customer lifetime value as collected cash. Lead qualification lag, repeat enquiries and repeat purchases need separate counting decisions. Reconcile the conversion cohort before concluding that this week's clicks produced this week's sales.
Use a preapproved low/base/high acquisition model. For illustration only: 1,000 paid clicks at 20 currency units cost 20,000; 5% accepted leads gives 50; 40% qualification gives 20; 20% close gives four customers. Cost per customer is 5,000. With 6,000 contribution before advertising per customer, contribution after advertising is 4,000 total, before other operating costs. A higher top-impression rate that doubles CPC without improving outcomes can erase that margin. These are hypothetical inputs, not predicted results.
Budgets need daily/account limits where genuinely supported, approval thresholds, currency, tax/fee assumptions, observation lag and emergency authority. Treat local alerts as alerts, not absolute remote spend caps. Do not automatically scale budgets from an immature cohort or a data outage. Review provider recommendations individually; an optimization score is not a substitute for the business objective.
67.5 Diagnose before changing bids
67.6 Required evidence
Deliver the campaign brief, effective-goal export, destination and asset manifest, budget authority, real-account policy status, reporting-source definitions, synthetic lead-to-CRM proof and correction/retry evidence. Reporting access, conversion ingestion, audience writes and campaign writes must be independently tested. A campaign must not begin or resume active spend while its intended destination, lead durability or known conversion configuration is broken.
68 | Meta Ads delivery, creative testing and lead-quality operations
68.1 Define success without a fictitious website rank
Meta placements do not give a website one permanent numbered position comparable to an organic search result. The operating objective is useful delivery to eligible audiences and verified business outcomes at acceptable cost. Meta's public auction help describes estimated action rates and ad-quality considerations; several full help/API pages require authenticated access, so account-specific controls remain evidence checks. [R118]
Maintain an objective/performance-goal/conversion-location register for awareness, traffic, engagement, leads, app promotion and sales where available. Treat these as planning categories; validate actual current account choices and the API mapping before activating a control. Website, instant-form, messaging, app and shop destinations have different observable journeys and cannot share fabricated website session evidence.
68.2 Creative operations as a maintained system
For each creative, store a concept ID distinct from individual exports, intended audience/problem, hook, proof, offer, CTA, source content, asset rights, creator consent, brand guidelines, expiry, subtitles, thumbnail, disclosure and allowed placements. Produce approved landscape, square and vertical derivatives as needed; preview current safe areas and UI overlays rather than relying on one hardcoded ratio forever.
Test concept changes separately from small copy changes where the analysis needs isolation. Keep a control and a predeclared outcome window. Review landing message match, mobile readability, sound-off comprehension and claims before release. A higher video-view rate does not by itself establish better customer acquisition. Automated creative enhancements require a documented review and rollback path; generated scarcity, altered prices or invented proof must be rejected.
Manage comment moderation and customer questions under a fair policy. Escalate complaints, accessibility issues, fraudulent impersonation and broken destinations to a named operator. Do not delete legitimate criticism solely to manufacture social proof. Creator/partnership advertising needs the relevant rights, account permissions, revocation process and ad-use approval; a public social post is not blanket advertising permission.
68.3 Audience, placement and budget decisions
Record hard targeting controls separately from platform suggestions or expansion inputs. Include geography, language where supported, audience source, exclusions, special-category applicability, existing-customer treatment, placement configuration, attribution settings, optimization event, schedule, budget and bid/cost strategy. Preview the effective configuration after remote publication; a local form's saved values are not proof of applied settings.
Use experiments to evaluate broad versus approved first-party audiences when eligible rather than assuming one is universally superior. Document audience overlap and shared budgets across tests. Prevent website personalization from making discriminatory or sensitive-trait assumptions. Do not invent a fixed universal learning-period duration, daily event threshold, frequency limit or ideal click-through rate; record the current account guidance and an explicit business guardrail.
68.4 Three lead paths with separate truth
Website lead: the backend commits an enquiry and stores the conversion event ID before the browser or server represents it as an accepted lead. Native lead: authenticate the provider retrieval, preserve native lead/form/ad identifiers and disclosed capture purpose, and create a lead without a fictional page-view chain. Messaging lead: distinguish click-to-message, actual inbound message, support conversation, confirmed structured capture and staff qualification.
All three paths must deduplicate provider retries without merging genuinely separate enquiries merely because phone or email matches. Use one qualification rubric and record rejection reasons such as spam, duplicate attempt, wrong service, unserviceable area or unreachable declared contact. Keep reason descriptions and private transcript details out of ad payloads. Measure response time from a trusted arrival time; a bot greeting is not a human agent response.
68.5 Diagnostics and conversion health
68.6 Guarded optimization and completion
Keep changes small enough to explain and large enough to answer a business question. Record each audience, budget, creative, goal and attribution change with date and reason. Use supported A/B or lift tools where eligible; do not interpret platform-attributed revenue as causal uplift. An automated recommendation can produce a draft change, but spending changes require explicit authority and remote-state confirmation.
Acceptance requires an actual account/asset/permission map, tested selected destinations, approved final event field map, browser/server same-action deduplication evidence, native-lead or messaging delivery tests where activated, sensitive-data rejection fixtures and CRM/reporting reconciliation. Denied collection or account ineligibility must produce a truthful disabled state, not a workaround that evades user choice or platform enforcement.
69 | Conversion engineering, SDK controls and destination contracts
69.1 One authoritative event with several representations
The business backend creates a stable event for a committed transition. A browser representation, an ad-platform representation and an internal warehouse row may refer to that transition, but they are not three extra leads. Store business_event_id, business_type, business_record_id, lifecycle_version, occurred_at, recorded_at, source_channel and allowed context references. Define a separate delivery identity for each destination configuration.
For every destination record the exact event/action name, source authority, counting unit, value rule, timestamp format, allowed identity fields, consent mapping, deduplication rule, supported late-arrival/correction window, retry class, receipt semantics and test fixture. External contracts are versioned snapshots, not strings sprinkled through components. Persist original occurrence time and do not make an expired event “fresh” by changing its timestamp.
69.2 Google ingestion and credential changes
The current offline/enhanced-lead guidance directs new integrations to Data Manager. The legacy Google Ads upload documentation retains the June 2026 access restriction and now notes a transition from developer tokens to Google Cloud projects. Use the current authentication and account-access contract rather than hardcoding a historical developer-token assumption. [R119] Reporting and account writes still need their separately selected API and permissions.
Google also documents a Data Manager path for GA4 recommended/custom server events. This has property/stream authorization and event timing requirements; it must not be silently added in parallel with an existing sender. [R120] Select an ingestion route per event family, document any migration, run dual-path validation only with isolated test destinations, and switch ownership without duplicate production conversion delivery. Browser/session context requirements and provider diagnostics still apply.
Keep ordinary GA4 event parameters free of direct contact identity. A separately supported user-provided-data feature is a distinct restricted destination contract, not permission to put email or phone into every event, user property or dataLayer field. Default it off until the actual account, purpose and field rules are approved.
69.3 Meta event and user-data mapping
The official Meta server-event SDK exposes event_name, event_time, event_id, action_source, source URL and user/custom data. Its event time is expressed in Unix seconds, not JavaScript milliseconds. [R121] Implement an explicit adapter with tested unit conversion, original event identity and action-source provenance. Do not assign website as the source to every offline or native-platform lead.
Meta's official user-data implementation handles different identifiers differently: certain contact fields are normalized/hashed, while network/browser and click-derived fields have their own handling. [R122] Never apply one generic hash function to every field, double-hash known hashed inputs, fabricate missing fbc/fbp values, or replace a visitor IP with the worker's egress IP. Preserve the original business contact unchanged; destination normalization occurs only in its reviewed projection.
69.4 Validate after SDK serialization, not only before it
A concrete integration risk is automatic SDK enrichment. The inspected Meta server-event implementation can fill missing fbc, fbp, client IP, source URL and referrer URL from request context during normalization. [R121] Therefore, a safe input object does not prove the transmitted payload is safe.
Required design: disable automatic request-context enrichment by default; never pass a complete incoming request or database record into a matching SDK. Build the destination projection explicitly, serialize with a pinned supported SDK, then validate the final payload against an allowlist and sensitive-value fixture before network transmission. Re-evaluate current policy/withdrawal immediately before dispatch. A serialization-added forbidden field causes suppression and an operational alert. Audit the SDK/configuration change without logging the sensitive payload.
A provider field called opt_out, limited-data-use or a similar processing control is not interchangeable with the site's consent decision. For example, the inspected server-event SDK describes opt_out as affecting optimization while still allowing attribution use. [R121] The site's no-send decision must stop delivery; setting a field on a still-transmitted request is not an adequate substitute.
69.5 Normalization and reconciliation fixtures
Create fixed synthetic fixtures for mixed-case email, whitespace, Unicode, international phone formats, country-specific postal data, invalid input, already-hashed input, genuine opaque click IDs and absent identity. Verify each against the selected destination's current formatting rules. Do not assume Google Ads, Google Analytics, Customer Match and Meta all normalize the same way. Any documented ambiguity creates a provider contract-review item instead of altering stored customer information.
Reconcile committed, eligible, suppressed, queued, attempted, accepted, rejected, matched where observable and attributed outcomes separately. Capture partial batch failures by item, not only HTTP status. Network timeout is uncertain delivery, not guaranteed failure; reuse the original idempotency mechanism and reconcile before unsafe retry. Corrections/refunds are distinct immutable business facts and use only supported destination adjustment mechanisms.
69.6 Data minimization and failure contract
The business must still accept a valid enquiry when optional tracking fails. An event without eligible marketing identity may be a valid internal business fact but ineligible for an ad destination. Keep unknown/refused/expired fields distinct from direct traffic or zero value. A critical policy or serializer failure suspends the affected destination, not source leads or retained blogs.
Deliver destination_contract_versions, payload_validation_results, conversion_goal_snapshots, delivery_attempts and reconciliation_runs with restricted roles and retention. The owner can inspect safe reason codes, retry eligible failures and see data freshness without revealing raw identities. Appendix O supplies an internal example; it is intentionally not a copy-paste provider request.
70 | Technical SEO edge cases and operational search reliability
70.1 Test the actual edge response
Validate responses at the public hostname through the real CDN/WAF, not just localhost or the origin. Compare status, redirect, content type, canonical, robots, language, main content, metadata, schema and caching for each representative route. Test desktop/mobile user agents, plain fetch, JavaScript rendering and social preview without changing substantive claims for reviewers.
For streamed/server-rendered applications, establish how unknown slugs and authorization failures receive the correct status before a response is irreversibly committed. A styled Not Found component sent with HTTP 200 is not a correct removal policy. A skeleton followed by a failed client fetch is not acceptable indexable main content. Test host-header poisoning, forwarded-host normalization and canonical generation behind every supported proxy profile.
70.2 Route decision table
70.3 robots.txt, XML and crawler access
Serve a deliberate, versioned robots.txt at the intended host/scheme/port. Google's documented treatment of robots.txt 4xx and server failures differs; returning 403 is not a reliable way to throttle crawling. [R123] Check the actual edge response during WAF challenges, bot mitigation, deploys and failover. Keep alerts for accidental disallow-all, invalid content type, oversized/generated files and stale cached policies.
Check sitemap escaping, canonical host, deduplication, indexable status, real modification dates, size/URL limits and nested sitemap references. Remove a URL from the relevant derived feed after manual unpublication while preserving source records. Verify recognized crawlers using the current official method before granting operational exceptions; a claimed user agent never grants authenticated access or different prices. Do not assume every bot is malicious or every automated request is a human visit.
70.4 Complex URL and content behavior
Test trailing slashes, percent encoding, case, Unicode normalization, repeated parameters, empty values, fragments, locale prefixes, alternate hostnames and invalid dates. Resource IDs and signed values may be case-sensitive. Normalize only according to the approved route contract. Never include personalized tokens or unbounded UTM combinations in internal links, canonicals, sitemaps or indexed page titles.
For product variants, choose canonical/URL/Offer representation based on actual distinct content and selection state; do not hide all useful variants or publish an unlimited permutation space. For pagination, provide meaningful page URLs and real links; do not canonicalize different result pages indiscriminately to page one. For international content, test missing/reciprocal alternatives, fallback language, user override, region-specific pricing and date/currency display independently.
Public PDFs, documents and downloadable resources need file-level index/access policy, MIME type, filename sanitation and content headers. Robots directives on the HTML download page do not automatically protect an independently public file. Media rights/provenance and privacy metadata must survive the approved derivative process. Public JSON endpoints require a deliberate discovery/security decision, not accidental exposure of every CMS field.
70.5 Search incidents and migrations
Create a diagnostic order: confirm measurement freshness and scope; identify affected templates/locales/surfaces; compare release/configuration history; test edge responses, index controls and content; inspect available Search Console/manual-action/security evidence; then assess demand, competition and official change notices. Do not assume every decline is an algorithm penalty or apply automatic disavowal/mass deletion.
A migration release manifest includes old URLs/assets, equivalents, redirects, domains/subdomains, external links, forms, tracking IDs, feed identities, subscriptions, signed-resource obligations and rollback constraints.
