Maintain redirects and monitor abandoned routes according to an approved operating period. A rollback must not lose new leads or resurrect lawfully deleted records. Track search and paid-destination recovery separately because their processing timelines differ.
70.6 Required delivery
Ship the route registry, edge-response test suite, bounded crawler, sitemap/feed reconciler, preview checker, robots-diff approval, Search Health dashboard and incident runbook. Every SEO change carries an expected effect, affected routes, approver, validation evidence and rollback decision. Do not let an automated SEO repair tool change production redirects, canonical settings, published claims or deletion state without the required approval.
71 | Full-funnel marketing, distribution and customer lifecycle
71.1 Research, positioning and the offer system
Maintain documented audience problems, buying triggers, decision roles, alternatives, objections, constraints, languages and accessibility needs. Use consented interviews, actual sales/support questions, permitted analytics and dated public-market research. Keep assumptions separate from observed evidence. A competitor claim must have a dated source and fair context, not an AI-generated assertion.
Every offer needs a stable ID, owner, audience, outcome, inclusions, exclusions, price/fee/tax presentation, validity, delivery capacity, eligibility, proof and support promise. Map it consistently across ads, landing pages, forms, downloads, bot answers, email, quotes and transactions. A central approved offer revision prevents a cheaper old PDF or chatbot response from contradicting checkout. Preserve accepted customer terms separately from later marketing edits.
71.2 The channel portfolio
The selected provider register can include Google, Meta, Microsoft/Bing, LinkedIn, TikTok, Pinterest, YouTube and other audience-relevant services. A name in the catalogue does not claim an implemented adapter. Each activated integration requires current permissions, supported fields, security, pricing, export, deletion and production evidence. Use native tools when safer than building unnecessary campaign-write interfaces.
71.3 Content distribution and reputation
Convert one approved source into appropriate articles, videos, resources, email and social excerpts with consistent claims and rights. Track reuse, canonical/original-source decisions, translation review and takedown propagation. Do not create thin near-duplicates solely to dominate query variations. Sponsored content, affiliates and customer endorsements need the applicable disclosures and current platform/legal review.
Provide a claims/evidence library, press kit, verified company information, correction contact and review-request workflow. Ask eligible customers neutrally; do not purchase fabricated reviews, selectively route only happy customers to public reviews or make a reward dependent on positive sentiment. Handle allegations and removal requests through documented fair moderation and protected audit. Never publish private customer details to rebut a complaint.
71.4 Lifecycle programmes and sales service levels
Map prospect → accepted enquiry → assigned → contacted → qualified → agreed transaction → onboarding → successful service → repeat/renewal or cancellation. Each stage needs a trusted event, responsible team, entry/exit rules, response target, communication purpose and exception queue. Prospects, current customers, applicants, donors and support requesters may need different lifecycles and must not be collapsed into one universal funnel.
Approved nurture examples include requested-resource delivery, consultation reminders, onboarding guidance, relevant renewal notices and eligible re-engagement. Enquiry acknowledgment is distinct from marketing subscription. A completed sale, opt-out, legal hold, complaint or service issue may stop a sequence. Review total contact frequency across channels, not only per workflow. A customer must have a practical way to reach a human and correct their details.
71.5 Economics, experimentation and reporting
Define cost per accepted lead, cost per qualified lead, customer acquisition cost, realized return on ad spend, contribution after marketing, payback and retention with explicit numerator, denominator, cohort and currency. Label estimated lifetime value and modeled attribution. Avoid adding Google- and Meta-attributed sales together as extra orders. Discounting, refunds, sales labor and service capacity affect the business outcome.
Maintain a prioritized growth backlog with hypothesis, evidence, expected impact range, effort, risk, owner and test design. Use interviews/usability review when traffic is too low for meaningful A/B inference. Separate creative, audience, page and lifecycle experiments where causal claims require isolation. No experiment may weaken accessibility, consent, truthful pricing or customer cancellation rights to improve a headline conversion rate.
71.6 Completion and owner control
Deliver the market/offer brief, channel calendar, creative/evidence library, consented contact lifecycle, marketing automation runbooks and metric dictionary. The owner should be able to launch an approved campaign page, update an offer, prepare a resource, simulate follow-up, inspect qualification quality and see unresolved operational tasks without editing code. Real ad spending and externally published posts remain explicit authorized actions.
72 | Browser lifecycle, tracking accuracy and consent edge cases
72.1 Navigation has more than one lifecycle
A new server request, a client-side route transition, a browser back/forward restoration, a prerender and a hidden tab are different observations. The browser back/forward cache can restore a page and its JavaScript state without a new document request; pageshow with persisted identifies that restoration. [R124] Define whether restoration creates a new logical page view and ensure only one instrumentation owner implements it.
Prerendering support is browser-dependent and requires feature detection. A speculative document is not automatically an actual visit or experiment exposure. [R125] Separate prefetch, prerender, activation, visible render and user interaction. Do not emit a business conversion from page loading, nor create customer contact records because a link scanner fetched a form URL.
72.2 Visibility, engagement and event semantics
Pricing-section exposure requires the declared marker/visible-area and continuous-duration rule together with a visible document. Pause/reset timing on hidden state or interrupted visibility; do not count time spent in a background tab as reading. The Page Visibility API exposes visible/hidden state, not proof of attention or comprehension. [R126]
Use stable page_view_id, action_id, section_id and schema version. Record click intent, server authorization, accepted submission, paid order and actual provider-confirmed delivery separately. Element text, full form answers, typed chat messages and raw search phrases must not become event labels. Keyboard activation and touch should produce the same semantic action without double counting pointer and click handlers.
The event policy defines duplicate scope, event ordering, maximum event age, allowed client-clock skew, maximum payload and missing-state handling. A dropped browser event remains missing; do not manufacture a complete path from a later purchase. Authoritative outcomes come from the business service even when optional browser telemetry is absent.
72.3 Consent across tabs, sessions and embedded tools
Maintain one application policy revision and consent-state authority. Update eligible tabs and widgets after withdrawal or policy change using a tested same-origin mechanism, with server rechecks before external effects. A persisted old page or worker may hold stale state. On restoration, resynchronize consent, authentication and sensitive context before resuming collection or showing protected data.
Test accept analytics/refuse ads, refuse all optional purposes, later accept, later withdraw, expired consent, policy-version change, shared-device logout, blocked cookies and unavailable storage. Basic blocking remains the baseline unless an alternative has an approved legal/technical decision. Rejecting advertising must not disable access to ordinary pricing, contact forms or support. A browser's location grant is not analytics consent and does not authorize continuous location tracking.
Third-party chat/iframe scripts need a permission and origin contract. Transfer only an expiring scoped context reference to an approved origin; verify message origin/source/schema. Do not reuse a chat session as a covert marketing identifier. Cross-domain transitions require reviewed transfer/linker rules and no private data in public URL parameters.
72.4 Transport and offline behavior
Optional analytics may use bounded batching and a permitted retry queue, with expiry and withdrawal checks. Never create an unconsented persistent queue for later replay. A beacon or successful browser send only indicates a transport attempt, not a committed business outcome. Mobile termination and network interruption can prevent final events; do not rely on unload as the only flush or save mechanism.
Form retries reuse a stable submission key, while a genuinely new enquiry uses a new key. Offline UI says pending/not sent until the server commits. Explicit saved applications or checkout recovery need a separate protected draft workflow and clear user choice; abandoned keystrokes are not submitted leads. Pause uploads cleanly and show per-file validation/quarantine state without falsely claiming a complete application.
72.5 Bot, synthetic and scanner traffic
Maintain classifications for verified search crawlers, social previews, uptime checks, approved test traffic, suspected automation and unknown traffic. These classifications are operational signals, not secret personal identities. Email security scanners, link previews and prerenders must not consume one-time customer actions through GET requests. Do not permanently whitelist a user-agent string or let a bot bypass access control.
Synthetic journeys use isolated recipients and conversion destinations or supported test mode. Keep their source marker outside user-editable campaign data and prevent them from entering production audiences, scores and primary conversion bidding. Internal traffic rules are reviewed because shared networks and VPNs can include real customers. Show classification/version and uncertain coverage in analytics.
72.6 Acceptance
Test reload, route transitions, back/forward restoration, duplicate tabs, hidden documents, prerender activation/cancellation, expired session restoration, location refusal, storage denial, tracker blocking, slow networks and late acknowledgments. Use network payload inspection and backend records together. The goal is accurate defined observations with visible limitations, not a false claim to watch every action of every visitor.
73 | Owner, public-experience and content-completeness contract
73.1 Complete route and page states
The owner must be able to maintain the public home, offers/services/programmes, pricing, about/trust, blog/resources, contact and selected campaign pages, plus utility routes: accessible search, not found, temporary failure, offline, form confirmation, consent preferences and relevant policy pages. Enabled modules add their own account, order, booking, application or support states. Links to unavailable modules must not remain as decorative buttons.
Every route receives loading, empty, validation, refused, expired, unavailable, success and retry behavior where meaningful. Forms expose persistent labels, instructions, accessible errors and submitted confirmation. Navigation, locale selection, contact details and policy access must remain usable without chat, video, optional tracking or a heavy animation. Do not make a critical message visible only on hover or through sound.
73.2 Safe editing beyond a page-builder canvas
The editing system includes keyboard alternatives to dragging, responsive previews, section visibility rules, approved design tokens, link validation, alt-text checks, shared-block impact preview, autosave acknowledgment, draft recovery, undo/redo and concurrent-edit conflict resolution. It must show which content is live and which remains a draft. A crash after acknowledged save must recover that revision; unacknowledged local edits need an honest recovery status.
Allow approved custom collections with typed fields, validations, references, permissions, template bindings, search/index policy, data-class labels and import/export mapping. Separate collection schema changes from content entry changes. Removing a field, category, author or asset must not cascade-delete protected posts or revisions. Test cloning across sites/locales so identifiers, permissions and private settings do not leak.
73.3 Editorial and media operations
Provide editorial assignments, review comments, publication scheduling, timezone display, version comparison, correction history, source rights, author/reviewer profiles, taxonomy, related content, resource versions and coordinated release manifests. A global pricing/banner edit should show affected pages, ads, bot knowledge, downloads and active experiments before approval. Source revisions remain recoverable after manual trash; indexes and public caches are derived copies.
A media asset carries allowed use, attribution, license expiry, source consent, transformation history, focal point, accessibility information, privacy metadata policy and current references. A license expiry may suspend public display through a reviewed policy but cannot silently erase source history. Public video needs captions/transcript alternatives where required; downloadable forms and PDFs need an accessibility decision, not just accessible surrounding HTML.
73.4 Practical lead and support management
The owner sees new/unassigned/overdue enquiries, safe source context, submitted fields with provenance, verification state, assignee, stage, tasks, notes, communication preferences and integration status. Include saved views, safe exports, merge/unmerge where supported, correction and a spam-review path. Restrict bulk actions with preview, counts, approval and rollback/compensation decisions.
For support chat, show delivery/pending status, human-versus-bot identity, business hours, assignment, transfer, closure and fallback. Persist message order and retries without replaying human actions. A visitor can correct chatbot-extracted details before they become submitted contact information. An attachment or knowledge document must not inject instructions that change the bot's tool permissions.
73.5 Accessibility regression is a release requirement
The accessibility scope includes public pages, consent controls, forms, chatbot, checkout, documents and essential owner workflows. WCAG 2.2 includes criteria addressing focus, target size, dragging alternatives, redundant entry and accessible authentication; conformance cannot be inferred from a single automated score. [R127]
Record selected level/version and representative manual tests: keyboard-only, screen reader, zoom/reflow, text spacing, contrast, reduced motion, captions, accessible authentication, errors and timed interactions. Errors should retain valid non-sensitive input where appropriate without leaking it into analytics or browser-shared storage. A new theme, popup or campaign template must pass the same checks as the original site.
73.6 Owner acceptance pack
A second operator, using only the supplied guide and allowed permissions, must create a collection/page, recover a draft, resolve a conflict, publish/rollback a release, manage media, receive and qualify a lead, create a UTM link, pause an experiment/workflow, review conversion health and restore a manually trashed blog. Record failures as real usability defects. Routine autonomy does not grant arbitrary code execution, secret access or deletion of one's own audit history.
74 | Security, data and service-failure completion drills
74.1 Risk model and accountable controls
Use the selected OWASP ASVS version as a traceable verification source, with risk-appropriate coverage and independent assessment where warranted. ASVS is a verification standard, not an automatic certification created by installing a framework. [R128] Add a threat model for the actual business: account takeover, public upload abuse, cross-tenant access, malicious CMS content, payment/state manipulation, tracking leakage, compromised integrations and source-content loss.
Every secret, service account, external processor and privileged role needs an owner, purpose, scope, rotation/revocation procedure and recovery path. Customer and staff authentication remain separate. Recovery, social sign-in and SSO linking require verified ownership; a matching email string is not enough to merge accounts. Step-up authentication protects high-risk changes and irreversible actions without making normal accessibility impossible.
74.2 Cross-system data controls
Document the data path from browser/CDN through API, database, cache, object storage, workers, provider SDKs, analytics, backups and support tooling. Mark fields at each boundary. Redaction must apply to proxy access logs, APM, error traces, callback URLs, export filenames and AI traces as well as application logs. Prevent tenant or PII leakage through metrics labels and high-cardinality resource names.
Encrypt and restrict raw IP/coordinates/contact/transcripts when their purpose justifies retention. Keep marketing, security and service purposes separate. A data-rights request propagates to authorized derived stores and providers; delayed webhooks or a restored backup must not recreate prohibited records. Maintain minimal justified tombstones/suppression proof without copying deleted text into immutable logs.
74.3 Security and integrity test catalogue
74.4 Resilience as separate service objectives
Define separate objectives for public rendering, lead acceptance, owner editing, publication propagation, chat, reporting freshness and optional ad dispatch. A slow third-party API should not hold a lead database transaction open. Use bounded timeouts, retries with jitter, resource isolation, bulkheads/circuit breakers where justified, and explicit last-known-good public content.
Database operations must cover connection pools, query timeouts, long transactions, lock contention, index maintenance, replication lag, disk growth, PITR/log retention and restore compatibility. Test outbox backlog catch-up without exhausting the database or blasting duplicate messages. Concurrency assumptions must match the native and Docker profiles; Kubernetes does not change business correctness.
74.5 Disaster recovery, keys and destructive infrastructure
Back up content, media, durable business records, configuration references and required ledgers, with independent copies and a recoverable key strategy. Prove that backups can be decrypted and restored under staff/account loss. A backup-success badge without a usable key is not a recovery system. Protect production Terraform plans and storage/database deletion with separated permissions and approved manifests.
Restore to an isolated environment, reapply deletion/suppression ledgers, replace credentials safely, verify representative pages and transactions, then control outbox replay. Do not resend already completed payments, emails or audience actions after a restore. Measure actual recovery time and data loss against the approved objectives; record the gaps as launch blockers or approved bounded exceptions.
74.6 Operational evidence
Deliver threat/control mapping, selected-standard tests, dependency/SBOM/license records, sensitive-data fixtures, ingress/egress policy, provider kill switches, backup/restore evidence and a named support rota. Security tests, penetration assessment, accessibility evaluation and a document review are different activities; report each accurately. No critical risk becomes acceptable because an advertising metric improved.
75 | Conditional specialist capabilities and complete transaction lifecycles
75.1 The conditional-module contract
This chapter deepens Chapter 61 rather than enabling everything on every website. For each chosen module, add a business specification covering actors, public/admin routes, states, transitions, money, permissions, data sensitivity, notifications, external providers, cancellation/correction, retention, export and recovery. A row marked conditional must still receive a written applicability decision. Selection converts its complete lifecycle into required implementation scope.
For provider-led or regulated functions, document what the website owns versus what the specialist provider owns. Do not represent an integration link as an implemented specialist platform. Capability availability, legal entitlement, account approval and actual tested behavior are separate checks.
75.2 Commerce, rentals and service fulfilment
Commerce includes variants, search/filtering, stock/reservations, cart persistence policy, discounts/eligibility, tax/fees, shipping/service coverage, checkout, verified payment, order snapshots, fulfilment, tracking, cancellation, returns authorization, partial refunds, disputes, settlement and customer support. Store order-line prices and terms immutably. Gift cards, credits, loyalty and affiliate rewards need a balance/eligibility ledger and reversals, not a mutable total in the browser.
Rental, travel, hospitality and appointment modules add availability intervals, capacity, blackout dates, timezones, holds, deposits, cancellation/no-show policy, rescheduling, external calendar conflicts and settlement. Prevent double booking with transactional state. A payment arriving after a hold expires needs an explicit refund/rebook/manual-review policy. Restaurant/order-ahead use additionally needs fulfilment windows, inventory and reviewed dietary/allergen information; do not infer safety from an AI description.
75.3 B2B, memberships and professional portals
B2B requires organizations, seats/roles, invitations, verified membership, negotiated catalogues/prices, quote revisions/expiry, approvals, purchase-order references where needed, fulfilment and invoicing handoff. Tax/credit/payment terms require actual business approval. A quote approval must bind to the exact revision and customer organization.
Subscriptions, courses and memberships need entitlements, trial rules, usage/quota accounting, upgrades/downgrades, dunning, cancellation, refunds, access windows and customer-export obligations. Education adds enrolment, curriculum/versioning, progress, assessments, accessible materials, certificates only when implemented and guardian relationships where necessary. Live meetings, proctoring, secure examinations and credential accreditation remain separately specified systems, not assumed from a booking widget.
75.4 Marketplace, community, publishing and nonprofit
Publisher ads.txt/seller declarations, header-bidding or other supply-chain integrations apply only to the selected monetization arrangement and must be checked against current specifications. They are not prerequisites for buying Google or Meta ads. A forum or public-upload module cannot launch without moderation and abuse-response ownership.
75.5 Sensitive sectors and advanced interfaces
Health/home care, finance, insurance, legal, housing, public services and age-restricted businesses need a jurisdiction/sector pack with qualified review of claims, licences, intake, advertising, accessibility, records, required notices and escalation. Never populate this pack with invented approvals. Keep protected application/service routes separate from general marketing tags and AI training data.
Advanced interfaces such as maps/geospatial service boundaries, configurators, calculators, file collaboration, e-signatures, real-time sessions, PWA/offline, voice/camera/device access, 3D/AR, embedded applications and agent tools need an explicit business case. Provide accessible alternatives, capability detection, permission prompts, resource budgets and graceful failure. Do not treat every experimental browser API as a core dependency.
75.6 Acceptance pack for every selected module
Test a full successful customer/admin journey, a refused/failed request, concurrent last-capacity use, duplicate/out-of-order callbacks, price/terms change, cancellation/refund/correction, authorization attack, export/deletion and restore. Confirm communications stop or change when business state changes. Record the required evidence in the master release register rather than creating a specialist appendix that has no tests or operator.
76 | Integrated build contracts and the complete owner console
76.1 One consistent architecture
Retain the React/Next.js public frontend, React owner console, TypeScript contracts, NestJS modular backend, PostgreSQL, object storage and durable workers/outbox. Native and Docker operation remain equivalent supported profiles. Redis, Python, GraphQL, SOAP, Kubernetes, specialized analytics engines and agent protocols remain bounded choices. No module may require a second conflicting customer, content, consent or pricing source of truth.
Use a manifest for each module: capabilities, public/admin routes, event schema, entities, permissions, migration, providers, jobs, cost limits, observability, tests and disable/exit behavior. Module activation cannot bypass legal/provider restrictions. Content preservation, authoritative lead durability and protected audit cannot be disabled through ordinary marketing settings.
76.2 Owner-console information architecture
Every screen needs permission-filtered actions, useful empty states, pagination/search where relevant, accessible validation, safe exports, version conflicts and a clear pending-versus-completed status. Missing provider permission is an actionable blocked state, not a green connected badge.
76.3 Additional durable entities and API boundaries
Add search_surface_settings, provider_contract_versions, effective_conversion_goals, destination_payload_policies, final_payload_checks, search_health_runs, media_provenance_rules, campaign_approvals and release_evidence_items. These extend existing tables and do not duplicate acquisition_touches, leads, consent_records or audit_entries. Every record has trusted site/account scope, owner, version, timestamps, access class and an approved retention rule.
Representative new use cases: GET /v1/admin/search-surfaces; POST /v1/admin/search-checks; POST /v1/admin/conversion-goals/resolve; POST /v1/admin/provider-contracts/{id}/validate; POST /v1/admin/campaigns/{id}/preflight; GET /v1/admin/conversions/reconciliation; POST /v1/admin/releases/{id}/evidence. Public clients cannot call campaign writes, inspect raw identities or bypass server consent decisions. Responses document safe 403/409/422/429 and retryable failure behavior.
76.4 Repository responsibilities
apps/admin/src/features/search-surfaces/
apps/admin/src/features/conversion-goals/
apps/admin/src/features/campaign-preflight/
apps/admin/src/features/evidence-center/
apps/web/src/lib/lifecycle/{navigation,visibility,restoration}.ts
apps/api/src/modules/provider-contracts/
apps/api/src/modules/search-health/
apps/api/src/modules/conversion-goals/
apps/api/src/modules/media-provenance/
apps/worker/src/jobs/{payload-validation,search-reconciliation}/
packages/destination-policies/
packages/route-contracts/
packages/campaign-contracts/
tests/{browser-lifecycle,sdk-egress,goal-resolution,metadata-preservation}/
docs/{source-merge,provider-snapshots,launch-evidence}/
These are responsibility boundaries, not a request to generate empty folders. Keep provider SDKs out of public bundles and final egress validation outside the AI model. Test contracts against implemented handlers, a real test database and configured providers.
76.5 Versioned change and deactivation
A schema/API/event/SDK change requires compatibility fixtures, a migration plan, rollback or forward-fix decision and affected-owner training. Revalidate the final serialized payload after SDK upgrades. A changed tracking purpose invalidates old assumptions even when the code compiles. Old content revisions must remain renderable or migrate through a reviewed process.
Disconnecting a provider stops eligible queued work, revokes credentials where possible, preserves source business records and creates any necessary removal/reconciliation tasks. Deactivation must address navigation, routes, permissions, jobs, tags, cost and old customer obligations. A retired integration cannot silently reactivate through an imported configuration.
76.6 Work-package completion
A work package is complete only with working owner UI, authorized API, schema/migrations, business rules, positive and negative tests, observable failure states, real provider evidence where used, recovery/exit instructions and an accountable operator. Source code and provider configuration must be reproducible from business-owned accounts. The final handover includes a second-team deployment and a second-operator task demonstration.
77 | Paid-traffic launch gates and the first 90 days
77.1 Gate sequence
A red critical gate blocks the affected launch or spending path. A documented noncritical exception needs owner, expiry, mitigation and evidence; it cannot authorize prohibited data processing. Synthetic tests and account configuration review are necessary, but neither guarantees a future ad placement or absence of all defects.
77.2 Before the first paid visitor
Walk the exact ad or native lead path using approved preview/test tools. Check final destination after redirects, in-app browser, consent choices, experiment assignment, location refusal, download access and accepted submission. Confirm the salesperson received the stored lead, not merely an email generated by the browser. Verify the correct account/stream/dataset/campaign mapping and that test markers do not leak into production bidding.
Record baseline traffic, qualified lead definition, reporting timezone/currency, expected data delay, escalation contacts and temporary exclusions. Confirm support capacity and business hours. Publish real policies and truthful business identity; do not launch with placeholder fees, unverified certifications or fabricated testimonials.
77.3 Days 1–7: stability and data quality
Treat this as an illustrative operating schedule, adapted to the business and risk. Review destination errors, spend/billing, policy restrictions, source coverage, lead acceptance, unassigned enquiries, delivery failures, duplicate conversions and unexpected tags. Investigate discrepancies by pipeline stage, not by forcing dashboards to match. Respond to customer questions and verify that advertising promises match delivered service.
Do not make repeated unplanned changes that destroy the baseline. Urgent privacy/security, false-price or broken-journey issues are repaired immediately. Cosmetic preferences and exploratory growth ideas enter the backlog unless they block the core task. Keep a dated change log that marketing and engineering both use.
77.4 Days 8–30: qualified demand and usability
Review search intent, audience/creative response, mobile friction, spam, qualification reasons and response time using mature cohorts. Test a prioritized offer, creative or page hypothesis only when traffic and measurement permit it. Improve useful content, internal links, local profile consistency and resource quality. Recheck newly edited templates for performance/accessibility/consent regressions.
Inspect email authentication and unsubscribe, audience removals, workflow frequency, reporting freshness and SDK/configuration drift. Renew or retire time-bound assets/claims. Never automatically delete blogs because their first month's traffic is low.
77.5 Days 31–90: economics and operating maturity
Evaluate realized acquisition cost, contribution, retention, refunds, support burden and capacity. Separate observed attribution from incremental impact. Decide whether to scale, narrow, pause or redesign a campaign through approved evidence. Extend SEO coverage around genuine customer needs rather than mass-producing near-identical pages.
Perform a restore and account-recovery exercise, review permissions, rotate temporary access, test provider disconnect/reconnect, and verify no accumulated unhandled queues. Refine budgets and service objectives from measured load. A successful first launch does not end patching, content review, source-policy checks or operator training.
77.6 Incident and stop conditions
Stop the affected processing/spend when there is known prohibited data transmission, compromised credentials, persistent lost leads, misleading primary conversion goals, false offers or broken critical destinations. Record request to pause separately from confirmed remote pause, because provider state may be delayed. Keep public help/contact available where safe. Restoration requires a verified repair, regression evidence and explicit authorization, not only a restarted service.
78 | Final consolidated standard and no-silent-omission acceptance
78.1 What “final” means for this edition
This is the final consolidated baseline for the requirements discussed and supplied in versions 1.0, 1.1 and 1.2. It retains their substantive requirements and adds current provider checks, concrete edge cases, implementation contracts and acceptance evidence. Appendix M records the source comparison and deliberate clarifications. This is not a concatenation that makes developers search three competing versions.
Use Chapters 01–65 for the inherited architecture and comprehensive capabilities, Chapters 66–77 for the final operational expansion, and this chapter for release precedence. The word final identifies an approved reference edition, not a promise that all future browser APIs, industries, laws or platform features have already been invented or implemented.
78.2 Authoritative precedence and status
An applicable legal/provider prohibition takes priority over a business preference to collect data. A reviewed v1.3 clarification takes precedence over an earlier ambiguous label; unaffected source requirements remain in force. The comprehensive reusable platform delivers the foundation and declared growth capabilities. Each individual website explicitly activates supported functions and selected industry modules, with recorded reasons for exclusions.
Track five independent dimensions: specification status; applicability; implementation status; activation status; verification status. Not applicable is not equivalent to not implemented. Implemented but disabled is not equivalent to tested in production. Every requirement maps to an owner, public/admin workflow, API/data responsibility, permission policy, test and operating guide. No blank row is a pass.
78.3 The completeness review procedure
Run a structured review across acquisition, discovery, public UX, owner editing, lead/service capture, transaction lifecycle, customer support, privacy, security, data integrity, integrations, deployment, recovery, accessibility and business ownership. For every selected capability, ask how it behaves on success, refusal, invalid input, retry, concurrent use, provider failure, correction/cancellation, deletion and restoration.
Then test cross-capability intersections: an experiment changes a form while attribution persists; consent changes while a workflow is queued; a blog is removed while a chatbot index is stale; a price changes while a quote is open; a session is revoked while a page is restored; an SDK updates after a safe payload map was approved. These intersections, rather than more decorative features, are common places for silent omissions.
Use the 120-entry capability register, the inherited acceptance packs and Appendix N's final scenarios. Project-specific functionality discovered during review gets a new requirement ID and the same completion contract. An exceptional website type cannot be waved through by checking an unrelated generic row.
78.4 Preserved non-negotiable invariants
Source blogs, drafts, revisions, editorial history and trashed content do not expire automatically. Manual trash/restore/purge records the authenticated initiator, approver where needed, executor, object/version, time, reason and outcome. A worker may execute an exact human-approved purge, not decide what old content to delete. Personal visitor data still follows its approved retention and rights policy.
IP-based geography is approximate; contact identity comes from explicit capture or authenticated evidence. Browser coordinates require a visible task and permission. Website/chat/CRM use shared purpose-limited context. No unrestricted transcript, private form fields, password, payment secret or precise location enters generic analytics, advertising custom data or model context. An explicitly approved destination-specific network-identifier projection is the narrow exception described in Chapter 69, not general sharing.
An accepted lead is durably committed; a notification or ad API failure does not erase it. A browser event cannot assert a verified sale. Statistical winners, provider acceptance, account permissions and production-readiness claims must match evidence. No feature flags can silently bypass these invariants.
78.5 Final delivery and owner sign-off
Deliver source repository, lockfiles, migrations, environment/deployment instructions, API/contracts, content/media exports, route/campaign/event/metric registers, permissions, provider maps, costs, tests, diagnostics, recovery, support and owner training. Verify the final public domain and real configured providers with synthetic data. The second-team installation and second-operator walkthrough must not depend on the original developer's personal machine or private account.
Sign-off records what passed, failed, is blocked, remains unimplemented or is not applicable; critical launch blockers must be resolved before activation. The website implementation is accepted only against this evidence. Finishing a Word document does not itself run the website's tests, approve a legal position or win an advertising auction.
78.6 The practical success contract
The target is an owner-operated, accessible, secure, maintainable website with relevant content, effective marketing workflows, trustworthy conversions, controlled advertising operations and measurable customer value. Technical eligibility, landing-page quality, bidding, creative, competition, business economics and service quality must work together. Neither a framework choice nor this document can guarantee first position or profitable delivery in every auction.
Freeze the edition used by a project, record its approved deviations and maintain a short change register. Update for real new requirements and verified platform changes rather than repeatedly replacing the entire plan. This prevents missed obligations while keeping the standard usable as the single reference for building, launching and operating the website.
Appendix A | Site brief and activation worksheet
A.1 Complete before implementation
A.2 Feature activation record
A.3 Architectural decision template
Decision ID; question; business constraints; options considered; chosen option; reason; cost; privacy/security effect; operational owner; migration/exit path; review trigger; approval date. Record rejected options briefly so future teams do not repeat the same debate without new evidence.
A.4 Required version 1.1 decisions
Complete the manual-versus-annual deletion interpretation; independent purge approver; audit retention/assurance; IP collection purpose and retention; precise-location task, permission copy and disposal; chatbot provider/data handling; UTM taxonomy/lookback; experiment traffic/MDE/maturation; and context reveal/export permissions. The default is manual-only deletion with no scheduled annual purge. Use Chapter 41 and Appendix G before sign-off.
A.5 Version 1.2 decisions
Complete Appendix H for all capability categories and Appendix K for the actual business. Resolve custom collection scope, workflow/send purposes, scoring inputs, audience eligibility, sender domains, channels/call attribution, domain recovery, campaign-write authority, catalogue/feed obligations and current provider verification. Annual-only human blog deletion is not assumed; manual-only preservation remains the default.
Appendix B | Requirement traceability and release checklist
B.1 Core requirement register
Copy this table into the project's acceptance register and add implementation links, evidence, responsible person, and status. The table defines a baseline, not a record of completed implementation. Also execute Appendix G for the v1.1 additions.
B.2 Optional event extensions
For commerce, add the selected catalogue/cart/checkout events such as view_item, add_to_cart, remove_from_cart, begin_checkout, and purchase/refund according to current destination support. For bookings, define availability_search, booking_start, booking_confirmed, booking_rescheduled, and booking_cancelled. For subscriptions, distinguish trial, activation, renewal, failed payment, cancellation, and entitlement changes. Do not map every lifecycle event to a primary ad goal.
Use server authority for monetary outcomes, confirmed reservations, and entitlement state. Browser interactions remain intent/experience signals. Every extension follows the same purpose, schema, event identity, deduplication, retention, and test requirements as the core catalogue.
B.3 Sign-off record
Project/version: __________. Release date: __________. Business owner: __________. Technical owner: __________. Marketing owner: __________. Privacy/security reviewer: __________. Operations owner: __________.
Critical blockers: __________. Accepted exceptions with expiry and owner: __________. Restore test reference: __________. Owner training evidence: __________. Production smoke test: __________. Decision: approve / conditional approval with stated constraints / reject.
Version 1.2 evidence extension
Execute Appendix I in addition to the inherited core and v1.1 test registers. Map the 90 capability entries in Appendix H to site-specific scope and implemented evidence. No test or provider capability is marked Passed by the preparation of this specification.
Appendix C | Data relationships and implementation contracts
C.1 Relationship map
C.2 Example outbox SQL sketch
This deliberately limited example illustrates integrity requirements. It is not a complete production migration. Real implementation must add project-specific access rules, schema validation, retention, foreign keys, encryption decisions, worker leasing, and migration tests. Event payloads contain safe references, not unrestricted copies of contact records.
CREATE TABLE outbox_events (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  site_id uuid NOT NULL,
  business_event_key text NOT NULL,
  event_name text NOT NULL,
  schema_version integer NOT NULL CHECK (schema_version > 0),
  occurred_at timestamptz NOT NULL,
  payload jsonb NOT NULL,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  UNIQUE (site_id, business_event_key)
);
CREATE TABLE destination_deliveries (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY,
  event_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES outbox_events(id),
  destination_config_id uuid NOT NULL,
  state text NOT NULL CHECK (state IN (
    'pending', 'eligible', 'in_flight', 'accepted',
    'retry_wait', 'rejected', 'suppressed', 'dead_letter'
  )),
  attempt_count integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  available_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  last_error_code text,
  UNIQUE (event_id, destination_config_id)
);
CREATE INDEX deliveries_ready_idx
  ON destination_deliveries (available_at)
  WHERE state IN ('pending', 'retry_wait');
A uniqueness constraint prevents duplicate rows but does not implement the full workflow. The lead and outbox write must share a transaction. Worker claims require safe concurrency. An uncertain network result retries using the same event identity. A destination's accepted status must not be renamed “attributed” unless the provider actually supplies that evidence.
C.3 Publish contract
Input: page ID, expected draft version, approved revision ID, publication mode/time, actor, and reason. Preconditions: scoped publish permission, approved workflow state, valid block schema, allowed route, valid dependencies, and content checks. Commit: update live revision atomically, append audit, and create publication outbox. Side effects: invalidate affected routes, update search/sitemap indexes, and report publication status.
Failure behavior: no partial live revision update when validation fails; no overwritten concurrent edit; retries do not publish duplicate revisions; an invalidation failure becomes visible and retries safely. A price/availability transaction remains authoritative even while a public display is refreshing.
C.4 Integration capability test record
For each provider, record authentication, read/reporting, write, backfill, incremental synchronization, webhook receipt, token renewal/revocation, rate limiting, retries, duplicate handling, deletion/suppression, and production verification separately. Mark unsupported capabilities Not Applicable. A connected OAuth account does not prove any of the other capabilities.
C.5 Extended relationships
Chapter 40 owns the new experiment/context/conversation/deletion/audit entity contracts. Appendix F supplies safe context/event/audit examples and the field-level retention matrix. In particular, source-post removal cannot cascade to audit evidence, and a visitor-context reference does not grant access to contact details or another conversation.
Appendix D | Operating registers and decision templates
D.1 Provider and account register
D.2 Release evidence packet
Include the scope/decision register; module activation; content approvals; API and database migration versions; security/accessibility/performance results; tracking/consent diagnostics; form-to-CRM proof; infrastructure change plan; restore report; owner training; and residual risks. Store sensitive screenshots and logs in a restricted location, not a public repository. Include the new experiment, context, deletion and audit-verification registers.
D.3 Change request template
Requested change; business reason; affected pages/modules/data; owner-editable or code change; SEO/ads impact; event/schema changes; privacy/security impact; migration/rollback; estimated cost; tests; approval; release date; post-release check. A new form field or third-party widget can require a privacy review even when no backend schema migration is needed.
D.4 Content and campaign preflight
Confirm the offer and proof are accurate; destinations work; mobile/keyboard forms succeed; pricing and terms agree; page metadata/index policy is correct; campaign IDs follow the naming standard; primary conversions are appropriate; contact routing works; consent controls pass; no test identifiers remain; reporting freshness is visible; and someone will handle responses.
D.5 Risk and exception template
Risk ID; affected requirement; failure impact; likelihood assessment; mitigation; compensating control; acceptance owner; expiry/review date; monitoring; and closure evidence. Exceptions must be time-bounded where possible. An exception cannot transform an unlawful processing practice into an approved one; legal applicability must be assessed independently.
Appendix E | Plain-language glossary
Appendix F | Extended event, context and retention contracts
F.1 Additional event dictionary
These entries extend Chapter 14; they do not replace its existing page, pricing, download, form and trusted conversion events. Each implementation must record event schema, authority, permission, allowed fields, duplicate scope and destination mapping. Operational events are not automatically forwarded to analytics. Never forward all internal audit events to Google or Meta.
Do not create a second chat_lead conversion in addition to generate_lead for the same accepted enquiry. Store submission_channel=chat in the approved internal business record; destination mappings preserve the single agreed primary conversion. event_id is generated once for the business fact and reused by eligible retries/browser representations.
F.2 Safe shared-context example
Illustrative backend projection for an authorized chatbot task. Values are synthetic; fields may be absent when not permitted. This is not an authentication token and is not a payload to send wholesale to an advertising destination.
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "context_id": "ctx_example_01",
  "site_id": "site_example",
  "conversation_id": "conv_example_01",
  "policy_version": "visitor_context_v1_1",
  "page": {"page_id": "pricing", "revision_id": "rev_42"},
  "language": "en",
  "visitor_selected_topic": "consultation",
  "service_area": {
    "id": "area_example",
    "source": "visitor_selected",
    "verification_state": "declared"
  },
  "allowed_actions": ["answer_public_faq", "request_lead_capture"],
  "expires_at": "2026-10-06T12:30:00Z"
}
The backend may separately associate eligible touch/assignment references with the conversation. They are not needed in the model prompt. When context expires or a purpose is revoked, resolve a new permitted projection rather than trusting this snapshot. Raw IP, latitude/longitude, phone, email, click identifiers and transcript history are intentionally absent.
F.3 Manual deletion command and audit contract
The admin submits post_id, expected_version, operation type, reason and a confirmation binding. The backend derives actor/site/permissions from trusted authentication, checks dependencies/holds, and commits the state transition plus audit/outbox. The caller cannot supply an authoritative actor_id or approved=true field. Purge additionally resolves an independently approved, unexpired manifest.
{
  "audit_schema_version": 1,
  "audit_id": "aud_example_01",
  "operation_id": "op_example_01",
  "site_id": "site_example",
  "action": "content.trashed",
  "resource_type": "blog_post",
  "resource_id": "post_example_17",
  "resource_version": 9,
  "actor_type": "human",
  "actor_id": "staff_example_04",
  "reason_code": "owner_requested_removal",
  "outcome": "committed",
  "occurred_at": "2026-10-06T12:00:00Z",
  "request_id": "req_example_01",
  "changed_fields": ["deletion_state", "deleted_at", "deleted_by"],
  "request_context_ref": "restricted_context_example"
}
The restricted request-context reference is optional and purpose-bound. A safe audit entry is not permission to retain an entire IP/geo/profile forever. Keep the human initiator and execution service distinct for asynchronous purge; do not show only “system deleted this post.”
F.4 Retention and destination matrix
The following are proposed starting defaults, not legal periods. The approved site-specific schedule governs actual deployment. Every field must have a purpose, owner, access rule and disposal behavior before activation. A data class may require shorter processing or a justified longer hold.
F.5 Privacy-state acceptance matrix
No optional consent: serve the ordinary control page, permit functional support and a submitted enquiry, and avoid optional tracking identities/history. Analytics only: permit approved internal/GA4 analysis and eligible experiments under the declared purpose, but suppress advertising destinations. Ads permission does not by itself grant location permission or allow prohibited data. Location permission supports only the stated location task. Withdrawal revokes future optional use and queued dispatch according to policy; a separate rights process handles past records where applicable.
A stored consent record is necessary evidence where required, not an eternal override of current choices. Backend dispatch and context projection recheck current eligibility. Browser privacy controls, required opt-outs and applicable jurisdictional rules remain part of the project's reviewed policy.
Appendix G | Extended release checklist and evidence register
G.1 Mandatory test cases
These are requirements to execute, not passed results. Add code/test links, named owner, environment, evidence, run date and status to the project register. Test with synthetic visitors, contacts, locations and accounts; do not leak real personal records into test artifacts. Playwright supports geolocation emulation for automated fixtures; also verify actual supported mobile and in-app browsers. [R61]
G.2 Sign-off and unresolved decisions
Project/site: __________. Blueprint version: 1.3 Build/release: __________. Business owner: __________. Technical owner: __________. Marketing/analysis owner: __________. Privacy/security reviewer: __________. Operations/support owner: __________. Evidence location: __________. Decision/date: __________.
Confirm manually versus annually intended deletion language; precise-location task and necessity; raw-IP purpose/retention; applicable jurisdictions and age restrictions; chatbot provider/processing terms; experiment traffic and analysis settings; independent purge approver; audit retention/storage assurance; third-party reporting/measurement permissions; and operating budgets. Until clarified, use the conservative defaults documented in this version rather than inventing permission or enabling automatic deletion.
Approval requires no critical unauthorized collection, cross-visitor disclosure, unaudited destructive action, automatic blog purge, lost acknowledged lead, or untested restoration. A document requirement is not implementation evidence. A live experiment may be legitimately inactive, but the delivered capability and safety controls must still pass their acceptance tests.
Appendix H | Master capability coverage and activation register
H.1 How to use the catalogue
This register is a discovery and acceptance index, not a checklist of features to install blindly. F = universal foundation; G = comprehensive growth-platform capability, with public activation governed by purpose/provider approval; B = conditional business module. The site-specific register adds applicable, activation, implementation status, accountable owner, rationale, selected provider, costs, data policy and evidence. No blank decision is treated as approval.
H.1.1 Final edition capability extensions
CAP-001–CAP-090 remain unchanged. These 30 additional entries make final operating and cross-system requirements explicit. F, G and B retain their existing meanings; implementation and activation are separate decisions.
H.2 Required activation answers
For each selected row, answer: What customer problem does it solve? Who operates it? What data is necessary? Which channels/providers are used? What happens on failure/refusal? What costs recur? What is the source of truth? Which customer obligations survive disabling it? Which tests prove it? What is the exit path? Record Not Applicable with a reason where appropriate; do not leave blank rows that hide decisions.
H.3 Non-negotiable preserved requirements
No automatic deletion of blog source, drafts, revisions or trash. Human attribution and protected audit for deletion/restore/purge. No identity or precise address inferred as fact from an IP. No location prompt merely because chat opened. No unapproved history sent to a model. No full form values/transcripts/secrets in generic analytics. No fake business facts, conversion events, statistical winners, provider success or production-readiness claims. No ranking, complete-tracking or zero-maintenance guarantee.

This register adds 192 concrete acceptance scenarios to the inherited tests. It is not a test-execution report. Every row starts Not executed. Apply each row to the selected capability; an inapplicable business module requires an explicit rationale, not a fabricated pass. The evidence record includes environment/build, configuration and provider versions, steps, observed result, safe screenshot/log/receipt reference, executor, date, defect/exception and approver.
Use ID V12-CC-NN, where CC is the controlling chapter and NN is its case number. Core integrity/privacy cases remain applicable even when optional marketing collection is disabled.

Appendix J | Implementation contracts and worked examples
J.1 Contract status and common envelope
These examples define design responsibilities and representative shapes. They are not a complete deployable codebase or a substitute for current provider documentation. Implement runtime validation, authorization, migrations, error handling and tests in the selected stack. IDs and addresses below are synthetic. The application resolves site and actor from trusted context; a supplied identifier is never proof of permission.
A business command carries schema_version, operation_id/idempotency key, expected_version where relevant and approved input. The server records request_id, actor, site, policy_version and occurrence time. A response distinguishes committed, queued, rejected, conflicted and uncertain external status. Do not return reusable secrets or full internal records merely to simplify the frontend.
J.2 Draft save and conflict contract
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "expected_version": 17,
  "collection_schema_version": "events_schema_03",
  "fields": {
    "title": "Example open day",
    "summary": "Approved informational draft."
  }
}
The client sends this to the authorized draft endpoint with an idempotency key. After a successful database commit, the response contains draft_revision_id, version=18, saved_at and status=saved_draft. The editor may now show Saved. A stale version returns a safe conflict with the current version and authorized comparison reference. It does not merge or publish silently. Retries with the same key and same request return the same result; a different request with the same key conflicts.
Draft editing, autosave, preview, release approval and public publication are distinct transitions. A renderer binds fields to an approved template; it cannot execute a custom JavaScript field. Historical schema versions remain interpretable under reviewed migration rules.
J.3 Example workflow definition
{
  "workflow_id": "wf_lead_followup",
  "version": 3,
  "site_ref": "site_example",
  "subject_type": "lead",
  "trigger": "lead_accepted",
  "purpose": "service_enquiry_response",
  "reentry": "one_per_trigger_event",
  "nodes": [
    {"id": "assign", "type": "action", "action": "assign_team"},
    {"id": "ack", "type": "action", "action": "send_acknowledgment"},
    {"id": "wait", "type": "delay", "duration_policy": "response_sla"},
    {"id": "check", "type": "condition", "field": "lead.contacted"},
    {"id": "escalate", "type": "action", "action": "create_staff_task"},
    {"id": "done", "type": "exit"}
  ],
  "edges": [
    ["assign", "ack"], ["ack", "wait"], ["wait", "check"],
    ["check", "done", "true"], ["check", "escalate", "false"],
    ["escalate", "done"]
  ],
  "limits": {"max_actions": 8, "max_duration_days": 14},
  "marketing_branch": "not_enabled"
}
The durations are illustrative policy inputs, not guaranteed response commitments. A promotional branch requires a separately approved purpose and channel eligibility; the service acknowledgment above cannot be used as blanket authorization for marketing. Graph validation checks known node types, edges, conditions, reachable exit, supported actions and bounded work. A simulation must use a separate dispatcher that cannot send live messages.
J.4 Dispatch decision and race boundaries
A robust implementation has a durable action intent and an external-effect state machine. The following is algorithmic pseudocode, not a claim that holding a database lock can atomically control a remote provider.
claim due action using a lease; keep its stable operation ID
load current run, subject, channel and provider configuration
reject expired/stopped/unauthorized actions
re-evaluate current purpose, consent and suppression
recheck business state, template approval and recipient binding
reserve allowed rate/frequency/cost capacity transactionally
record dispatch decision and policy version durably
send via the approved adapter using supported idempotency
record accepted/rejected/uncertain result without exposing payloads
reconcile uncertain remote effects before an unsafe retry
release/settle reservations under the declared result policy
A withdrawal arriving after a provider has already accepted a message cannot retract history automatically. Stop future effects and use the provider's supported cancellation/removal where available. The system must identify the policy check time and in-flight boundary instead of promise impossible cross-system atomicity. Suppressed actions retain minimal reason evidence, not copied contact details.
J.5 Relational keys and action-state requirements
Illustrative PostgreSQL invariants, adapted to the implemented schema:
-- Examples: migrations must bind to actual scoped parent tables.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX workflow_enrollment_once
  ON workflow_runs
  (site_id, workflow_version_id, subject_id, trigger_event_id);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX workflow_action_once
  ON node_executions (site_id, run_id, node_id, iteration);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX provider_receipt_once
  ON provider_receipts (site_id, provider_account_id, provider_event_id);
CREATE INDEX due_workflow_timers
  ON workflow_timers (due_at)
  WHERE status = 'waiting';
A nullable provider_event_id needs a declared strategy; an internal operation ID may be necessary when the provider supplies none. Uniqueness alone does not establish authorization, truthful provenance or end-to-end exactly-once delivery. Add foreign keys, expected-version transitions, restricted roles and retention rules. Never grant a workflow worker unrestricted DELETE/TRUNCATE over protected blog source.
J.6 Segment definition and safe evaluation
{
  "segment_id": "seg_consultation_pending",
  "version": 2,
  "subject_type": "lead",
  "purpose": "approved_service_followup",
  "include": {
    "all": [
      {"field": "interest", "op": "eq", "value": "consultation"},
      {"field": "stage", "op": "in", "value": ["new", "assigned"]}
    ]
  },
  "exclude": {"any": [
    {"field": "suppressed", "op": "eq", "value": true},
    {"field": "test_record", "op": "eq", "value": true}
  ]},
  "destinations": ["internal_staff_queue"]
}
The service compiles only allowed fields/operators into parameterized queries. It applies tenant/role/purpose restrictions and query limits independently of this structure. Advertising export requires its own approved destination and permission; internal follow-up eligibility does not imply Customer Match eligibility. The preview shows count, as_of, excluded counts and unknown coverage without disclosing unauthorized contacts.
J.7 Safe context and field-destination matrix
This matrix strengthens the inherited context contracts. A field being available in the backend is not permission to copy it to every consumer. Field provenance, expiry, scope and access/reveal audit accompany sensitive service records.
J.8 Feed and report contracts
Feed item snapshots reference source product/variant, source revision, destination/account, safe URL, current price/currency, availability, validated attributes, generated_at and destination status. Each feed run records its exact manifest and mode: incremental patch versus full replacement. Unsupported or absent attributes remain invalid/unknown, not invented values. Item deletion requires explicit source/eligibility evidence.
A metric response includes metric_id, definition_version, grain, value, numerator/denominator where relevant, period, timezone, currency, attribution_rule, coverage, freshness, source_runs and warnings. A report may show platform-attributed purchases alongside internal paid orders, but cannot add them as separate sales. User-level drilldown is separately permissioned.
J.9 Configuration invariants
BLOG_AUTO_DELETE_ENABLED = false        # enforced invariant
PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_INITIAL_STATE = denied # baseline until eligible
AD_DESTINATION_DEFAULT = denied
PRECISE_LOCATION_MODE = explicit_task
CHAT_CONTEXT_PROJECTION = task_limited
AUDIT_CRITICAL_MUTATIONS = required
CAMPAIGN_WRITE_MODE = disabled_until_approved
AUTOMATION_SIMULATION_EXTERNAL_SENDS = forbidden
PRODUCTION_DEMO_SEED = forbidden
Actual environment names may differ. The production configuration validator must reject unsafe combinations, missing secrets, placeholder accounts and incompatible provider versions. Do not expose server-only fields with frontend-public environment prefixes. Separate capability existence from enabled data collection and from provider spending authority.
Appendix K | Operating templates and reusable decision records
K.1 Project-specific completion sheet
K.2 Campaign launch record
Record campaign_id, offer/version, customer intent, channel/account, objective, primary and secondary goals, audience/region/category, exclusions, approved budget, currency/timezone, schedule, creative IDs/rights, destination revisions, UTM/click-ID rules, provider settings, consent eligibility, human response SLA, measurement test and rollback/stop owner.
Approval evidence includes truthful claims, working mobile/in-app destination, correct price/terms, accessible form or native-lead handling, synthetic lead/CRM outcome, eligible conversion diagnostics, reporting access, budget authority and policy review. Final status must distinguish draft, approved, submitted to provider, remote active, rejected, paused and uncertain. Never launch from an unresolved macro or placeholder URL.
K.3 Editorial and collection brief
Record page/collection ID, purpose, reader task, target intent, locale, owner/reviewer, unique contribution, source evidence, rights, approved template/schema, route/canonical/index policy, related pages, primary CTA, event hooks, media accessibility and next review trigger. Identify changes affecting active campaigns, offers, chatbot knowledge, translations and downloads.
Completion requires saved/recoverable draft, factual/rights review, preview, link/SEO/accessibility checks and an approved publication manifest. Review_due_at creates a task, not a deletion timer. Collection-schema changes include migration impact and historical revision compatibility.
K.4 Data and processor decision record
For each field: name/class, source, task/purpose, required/optional, lawful-processing assessment, notice version, permission trigger, retention, access roles, export/reveal rule, destinations, geographic processing, deletion/hold behavior and test. For each provider: service/account, data categories, contract, subprocessor/region/training settings, supported deletion/export, security review, cost and exit plan.
Do not place actual customer records or credentials in this decision template. Use references to restricted evidence. The legal reviewer signs applicability and exceptions; engineering signs implemented controls. A legal decision still requires technical proof that tags/queues/widgets behave as described.
K.5 Sender and DNS evidence record
Sender: From/Reply-To/return path, message purpose, provider/account, estimated volume, SPF record reference, DKIM selector/rotation, DMARC policy/alignment, received-header evidence, complaint/bounce handling, visible and one-click unsubscribe tests, monitored replies and suppression/export process.
Domain/DNS: registrar owner, billing/renewal, recovery outside the affected domain, delegated admins, record inventory, DNSSEC/DS where enabled, TLS/CAA/challenge path, expiry monitoring, change approval, zone export, cutover checks and rollback. Values containing credentials remain in the secret manager, not this document.
K.6 Workflow, scoring and audience approval
Workflow: trigger authority, subject/re-entry, graph/version, purpose, conditions, timers/timezone, allowed actions, caps, exit/suppression rules, simulation fixtures, uncertainty handling, pause/stop and operator owner. Scoring: allowed inputs, weights/caps/decay, unknowns, explanations, overrides, version/recalculation and qualification separation. Audience: purpose, provider eligibility, source segment, included/excluded fields, membership window, removal priority, provider evidence and retirement.
Approval requires synthetic simulation and policy-negative cases. Any new sensitive input or destination reopens review. A score is a derived recommendation; a provider-matched audience is not a verified identity list or a guarantee of targeting reach.
K.7 Change, incident and exception templates
Change request: ID, reason, requirements affected, before/after configuration, UI/API/schema/provider impact, personal-data impact, SEO/ads/financial effects, tests, migration, rollback, approval and post-release check. Include content and tag changes, not only code.
Incident record: detection, severity, affected journeys/data, timeline, owner/backup, containment, customer/provider/legal communications, evidence, recovery, residual exposure, root cause, corrective actions and regression tests. Preserve sensitive evidence with restricted access. Do not copy credentials or customer messages into public incident notes.
Exception record: requirement, reason, risk, compensating controls, owner, approval, expiry/review and monitoring. Exceptions do not authorize unlawful collection or unsupported provider behavior. An unavailable provider test remains Blocked, not an exception that magically makes it Passed.
K.8 Release packet and owner handover
Release: code/build, schema/migrations, content/offer/release manifest, policy/tag/workflow versions, provider accounts/API versions, activation matrix, tests/evidence, known defects, approved risks, restore report, access review, cost model, training and support. Retain evidence for safe rollback and investigation.
Owner sign-off: independently create/edit/publish/recover content; inspect campaign attribution; simulate and stop a workflow; receive and route a form/chat lead; review delivery/audience/feed diagnostics where active; manually trash/restore a blog; identify deletion actor; export permitted data; recover accounts; reach support. A second technical operator installs and restores the platform from documented artifacts.
Appendix L | Verification, policy refresh and expanded glossary
L.1 Evidence classes used in this edition
Inherited baseline: R01–R61 are retained from the uploaded v1.1 standard. The original verification caveats remain. Selected high-impact topics were rechecked during the v1.2 expansion; this is not a claim that every historical paragraph or legal provision was revalidated worldwide.
Consulted primary guidance: the added reference register records official documentation used during preparation dated 7 October 2026. It supports external facts and standards. The lifecycle designs, permission defaults, proposed targets, schema examples and tests are this document's engineering specifications.
Account-level verification required: live account eligibility, permissions, supported advertising objectives/fields/limits, external campaign writes, provider diagnostics and specific integration behavior require authorized account access and tests. The preparation of a document did not access or modify the user's actual ad/CRM/payment accounts.
L.2 High-impact verification register
L.3 Provider review template
Record provider/topic; authoritative source URL; reviewed_at; reviewer; account/region; API/runtime/version; affected features; exact settings/limits; credentials/scopes by reference; data restrictions; deprecation dates; regression tests; next review date/trigger; unresolved issue; owner and status. A blog post or an old tutorial must not override a current official specification. When official pages disagree or are account-gated, record uncertainty and verify before activation.
Review after provider notices, major releases, account restriction, changed data purposes, new regions/audiences, new campaign types or incidents. A scheduled quarterly review does not replace urgent handling of deprecations or security advisories. Use a human-approved change to update this standard and project contracts; never silently change the expected behavior of a running workflow or experiment.
L.4 Additional glossary
L.5 Final no-silent-gap rule
No applicable requirement may disappear between design, implementation, deployment and handover. Each must be implemented and tested, explicitly blocked, or deliberately excluded through an approved scope decision. The standard preserves owner autonomy and portability while keeping security, privacy, content preservation and truthful measurement non-negotiable. Future unknown requirements are handled through this recorded extension process rather than a false promise that one static edition can predict everything.
Appendix M | Source merge, changes and evidence of preservation
M.1 Merge method and controlling source
The source of the consolidated body is the uploaded Website_Master_Blueprint_v1_2_Comprehensive(1).docx. The v1.1 and v1.0 uploads were compared against it from the first actual chapter heading through the source register, including paragraph text inside tables. Covers, headers, footers and contents-page repetitions were excluded from that comparison. Whitespace was normalized; exact matching is a text-preservation check, not a semantic certification or an executed software test.
The reviewed differences concern version/research notes, two expanded headings, capability-activation descriptions and strengthened acceptance/evidence language. No substantive older feature was identified as lost through those differences. The final edition uses the v1.2 wording where it expands the earlier requirement; it does not repeat the full older document as a conflicting appendix.
M.2 Source identity
Hashes identify the supplied versions; they do not certify their accuracy. The output is a new document and does not overwrite the originals. The source chapters, requirements, worked examples and R01–R106 reference register remain the inherited basis. New R107–R128 entries support the final expansion or record an explicit retrieval limitation.
M.3 Reviewed historical differences
M.4 Final clarification register
M.5 Research and implementation boundaries
The new engineering workflows, defaults, schemas and test scenarios are proposed requirements for implementation. They are not statements that a website, customer account, legal jurisdiction or external provider was tested. Official public documentation was consulted for the final high-impact search, Google Ads, SDK, browser and standard changes. The Meta SDK source is an implementation reference from a mutable branch; actual projects must pin and test the chosen released version. Public Meta auction help was visible through search material, while full account-specific documentation remained restricted; record authenticated verification before activation.
The inherited references were preserved, not all revalidated line by line worldwide. Qualified legal review and actual account-level diagnostics remain project work. These are clearly assigned evidence tasks, not hidden omissions or permission to fabricate a completed integration.

The following is illustrative internal configuration, not a provider API request. Replace example IDs through approved site setup. It intentionally includes no real contact identifiers, secrets, precise location or live spending authorization.
{

O.2 Canonical internal event and representations
{
  "schema_version": "business-event/1",
  "business_event_id": "evt_demo_001",
  "event_type": "lead_accepted",
  "site_id": "site_demo",
  "record_ref": "lead_demo_001",
  "lifecycle_version": 1,
  "occurred_at": "2026-10-07T10:00:00Z",
  "source_channel": "website_chat",
  "context_refs": {
    "acquisition": "touch_demo_01",
    "experiment_assignment": "assignment_demo_01"
  },
  "consent_ref": "choice_demo_01",
  "privacy_class": "restricted_business",
  "destination_eligibility": "evaluate_at_dispatch"
}
This event is not sent unchanged to GA4 or Meta. The consent reference is evidence, not an eternal permission token. Context references are scoped database links, not public credentials. The marketing event is constructed from an approved minimal projection; the transcript and contact fields stay in protected business records.
For Meta, a permitted same-action browser/server representation reuses the agreed event name and stable event ID, with server event_time in seconds. For Google, deduplication follows the selected action/product contract, not the assumption that an internal UUID is universally sufficient. A later qualification has a new lifecycle event ID, not a rewritten original lead timestamp. A correction preserves the original fact plus an authorized adjustment.
O.3 Post-serialization dispatch policy
1. Load the trusted business event and destination contract version.
2. Resolve current site/account, purpose, consent, suppression and expiry.
3. Return suppressed when delivery is not eligible.
4. Build only the destination's approved field projection.
5. Normalize using destination-specific tested fixtures.
6. Serialize with a pinned SDK; disable automatic request enrichment.
7. Reject any unexpected key or prohibited sensitive value.
8. Recheck revocation/version changes at the dispatch boundary.
9. Send with original identity/time and record a safe receipt.
10. Reconcile uncertain or partial delivery without inventing success.
The result of step 7 is a protected validation record containing contract/SDK versions and safe error codes, not a copy of the customer payload. A failed post-serialization check must not be bypassed through a generic retry button. The public form or chat already has its durable accepted result regardless of optional destination failure.
O.4 Internal route policy example
These are example families, not a mandate to use identical slugs. Verify HTTP and rendered output through the actual edge. A robots rule does not substitute for account or resource authorization.
O.5 Scope and provenance vocabulary
Do not replace unknown with direct, not collected with zero interest, or approximate location with precise coordinates. A log entry should explain the action and provenance without spreading raw protected details.
O.6 Example business-outcome scorecard
For each period/cohort, show spend and currency; eligible measured visits; accepted unique enquiries under the declared unit; qualified enquiries; customer outcomes; refunds/cancellations; net realized value; contribution assumptions; acquisition cost; data freshness; source coverage and attribution method. Separate channel-attributed reports from the authoritative business total. Include a visible interpretation note and accountable owner for every metric.
