P.1 Required project decisions
P.2 Requirement evidence schema
requirement_id; controlling_section; capability_id; source_version;
applicability; rationale; specification_status; implementation_status;
activation_status; verification_status; accountable_owner;
public_workflow; owner_workflow; api_or_service; tables_and_constraints;
permission_and_data_policy; failure_behavior; automated_test_reference;
manual_provider_evidence; build_config_versions; residual_risk;
approver; decision_date; next_review_trigger
Allowed verification outcomes are Passed, Failed, Blocked, Not executed and Not Applicable with rationale. Implementation status separately records Not implemented, In progress and Implemented. Do not mark a critical requirement passed because it is merely described in this document.
P.3 Evidence packet contents
Include source/build identifiers; installed runtime/SDK versions; migrations; content and offer manifest; sitemap/route crawl; privacy/consent map; effective campaign goals; final serialized event fixtures; real integration diagnostics; deduplication/correction evidence; owner task results; accessibility/performance/load/security results; backup/restore and deletion-ledger reconciliation; domain/account recovery; cost controls; known issues; approvals and support contacts.
Store sensitive evidence in a restricted repository or document store. An evidence link must be readable by the authorized reviewer, not publicly accessible by accident. Screenshots should mask personal data and secrets. Ensure the actual test date, environment and configuration are visible so a stale staging pass cannot stand in for current production proof.
P.4 Final release decision
The accountable business owner, technical lead, marketing lead and privacy/security reviewer approve their respective areas. Record the exact edition and project scope, release version, critical gate results, accepted noncritical exceptions, expiry/review dates and production activation plan. A refusal or legal restriction on optional tracking may be a correct operating state; an unresolved mandatory privacy, security, lead-integrity or recovery failure is not.
Final baseline edition: 1.3. Project/site: __________. Build/configuration: __________. Scope manifest: __________. Evidence location: __________. Business approval: __________. Technical approval: __________. Marketing approval: __________. Privacy/security review: __________. Date: __________. Activation decision: __________.
P.5 What has and has not been verified by this deliverable
The deliverable consolidates the supplied documents, compares source text, records deliberate clarifications and supplies expanded implementation/testing requirements. The file's structure, reference navigation and rendered layout are checked during preparation. No live website, ad account, CRM, legal position, payment integration or marketing campaign is certified by preparing this document. Their verification is the explicit project release work above.
Use this single merged edition as the reference. Preserve the approved project snapshot and append dated decisions when the business or an external platform changes. This is how the website remains complete against its actual obligations without pretending that a static file can guarantee every future requirement or auction result.
Sources | Primary guidance and verification notes
Final edition research note: R01–R106 are inherited source references. R107–R128 are the final expansion references, reviewed on 7 October 2026 unless explicitly labeled limited retrieval. Appendix M distinguishes document comparison, newly consulted official guidance, proposed engineering requirements and account-level evidence still to be obtained. A link or SDK symbol is not proof of live account eligibility.
How to use the references
Version 1.2 research note: the supplied v1.1 Chapters 01–41, Appendices A–G and references R01–R61 are retained with targeted integration notes. R62–R106 document the new primary research and explicit verification links for preparation dated 7 October 2026. Selected high-impact inherited topics were rechecked. This is not a claim that every historical statement, legal jurisdiction or private account was fully revalidated. Appendix L records the boundaries.
References support provider facts and external standards. Lifecycle designs, permission defaults, proposed targets, schema examples and acceptance tests are engineering specifications, not quotations or executed results. Baseline preparation was dated 6 October 2026; the v1.2 expansion is dated 7 October 2026. URLs and API support may change; validate the actual account before activation.
Google and other publicly accessible primary documentation were consulted for the cited topics. Meta overview material was available, but several detailed developer/API pages returned rate-limit errors. References R19 and R46 are implementation verification links, not claims that their full current specifications were successfully retrieved. Confirm them through authenticated official access and record the API versions and tests used.
The India Gazette reference supports the existence and phased commencement of the 2025 rules; it is not a complete legal compliance assessment. Current notifications, amendments, sector requirements, and the project's applicability still need qualified review. Retention examples, budgets, schedules, and recovery targets in this document are explicitly proposed project decisions.

Requested ambition | Contractual interpretation
Top-ranking website | Build technical eligibility, helpful content, excellent landing pages, trustworthy offers, and measurement. Neither organic position nor ad placement is guaranteed.
Track everything | Instrument an agreed catalogue of meaningful interactions and authoritative outcomes, subject to consent, browser limits, platform rules, and data minimization.
Fully dynamic frontend | Pages, approved layouts, content, navigation, forms, themes, SEO settings, and configured business rules are editable through the owner console.
No developer dependency | Routine business operation must not require a developer. New component types, integrations, security updates, and infrastructure changes still require qualified maintenance.
No dependencies | Own accounts, source code, content, exports, deployment instructions, and migration paths. Minimize replaceable dependencies; do not claim software or external services can have none.
All features | Deliver the core catalogue, then select and test applicable modules. Unsupported features must be marked absent, not represented by decorative buttons.
Decision | Required project answer
Business and audience | Legal entity, offer, target customer, audience age range, operating countries, languages, accessibility needs, and competitors.
Primary outcome | One principal business outcome: qualified lead, paid order, confirmed booking, application, or another explicitly defined result.
Economics | Typical order value, gross margin assumptions, qualified-lead definition, sales cycle, acceptable acquisition cost, and refund or cancellation rules.
Content ownership | Named owner for every important page, subject-matter reviewer, publishing permissions, image rights, and review frequency.
Existing assets | Domain, DNS, branding, URLs, analytics, ad accounts, CRM, customer data, source repository, hosting, and migration obligations.
Capacity and resilience | Expected monthly visits, peak concurrent activity, form volume, media volume, staff users, uptime target, RPO, RTO, and support availability.
Data and integrations | Exact fields collected, sensitivity, destinations, lawful processing assessment, retention, CRM, payment, communication, and legacy interfaces.
Delivery constraints | Budget, decision makers, launch dependencies, staff skills, cloud restrictions, region requirements, and training needs.
Tier | Scope | Activation rule
Core: every production website | Public pages, responsive design, CMS, blogs or resource capability, forms, SEO controls, consent controls, measurement, security, backups, monitoring, owner training. | Mandatory baseline; unused content collections can remain hidden.
Growth: selected when useful | CRM automation, advanced landing pages, qualified-lead feedback, experiments, multilingual publishing, search, reporting exports, approved replay. | Clear business case, owner, privacy review, and ongoing budget.
Business modules: conditional | Commerce, bookings, subscriptions, customer portal, courses, inventory, jobs, partner directory, community, AI assistance. | Separate workflow specification, data model, access model, tests, and operating procedures.
Layer | Default | Responsibility and boundary
Public frontend | Next.js, React, TypeScript, semantic HTML, Tailwind/CSS | Render pages, progressive enhancement, accessible interactions, metadata, caching, and consent-gated events.
Owner console | React/TypeScript, shared design system | Content editing, preview, approvals, forms, media, SEO, business settings, roles, and operational dashboards.
Backend | NestJS modular monolith | Authentication, authorization, validation, business transactions, CMS workflows, integrations, and API contracts.
Primary database | PostgreSQL with migrations; Prisma as default ORM | Durable relational data, versioned JSONB content, business integrity, audit references, and transactional outbox.
Media | S3-compatible object storage and CDN | Images, downloads, private uploads, transformations, lifecycle rules, and signed delivery.
Cache and jobs | Optional Redis; database-backed outbox mandatory for external effects | Derived caches and worker coordination. Durable business facts stay in PostgreSQL.
Measurement | First-party event contract, CMP, GTM/GA4, Ads/Meta adapters | Purpose-controlled event collection, reporting, and conversion feedback.
Operations | Native processes or Docker; Terraform; optional Kubernetes | Reproducible environments, deployments, secrets, backups, monitoring, and recovery.
Family | Baseline content and behavior
Home | Clear offer, audience fit, primary action, services/products overview, truthful proof, process, helpful questions, final action.
Service/product/program | Outcome, suitability, inclusions, constraints, delivery process, pricing or clear enquiry path, evidence, related resources, and next step.
Pricing | Current packages, currency, tax/fee clarity, exclusions, billing terms, comparison, date applicability, and action. Server validates payable prices.
About and trust | Accurate organization identity, real team or accountable contacts, experience evidence, policies, and customer-support routes.
Blog/resources | Searchable or browsable index, categories, author/reviewer information, useful article pages, related content, and resource downloads.
Contact and conversion | Accessible enquiry form, permitted contact channels, honest response expectations, confirmation, and fallback contact method.
Utility and legal | Search results, 404/500/offline states, privacy/cookie notices, consent preferences, terms, accessibility information, and module-specific policies.
Campaign landing | Message-matched offer, focused action, concise navigation where appropriate, fast page, truthful proof, and consistent legal access.
Area | Required implementation test
Structure | Meaningful landmarks, logical headings, descriptive page titles, skip link, and correct semantic controls.
Input | Persistent labels, appropriate autocomplete, clear instructions, programmatic errors, error summary, and no color-only feedback.
Keyboard and focus | All actions reachable; visible focus; dialogs contain and restore focus; sticky bars do not conceal focused controls.
Reading and display | Readable contrast, zoom/reflow support, no essential horizontal page scrolling on narrow layouts, and usable text spacing.
Media and motion | Useful alternative text, captions/transcripts where needed, no essential autoplay, reduced-motion support, and decorative media hidden appropriately.
Interaction | Adequate touch targets, alternatives to dragging, accessible authentication, and no unnecessary repeated entry of known information.
Route type | Rendering and caching rule
Public evergreen page or article | Pre-render or cache server output; refresh through validated publication events and a bounded fallback freshness policy.
Time-sensitive public information | Server-render or use short controlled caching; label relevant dates and define acceptable staleness.
Pricing and product display | Cache presentation only within approved limits; revalidate transaction price and availability on the server.
Authenticated portal or admin | Per-user authorization; no shared cache of private data; prevent indexing and sensitive response caching.
Preview | Signed, short-lived, authorized draft access; no shared public cache; no production marketing tags.
Search and filters | Stable routes and accessible pagination; deliberate indexing policy; separate performance and query-cost limits.
Measure | Starting acceptance target | Method
Core Web Vitals | LCP ≤ 2.5 seconds, INP ≤ 200 milliseconds, CLS ≤ 0.1 at the 75th percentile. | Field data by device class and major route; lab checks before sufficient field traffic exists.
Initial transferred JavaScript | Aim for ≤ 200 KB compressed on core marketing routes; approved exceptions documented. | Build report including framework and first-party code; measure third-party cost separately.
Initial mobile page transfer | Aim for ≤ 1 MB for the principal marketing experience, excluding user-requested video/downloads. | Repeatable mobile network test with cache state recorded.
Lead API latency | Proposed p95 ≤ 800 milliseconds at approved peak load, excluding deferred third-party delivery. | Load test and production APM; never bypass validation to meet target.
Publish visibility | Proposed p95 ≤ 60 seconds after successful publication for affected public routes. | Synthetic publish/read test across deployed instances and CDN.
Reliability | No lost acknowledged lead in tested crash/retry scenarios. | Transaction, outbox, restart, and reconciliation tests.
Console area | Required capabilities
Overview | Business outcomes, recent leads, content status, publication health, and action-required alerts.
Pages and navigation | Create, duplicate, arrange approved sections, edit routes, manage menus/header/footer, preview, schedule, and roll back.
Blog and resources | Articles, categories, authors, reviewers, related content, downloads, redirects, and editorial calendar.
Media | Upload, organize, replace safely, crop/focal point, alt text, rights, usage references, and protected assets.
Forms and leads | Form builder, validations, consent copy, submissions, routing, statuses, notes, exports, and deletion requests.
SEO and campaigns | Metadata, index policy, sitemap status, canonical guidance, redirects, campaign pages, and approved UTM builder.
Business modules | Enabled services, prices, bookings, orders, subscriptions, or portal settings under role-specific permissions.
Settings and governance | Branding, domain status, locales, integrations, consent configuration, users, permissions, audit, backups/export status.
Experiments and attribution | Variant builder, analysis/guardrails, campaign links, acquisition diagnostics and promote/rollback.
Visitor context and conversations | Authorized context, explicit capture, service-area workflow, inbox/handoff, protected data reveal and audit.
Content preservation and Audit Center | No-expiry trash, restore, independent purge approvals, actor history, integrity status and governed exports.
Owner can do without code deployment | Requires specialist change control
Add or edit pages, articles, approved blocks, images, menus, pricing content, and downloadable resources. | Introduce new block behavior, modify data schemas incompatibly, add executable scripts, or change security controls.
Build forms from approved fields, validation rules, consent text, and routing options. | Add new sensitive data categories, payment handling, custom integrations, or unsupported data destinations.
Duplicate campaign templates, schedule publication, edit SEO, create approved redirects, and review results. | Change framework versions, infrastructure, database engines, authentication design, or custom bidding integrations.
Manage approved roles and operational settings within permission limits. | Remove audit evidence, bypass approval, expose secrets, or grant unrestricted platform access.
Block family | Owner-editable fields | Built-in measurement hooks
Hero and campaign intro | Heading, description, image, alignment, primary/secondary actions. | cta_click with stable action_id.
Service/program/product cards | References, summaries, images, labels, ordering. | item_select with entity_id.
Benefits and process | Steps, icons, proof links, layout variant. | Optional meaningful action events, not every decorative view.
Pricing and comparison | Package references, display notes, currency presentation, included features. | pricing_view, plan_select; transaction prices remain server-owned.
Proof and case study | Authorized quote, source record, role, image, case-study links. | proof_open or case_study_select where useful.
FAQ and accordion | Questions, reviewed answers, related links. | faq_open with question_id, not answer text.
Media and gallery | Images, captions, video reference, transcript, focal point. | video_start/progress only when permitted and implemented.
Resource/download | Asset reference, description, access rule, form reference. | download_click, download_authorized when server-confirmed.
Form and booking action | Approved form definition, intro, success content, routing reference. | form_start, validation_error, authoritative conversion confirmation.
Article/content | Safe rich text, headings, tables, references, related items. | content_view, meaningful navigation actions.
Navigation and footer | Menu references, contact links, policies, social links. | navigation_click, contact_click with controlled identifiers.
Utility layouts | Search, location finder, announcement, CTA band, newsletter, approved embed. | Module-specific allowlisted events.
Area | Required behavior
HTTP status | Valid pages return the correct success status; removed pages use 404/410 as appropriate; permanent moves use tested permanent redirects. No universal 200 fallback.
Metadata | Unique useful titles and descriptions; consistent canonical URL; correct locale; useful social metadata and share images.
Index controls | Explicit policy for each route family. Private/admin pages require authorization; robots.txt is not a security boundary.
XML sitemaps | Include intended canonical indexable URLs with truthful modification dates; update on publication and removal.
Links and navigation | Crawlable links, descriptive labels, breadcrumbs where useful, no orphan important pages, and accessible pagination.
Duplicates | Consistent canonical signals; redirect obsolete equivalents; deliberate handling of filters, sorting, UTMs, and campaign variants.
Structured data | Supported, relevant types only; generated from the same records as visible content; no invented ratings or invisible claims.
Monitoring | Search Console ownership, sitemap monitoring, index investigation, broken links, redirect loops, and template regression checks.
Asset | Implementation and maintenance
Topic and intent map | Associate a specific audience need with each important page; identify overlap and missing journeys.
Editorial calendar | Named writer/reviewer, evidence needed, production status, publication target, and update trigger.
Internal link map | Connect supporting articles with relevant services and decision resources; review orphan and broken links.
Evidence library | Approved case studies, product facts, research sources, customer permissions, and claim expiry dates.
Search performance view | Queries, impressions, clicks, landing pages, device/region, index coverage, and downstream business outcomes.
Ethical distribution | Relevant partnerships, useful resources, newsletters, community participation, and genuine earned references.
Requirement | Owner-controlled implementation
Accounts | Business owns Ads, Analytics, Tag Manager, Search Console, billing, recovery methods, and administrator access. Agencies receive delegated access.
Landing pages | Working HTTPS destinations, matching domain/message, usable navigation and contact information, accurate pricing/claims, and no cloaking.
Campaign identifiers | Controlled UTM builder; preserve approved Google click identifiers where consent and configuration permit. Do not fabricate missing identifiers.
Conversion catalogue | Named actions, business definitions, primary/secondary status, counting rule, value policy, currency, and authoritative source.
Validation | Tag diagnostics, consent tests, backend reconciliation, conversion deduplication, real test lead, and sales-team confirmation.
Policy review | Industry, location, audience-age, advertiser verification, claims, and destination requirements checked in the actual account.
Internal business event | Intended Meta mapping | Required control
Public page view | PageView, where enabled and permitted | Exactly one configured browser page-view owner per navigation.
Meaningful offering view | ViewContent where suitable | Map approved entity/content IDs; do not send sensitive page context.
Confirmed lead | Lead or the currently appropriate configured event | Browser and server representations of the same action share the agreed deduplication identity.
Verified registration | CompleteRegistration where appropriate | Emit after account/registration success, not on a button click.
Completed paid order | Purchase | Trusted order total/currency and stable order/event identifiers; never trust browser price.
Qualified lead / later sale | Supported standard or custom event chosen in account design | Verify current optimization support and privacy restrictions before implementation.
Event | Trigger and authority | Scope / critical parameters
page_view | Completed public route navigation; one frontend owner. | Once per page_view_id; route_id, page_id, content_revision, locale.
navigation_click | User activates a meaningful menu/breadcrumb link. | action_id, destination_route; no arbitrary link text.
cta_click | Approved call-to-action activated. | action_id, block_id, placement, entity_id when relevant.
section_view | Defined visible section meets its agreed threshold. | section_id, visibility_rule; once per page view unless justified.
pricing_view | Pricing heading/first-card marker visible ≥ 50% for 1 continuous second while the document is visible. | pricing_id, page_view_id; reset timer when visibility is interrupted.
scroll_depth | Optional coarse progress milestones, not continuous telemetry. | Approved milestones such as 25/50/75/90; once each per page view.
plan_select | Visitor chooses an offered package. | plan_id, context; not a payment or lead by itself.
faq_open | Visitor expands a tracked question. | question_id, block_id; never the answer or user text.
download_click | Visitor activates a resource link. | asset_id, version, file_type; label as intent.
download_authorized | Server grants access to a protected resource. | asset_id, access_method; does not prove full download or reading.
form_start | First meaningful interaction with an approved form. | form_id, form_revision; no field values.
form_step_complete | Multi-step form passes a validated step. | step_id; never input content.
form_validation_error | Client/server rejects approved validation rule. | field_key and error_code from allowlists, not entered text.
form_submit_attempt | User sends a submission attempt. | form_id, attempt_id; secondary diagnostic only.
generate_lead | API validates and durably commits a genuine accepted lead. | lead event_id, form_id; server authority.
lead_qualified | Authorized CRM rule or staff action marks qualification. | lead_id internally, reason_code, qualification_rule_version.
lead_disqualified | Business classifies spam, invalid, or unsuitable lead. | Controlled reason_code; exclude confidential staff notes.
booking_confirmed | Booking committed with capacity/time checks satisfied. | booking_id internally, service_id, actual confirmation time.
sign_up | Required registration state successfully completed. | Opaque account reference internally; policy-controlled destination mapping.
purchase | Verified payment/business completion rule satisfied. | order_id, amount, currency; server authority.
refund | Verified full or partial refund recorded. | refund_id, order_id, amount, currency; distinct lifecycle event.
site_search | Approved search interaction with sanitized query policy. | Category/result_count by default; raw queries off unless reviewed.
contact_click | Click-to-call, email, chat, or WhatsApp action. | channel and action_id; not proof of a completed conversation.
video_progress | Approved player reports defined milestones. | video_id and milestone; only for supported players and consent.
experiment_exposure | A visitor actually receives a rendered variant. | experiment_id, variant_id, allocation_version.
consent_change | User saves or changes preferences. | Restricted consent ledger; not an excuse to load rejected marketing tags.
Time | Visitor action | Reliable evidence and limitation
10:00:00 | Opens a campaign page. | page_view, sanitized route, eligible campaign touch, pseudonymous session; not automatically a named person.
10:00:12 | Clicks “Explore packages.” | cta_click with stable action_id and block_id.
10:00:19 | Views the pricing marker for the defined dwell time. | pricing_view; indicates exposure to that marker, not that every price was read.
10:00:45 | Clicks a brochure download. | download_click; optional server authorization record if gated.
10:01:10 | Begins the enquiry form. | form_start, then approved field-level error codes if applicable; input values stay out of analytics.
10:01:35 | Submits valid details. | API commits lead and outbox; generate_lead event_id returned. Contact details are stored only in the protected lead record.
11:30:00 | Staff confirms suitability. | lead_qualified from CRM/staff action with auditable rule/reason.
Next day | Customer pays or confirms service. | Verified purchase/booking event under the actual business workflow; eligible ad feedback queued.
State | Meaning | Required action
pending | Durable outbox entry created. | Worker claims work using a lease/lock and bounded batch size.
eligible | Purpose, consent, destination policy, and schema checks pass. | Transform the minimum fields for that destination.
in_flight | Delivery attempt underway. | Record attempt ID, original event ID/time, and timeout.
accepted | Provider acknowledged the request/event at its documented level. | Store response metadata; do not label automatically attributed.
retry_wait | Recoverable error or uncertain transport result. | Retry with backoff/jitter and the same event ID.
rejected | Permanent schema, permission, or policy error. | Alert responsible owner; do not retry indefinitely.
suppressed | Consent, policy, expiry, or business rule prevents dispatch. | Record non-sensitive reason and apply retention policy.
dead_letter | Retry policy exhausted. | Provide restricted review/replay tools after root cause correction.
Requirement | Baseline behavior
Initial state | Nonessential analytics and advertising disabled until the applicable permission rule is satisfied. Essential business functionality remains available.
Choice interface | Clear purposes, understandable accept/reject/manage options, no preselected optional permissions, accessible interaction.
Persistence | Versioned choice record with timestamp, policy version, region/rule basis where used, and configured expiry/re-prompt behavior.
Withdrawal | Preferences always accessible; stop future nonessential collection and eligible queued delivery; process deletion/opt-out obligations separately.
Destinations | Consent state mapped to each destination's supported controls; no blanket assumption that one toggle covers all processing.
Evidence | Restricted consent ledger supports audit without leaking choices or identifiers into unrelated tools.
Data class | Proposed starting policy | Controls
Raw behavioral events | 30–90 days for troubleshooting/funnel analysis, then delete or irreversibly aggregate. | Separate store/access; no unnecessary PII; automated partition/lifecycle jobs.
Aggregated business reporting | 12–24 months where useful and justified. | Avoid re-identification from tiny groups; document aggregation method.
Optional session replay | Disabled by default; short approved period such as 14–30 days when enabled. | Sampling, consent, strict masking, exclusions, and access audit.
Enquiries and CRM records | Business/jurisdiction-specific schedule based on sales process and obligations. | Purpose review, inactivity rules, suppression/deletion, and hold handling.
Consent and audit evidence | Legal/security owner defines justified duration and contents. | Tamper-resistant restricted records; minimize copied personal details.
Orders, invoices, tax records | Applicable financial/legal schedule; not a universal website default. | Restricted exports, retention holds, reconciled deletion exceptions.
Backups | Approved rolling schedule with documented expiry and restore-time deletion reconciliation. | Encryption, restricted access, recovery tests, and deletion reapplication.
Record | Minimum fields and rule
Acquisition touch | touch_id, eligible session reference, captured_at, sanitized landing route, referrer category, allowed campaign fields.
Click identifier | Type, value, capture source/time, consent/purpose eligibility, expiry, destination restrictions; encrypted/restricted as appropriate.
Lead attribution | lead_id, first_eligible_touch_id, last_eligible_touch_id, declared source if supplied, attribution_rule_version.
Identity link | Source identity, target identity, linkage method, evidence/permission reference, created_at, revocation/deletion state.
CRM outcome | lead/order reference, verified stage, occurred_at, responsible actor/system, value policy, correction history.
External result | Destination, event reference, accepted/matched/attributed status where available, report date, attribution settings.
Audience | Dashboard questions
Owner | How many valid leads/orders/bookings arrived? What needs action? Which sources produce useful outcomes? Are forms and publication healthy?
Marketing | Which campaigns and landing pages produce qualified leads and revenue? Where do pricing exposure, downloads, and form steps lose visitors?
Editorial | Which resources attract the right audience, support service journeys, need updating, or produce zero useful engagement?
Sales/service | Which leads are unassigned, overdue, contacted, qualified, won, lost, or awaiting response?
Operations/privacy | Are queues delayed, tags failing, data exports pending, consent rules working, backups current, and alerts acknowledged?
Step | Required behavior
Validate | Check schema/version, required fields, allowed choices, length, file status, rate limits, and relevant anti-abuse controls.
Check idempotency | Same key and same request returns original result; different request with same key is rejected.
Commit | Store protected submission, lead, consent evidence references, eligible attribution references, audit, and outbox in one transaction.
Respond | Return safe confirmation, lead receipt reference, and conversion event_id after commit; never echo unnecessary PII.
Deliver asynchronously | Assign/rout lead, notify staff, send approved acknowledgment, synchronize CRM, and dispatch eligible conversion events.
Reconcile | Alert on unassigned leads, delayed notifications, failed CRM delivery, or missing outcomes.
Module | Owned responsibility
Identity and access | Staff/customer authentication, sessions, roles, permissions, recovery, MFA, and authorization policy.
Sites and configuration | Site identity, locales, domain associations, approved feature flags, and public/private settings separation.
Content and publishing | Pages, blocks, articles, revisions, workflow, preview, publication, redirects, and cache invalidation requests.
Media | Asset metadata, upload authorization, quarantine, scanning workflow, derivatives, and access policy.
Forms and leads | Versioned forms, submissions, lead lifecycle, assignment, validation, consent references, and attribution links.
Measurement and privacy | Event schema validation, consent policy, eligible collection, delivery ledger, retention, and rights workflows.
Integrations and notifications | Provider adapters, credentials references, webhooks, synchronization, message templates, and suppression.
Business modules | Separate commerce, booking, subscriptions, portal, courses, or other enabled capabilities.
Operations | Audit, health, job control, exports, backup status, and administrative diagnostics.
Group | Entities | Important constraints
Site and access | sites, locales, users, memberships, roles, permissions, sessions | Unique validated domains; scoped memberships; revoked/expired sessions; no plaintext passwords.
Content | pages, page_revisions, block_definitions, shared_blocks, publication_jobs | Unique site/locale/slug; explicit live revision; immutable published snapshot; schema version.
Editorial | posts, post_revisions, authors, reviewers, categories, post_topics | Stable IDs, unique scoped slugs, reviewer references, real publication dates.
Navigation and SEO | menus, menu_items, redirects, seo_overrides | No redirect loops; permitted status codes; internal link references; canonical/index policy.
Media | assets, asset_derivatives, asset_usages, upload_jobs | Unique storage keys/checksums as appropriate; quarantine state; protected/public access class.
Forms | forms, form_revisions, submissions, submission_files | Each submission references the exact form revision; schema validated; idempotency scoped.
CRM | leads, lead_contacts, lead_stages, lead_activities, assignments | Protected PII; auditable transitions; multiple enquiries allowed; controlled merge rules.
Privacy | consent_records, policy_versions, privacy_requests, suppressions | Purpose/version evidence; restricted identities; withdrawal/deletion workflow.
Attribution | sessions, acquisition_touches, identity_links, lead_attribution | Only permitted identities; rule versions; no assumed cross-device identity.
Events | event_definitions, behavioral_events, outbox_events, destination_deliveries | Stable event IDs; schema validation; distinct trusted business events; deduplication keys.
Integration | integrations, external_mappings, webhook_receipts, sync_cursors | Secrets by reference; unique provider event IDs; scoped mappings; resumable cursors.
Operations | audit_entries, job_runs, export_jobs, retention_runs | Append-oriented audit; actor and reason; recorded outcomes; restricted exports.
Optional commerce | products, prices, orders, order_lines, payments, refunds, subscriptions | Exact-money representation; server pricing; immutable order snapshots; verified payment states.
Optional booking | resources, availability, holds, bookings, cancellations | Capacity concurrency protection; timezone metadata; unique provider references.
Optional portal | customer_accounts, entitlements, documents, access_grants | Object-level authorization; expiry/revocation; no public access by guessed IDs.
Endpoint | Role and contract
GET /v1/public/site | Public-safe site configuration only; no credentials or unpublished settings.
GET /v1/public/pages/by-path | Published page for validated site/locale/path; deterministic revision and cache policy.
GET /v1/public/posts | Published, paginated content with controlled filters.
GET /v1/public/forms/{id} | Public approved form schema/revision; excludes internal routing and secret rules.
POST /v1/forms/{id}/submissions | Validated lead creation with Idempotency-Key; public anti-abuse controls.
POST /v1/events | Allowlisted behavioral collection; cannot assert trusted business outcomes.
POST /v1/consent/choices | Record applicable preference evidence; protected against abuse; no unrelated tags triggered.
POST /v1/auth/login | Rate-limited staff/customer authentication with secure session handling.
POST /v1/auth/logout | Revoke applicable server session and expire client credential.
GET /v1/admin/pages | Authorized filtered content listing.
POST /v1/admin/pages | Create draft with scoped route uniqueness and audit.
PATCH /v1/admin/pages/{id}/draft | Optimistic concurrency using expected version or If-Match.
POST /v1/admin/pages/{id}/submit-review | Validate workflow transition and reviewer assignment.
POST /v1/admin/pages/{id}/publish | Authorized publish request, immutable revision, outbox, audit.
POST /v1/admin/pages/{id}/rollback | Publish compatible earlier revision as audited new action.
POST /v1/admin/media/uploads | Authorize scoped upload; return bounded signed upload policy.
GET /v1/admin/leads | Permission-filtered paginated lead list; sensitive fields minimized.
POST /v1/admin/leads/{id}/transitions | Validate stage transition, actor, reason, and conversion side effects.
POST /v1/admin/exports | Authorized asynchronous export with expiry and access audit.
POST /v1/privacy/requests | Verified-rights workflow; do not leak whether an email has an account.
POST /v1/webhooks/{provider} | Provider-specific signature verification, durable receipt, deduplication.
GET /health/live and /health/ready | Minimal liveness/readiness for operations; no public secrets or detailed topology.
Threat | Mandatory control
Account takeover | MFA for privileged users, secure recovery, rate limits, session revocation, suspicious-login handling, and no shared admin accounts.
Unauthorized record access | Server-side role and object-level checks on every endpoint, export, job, and download.
Script/content injection | Safe rich-text allowlist, context-aware escaping, sanitized embeds, CSP, and no owner-injected arbitrary JavaScript.
Request forgery | Appropriate CSRF protection for cookie-authenticated writes; restrictive CORS; origin validation where applicable.
Data exposure | TLS, encryption at rest where appropriate, protected backups, field/access minimization, secret isolation, and log redaction.
Malicious uploads | Quarantine, file limits, type validation, scanning, safe storage, and isolated processing.
Infrastructure abuse | Reverse proxy/WAF controls where justified, request budgets, private databases, patched systems, and protected admin routes.
Supply-chain compromise | Dependency locking, updates, scanning, license review, provenance/SBOM, and CI secret protection.
Module | Required end-to-end scope
Commerce | Product/variant catalogue, current prices, tax/fee policy, stock rules, cart, checkout, verified payment, order history, fulfilment, cancellation, refund, and reconciliation.
Bookings | Services/resources, availability, timezone, duration, buffers, temporary holds, capacity checks, confirmation, reminders, rescheduling, and cancellation.
Subscriptions | Plans, billing lifecycle, trials if used, renewal, failed-payment handling, cancellation, prorations where supported, entitlement changes, and refunds.
Customer portal | Account/profile, secure history, protected documents, support requests, preferences, and object-level access enforcement.
Courses/memberships | Enrollment, entitlement, content access, progress where needed, assessments/certificates only when actually implemented, and support.
Profile | Components and purpose | Tradeoff
Native small-site | Reverse proxy, web/API/worker as supervised processes, PostgreSQL, object storage, optional Redis. | Low orchestration overhead; team owns OS, process, patch, and recovery operations.
Docker Compose | Same services in pinned images with persistent volumes, health checks, resource controls, and external backup. | Repeatable deployment; single-host setup is not automatically highly available.
Managed containers | Managed application/container services plus managed database/storage where justified. | Less infrastructure labor; verify portability, pricing, region, and provider limitations.
Kubernetes | Helm-managed workloads, ingress, network policy, autoscaling, secrets integration, monitoring, and external/stateful data strategy. | Use only with scale/availability needs and a team capable of operating it.
Signal | Required examples
Availability | Public route and form endpoint checks from appropriate regions; admin/API readiness; DNS/TLS expiry.
Performance | Page field experience, API latency, error rate, database query time, connection pressure, cache behavior.
Jobs/integrations | Oldest pending job, retry/dead-letter rate, publication lag, notification backlog, CRM sync health.
Business | Accepted leads/orders/bookings, unusual drops, duplicate anomalies, unassigned leads, payment mismatches.
Privacy/security | Unexpected tag firing, export activity, authentication anomalies, rejected event fields, retention failures.
Cost | Compute, database, storage/egress, log volume, third-party calls, replay volume, and AI usage where enabled.
Test layer | Scope
Unit | Domain invariants, schema validation, consent eligibility, price calculations, transformations, and error handling.
Integration | Real test PostgreSQL, transactions, migrations, outbox, authentication, object access, and job retries.
Contract | OpenAPI/client compatibility, block schema versions, event schemas, CRM mappings, provider fixtures.
End-to-end | Owner editing, publication, public pages, forms, lead routing, optional payment/booking/portal flows.
Accessibility/visual | Keyboard/screen reader, zoom/reflow, templates, responsive behavior, content extremes, and image fallbacks.
Performance/resilience | Agreed peak load, slow clients, queue/provider failure, restart, database restore, and multi-instance cache refresh.
Security/privacy | Authorization, injection, uploads, secrets, PII leakage, consent denial/withdrawal, export and deletion.
Marketing measurement | Browser/server event mapping, attribution preservation, duplicates, platform diagnostics, and outcome reconciliation.
ID | Scenario | Expected result
EVT-01 | First visit before a choice, under the default blocking policy. | No nonessential analytics/advertising requests or stored identifiers.
EVT-02 | Accept analytics but reject advertising. | Approved analytics works; advertising destinations remain suppressed.
EVT-03 | Reject optional purposes, then submit a valid enquiry. | Enquiry accepted and routed; no forced advertising consent or forbidden retrospective replay.
EVT-04 | Pricing marker visible briefly, then one continuous qualifying second. | No early event; exactly one pricing_view per page_view_id after the rule is met.
EVT-05 | Click brochure but cancel/interrupt transfer. | download_click recorded when allowed; no false completed/read claim.
EVT-06 | Start form and enter private test values. | Form event allowed fields only; no values in dataLayer, analytics, error logs, or replay.
EVT-07 | Invalid form or server failure. | No generate_lead; useful accessible error and safe retry.
EVT-08 | Double click, client timeout, retry, worker restart. | One business lead/outbox event for the same idempotent action; destination retry uses stable identity.
EVT-09 | Browser and server Meta events for the same lead. | Current account diagnostics confirm correct deduplication contract.
EVT-10 | Client-side navigation, back/forward, reload, in-app browser. | Page-view policy consistent; no unintended double firing.
EVT-11 | Withdraw permission with queued optional events. | Future collection and ineligible queued sends stop according to policy.
EVT-12 | Lead later qualified/sold, then corrected/refunded where applicable. | Audited lifecycle events and supported destination adjustments; no repeated accidental conversions.
Phase | Indicative window | Exit deliverables
Discover and decide | Weeks 1–2 | Site brief, modules, journeys, data inventory, architecture decisions, acceptance criteria, and account ownership.
Design and foundations | Weeks 2–5 | Design system, templates, monorepo, environments, auth, database foundation, CI, and consent/event schemas.
CMS and public experience | Weeks 4–10 | Owner editing, blocks, blog, media, preview/publish/rollback, responsive public pages, SEO controls.
Business and measurement | Weeks 7–13 | Forms, leads, CRM, outbox, GA4/Ads/Meta integration, reporting, notification, privacy workflows.
Hardening and launch | Weeks 12–16 | Security, accessibility, load/recovery tests, migration, owner training, pilot, and launch evidence.
Advanced modules | Separate scope after core or parallel dedicated team | Commerce/bookings/portal/multilingual/AI with their own acceptance and operations.
Cost group | Include in the estimate
One-time delivery | Discovery, UX/design, implementation, content migration, copy/media, integration setup, testing, training, security review, and contingency.
Core recurring | Domain/DNS, hosting, database, storage/CDN/egress, backups, monitoring, support labor, and security maintenance.
Marketing recurring | Ad spend, campaign management, content production, creative, consent/tagging tools where paid, and reporting.
Communication | Transactional email, marketing email, SMS, WhatsApp, telephony, verification messages, and provider platform fees.
Optional modules | Payment fees, CRM/CMS licenses, search, replay, AI inference, video, scheduling, or commerce services.
Hidden/variable | Log growth, export volume, image transforms, API overages, failed-job retries, data transfer, tax, currency changes, and incident response.
Asset | Required handover evidence
Ownership | Domain/DNS, hosting, repository, CMS, analytics, ad accounts, payment/CRM, email domain, billing, and recovery access belong to the business.
Source and environments | Complete source, lockfiles, migrations, infrastructure code, deployment profiles, environment variable catalogue, and release manifest.
Content and data | Content export, media manifest, redirects, forms/revisions, consent configuration, business-data export procedure, and restore test.
Documentation | Architecture, API, event dictionary, privacy map, owner guide, deployment/rollback/restore/incident runbooks, and known limitations.
Training | Recorded or written task walkthroughs, owner acceptance tasks, support escalation, and secondary staff training.
Operations | Support owner, patch schedule, monitoring access, backup alerts, cost alerts, and agreed service objectives.
Configuration | Required implementation
Identity and ownership | experiment_id, experiment_version, site_id, owner, reviewer, hypothesis, business rationale, and expiry/review date.
Eligible population | Route, locale, device class where justified, and permitted acquisition cohort; eligibility must be determined before treatment. No sensitive-trait or precise-location targeting by default.
Treatments | Stable variant IDs; pinned page/block/form/chat revisions; allocation weights; fallback/control revision; compatibility and accessibility checks.
Measurement | One primary metric, denominator/randomization unit, conversion window, guardrails, baseline, minimum detectable effect, sample requirement, and named analysis method.
Runtime controls | Start/end timestamps, timezone, enrollment limit, mutual-exclusion namespace, consent requirements, kill switch, and signed configuration version.
Decision record | Counts, uncertainty interval, sample-quality checks, exclusions, analysis version, decision owner, reason, rollout evidence, and rollback target.
Field | Internal meaning and rule
utm_source | Referring platform or publisher from a controlled vocabulary. Preserve the received sanitized value and record any normalized mapping separately.
utm_medium | Controlled channel such as cpc, paid_social, email, referral, or qr; keep spelling consistent.
utm_campaign | Human-readable campaign name; do not use it as the only durable campaign key.
utm_id | Stable campaign identifier used for internal and approved reporting joins.
utm_content | Creative, placement, or link variant; distinct from the website experiment assignment.
utm_term | Paid-keyword or approved targeting label, not a captured visitor search or form answer.
utm_source_platform | Source/buying-platform metadata, maintained under the campaign naming standard.
Additional internal fields | Optional creative_format, marketing_tactic, campaign/ad/ad-set IDs, placement, partner/referral code, or QR code ID; explicitly map destinations.
Click identifiers | Genuine gclid, gbraid, wbraid, fbclid, and other explicitly activated provider fields; protected, purpose-bound, and never fabricated.
Data | Source and confidence | Required boundary
Connection IP | Server/reverse-proxy observation; may be a VPN, mobile gateway, corporate proxy, or shared connection. | Restricted security/approved matching use; not a person's identity or exact address.
Approximate country/region/city | Approved IP-geolocation lookup; accuracy varies and results may be absent. | Display source and accuracy radius where available; default coarse output, no street-level claim.
Device/browser context | Basic request information and approved browser APIs. | Coarse classes and declared purpose; no fingerprint, installed-font inventory, canvas hash, or hardware identity.
Language/timezone | Browser preference, selected locale, or voluntarily supplied value. | A hint, not proof of nationality, residence, location, or applicable law.
Browser coordinates | Geolocation API after the required browser permission and contextual request. | Store accuracy, timestamp, purpose and provenance; location is not necessarily GPS-derived or exact.
Entered location/contact | The visitor deliberately enters city/postal code, address, name, email, or phone. | Treat as declared until appropriately verified; keep in protected business records.
Context projection | Permitted contents by default | Excluded by default
Website SDK | Safe page/block IDs, approved event schema, permitted assignment, consent state needed by the client. | Raw IP, contact details, privileged notes, precise coordinates outside the explicit location control.
Chat response context | Current approved page/offer, selected language, visitor-selected topic, approved coarse service area, necessary conversation turns. | Full browsing history, raw IP, advertising click IDs, precise coordinates, unrelated CRM/customer data.
Lead-capture service | Explicitly submitted contacts, field provenance, permitted acquisition/assignment/location references, consent evidence. | Arbitrary hidden-field identity claims or inferred personal attributes.
Authorized human agent | Assigned conversation, relevant accepted contact details, eligible journey summary and service-area result. | Unrelated visitors; raw-IP or precise-location access unless separately authorized.
Analytics/ad adapter | Approved event names and safe identifiers; eligible destination-specific matching fields only. | Transcript text, answers, exact location, private notes and unrestricted URL parameters.
Stream | What it records | Storage/access and loss policy
Administrative audit | Content/config/role changes; deletion, approval, restore, export and privileged data access. | Durable append-oriented records; no sampling of critical actions; protected reader/export roles.
Business ledger | Lead/order/booking transitions, communications, consent evidence, integration delivery and corrections. | Transactional records and outbox; authoritative facts are not replaced by analytics.
Security telemetry | Authentication, denied access, abuse controls, suspicious changes and relevant network evidence. | Restricted security store; approved redaction, retention and alerting.
Operational telemetry | Request metadata, latency/errors, job health, database/cache behavior, deploys and recovery. | Structured logs/metrics/traces; bounded sampling/volume where justified.
Behavioral analytics | The consented event dictionary, experiment exposure and approved chat engagement. | Purpose-limited collector/warehouse; observation gaps and retention disclosed.
Conversation records | Actual messages and submitted chat fields needed for the support/lead purpose. | Protected conversation store, not a generic log sink or model-debug transcript archive.
Surface | Components and responsibility
Public website | ExperimentBoundary, consent-aware tracking SDK, AcquisitionContextProvider, LocationRequestButton, ChatLauncher, ChatCaptureForm, and safe error/fallback states.
Shared client libraries | Typed event schemas, URL sanitizer, permission selectors, route/section hooks, bounded context client, and independently tested redaction. No browser-exported secrets or database clients.
Owner experiments | Experiment list/builder, revision/variant preview, metric/traffic feasibility, conflict check, live status, result quality and promote/rollback.
Owner marketing | Campaign registry/link builder, attribution diagnostics, source-quality reports, connector freshness and conversion reconciliation.
Owner conversations | Inbox/assignment, approved fields/flows/knowledge, handoff, authorized context panel, privacy controls and transcript export.
Owner content | Trash without expiry, dependency preview, restore, separate purge request/approval, and per-post actor/revision history.
Owner governance | Audit Center, retention-by-data-class, access reviews, raw-data reveal approval, integrity/backup status and feature policies.
Entity/group | Minimum columns and constraints
experiments / experiment_versions | Scoped stable key, owner, status, eligibility, metric_definition_id, allocation_version, salt reference, start/end, config hash; immutable running definitions.
experiment_variants | Version/site FK, stable variant key, approved revision references, weight; unique per experiment version; no cross-site content.
assignments / exposures | Site, experiment version, permitted unit reference, variant, assigned/exposed timestamps, trigger/version; assignment uniqueness and exposure deduplication.
experiment_results | Analysis version, cohort bounds, sample and conversion counts, effect/interval, quality status, method, decision actor and evidence object.
campaign_links / acquisition_touches | Safe destination, received sanitized fields, normalized mapping/version, capture/eligibility times, consent reference; no raw query dump.
click_identifiers | Scoped touch/type, encrypted value where retained, original provenance, purpose, captured_at/expires_at; destination-restricted access.
request_contexts | Restricted client IP if justified, trusted-source/config version, coarse device/geo, created_at/expires_at; no analytics-wide join permission.
location_contexts | Location source, coordinates only if necessary, accuracy/unit, purpose, obtained_at/expires_at, consent reference, derived service-area result.
conversations / messages | Site, scoped session/account owner, bot version, status/assignee; messages with monotonic sequence and client idempotency key; encrypted/restricted content.
visitor_context_links | Conversation/lead/touch/assignment/location references, purpose/evidence, created_at, revoked_at, expiry; composite site-scoped FKs.
deletion_requests / approvals | Exact object/version manifest hash, human requester/approver, reason, expiry, hold decision, status; distinct approver by default.
deletion_operations / tombstones | Operation ID, initiator and executor, manifest, phase/outcomes, minimal preserved object reference; independent of deleted post row.
audit_entries / export_batches | Actor/action/resource/outcome, sequence, operation/request IDs, redacted change metadata, policy/config versions, digest/checkpoint references.
Endpoint or family | Contract and security rule
POST /v1/attribution/touches | Permitted sanitized capture; site/session resolved by server; idempotent per declared navigation/capture policy; returns scoped reference.
POST /v1/experiments/resolve | Only approved public configuration for eligible routes; cannot choose arbitrary arm or edit allocation. Server rendering can call the same internal use case.
POST /v1/experiments/exposures | Validates assignment binding/trigger; behavioral authority only; never creates a sale.
POST /v1/locations/resolve | Accepts a deliberate location request or manual selector; validates purpose/permission, source and freshness; returns minimal task result.
POST /v1/chat/conversations | Starts a scoped operational conversation with approved optional context; no bulk visitor-profile lookup.
POST /v1/chat/conversations/{id}/messages | Conversation authorization, idempotency, length/rate limits and attachment checks; API owns message order.
POST /v1/chat/conversations/{id}/leads | Explicit capture confirmation; reuses form/lead transaction, eligible attribution, audit and outbox.
POST /v1/chat/conversations/{id}/handoff | Authorized handoff with bounded context; distinguishes request from accepted agent assignment.
GET /v1/admin/visitors/{id}/context | Role- and purpose-projected context; sensitive reveals are separate permissioned actions with audit.
/v1/admin/experiments/{id}/... | Approved create/review/start/pause/complete/promote actions with expected versions and recorded actors.
POST /v1/admin/posts/{id}/trash or /restore | Human-authorized, reasoned, concurrency-checked, audited reversible lifecycle action.
POST /v1/admin/deletion-requests | Exact manifest and impact preview; scoped requester, reason, hold checks and expiry.
POST /v1/admin/deletion-requests/{id}/approve or /execute | Independent approval and fresh execution authorization; no arbitrary manifest substitution; idempotent operation.
GET /v1/admin/audit and POST /v1/admin/audit/exports | Redacted bounded read; stronger export permissions; expiry and audit of export/read as defined.
Frequency or trigger | Required owner action
Before a campaign | Validate generated URL, consent states, landing/variant message, test lead, CRM routing and conversion diagnostics.
During an experiment | Check sample quality, guardrails and implementation stability; do not promote from a transient p-value. Record changes and decisions.
Daily support operation | Review unassigned chats/leads, failed handoffs, delayed notifications and unresolved customer requests.
Manual content removal | Inspect impact, enter reason, confirm exact record; use trash first and independent approval for purge.
Annual content review | Review stale content and ownership; create human tasks. No automatic content removal or trash expiry.
Privacy or access change | Apply withdrawal/revocation to eligible queued work and context projections; process rights requests under the approved schedule.
Operational review | Inspect audit export/integrity, retention exceptions, role grants, raw-data reveals, provider/API changes and restore evidence.
Class | Required treatment | Release rule
Universal foundation | Public experience, owner CMS, content preservation, security, accessibility, technical SEO controls, safe forms, data governance, audit, recovery and ownership. | Required for each production site, adjusted only through a justified site-specific decision.
Growth-platform capability | Experiments, campaign builder, attribution, contextual chatbot, automation, segments/scoring, audience adapters, marketing reports and communication controls. | Specify and deliver in the comprehensive reusable platform profile. Activate real collection, sending or advertising only after approval, supported providers and tests.
Conditional business module | Commerce, courses, booking, subscriptions, marketplaces, community, publisher advertising, specialized portals and industry-specific services. | Every category receives an applicability decision. An activated module becomes mandatory end-to-end scope; an inactive module creates no claim of delivery.
Earlier gap | Controlling expansion | Evidence required
Visual marketing automation | Chapter 45 | Owner simulation, durable execution, cancellation and suppression tests.
Scoring and segments | Chapter 46 | Explainable rules, versioned scores, boundary cases and qualification separation.
Advertising audience lifecycle | Chapter 53 | Membership/exclusion changes, provider eligibility, revocation and reconciliation.
CMS editing safety and productivity | Chapter 44 | Autosave acknowledgment, recovery, edit conflicts, imports and release rollback.
Custom content collections | Chapters 44 and 63 | Approved schema builder, renderer bindings, migrations and access tests.
Email deliverability | Chapter 47 | Authentication headers, unsubscribe, bounce/complaint and suppression evidence.
Domain/DNS/account continuity | Chapter 57 | Business ownership, renewal alerts, change approval and recovery exercise.
Channel-specific attribution | Chapter 48 | Real provider callbacks, deduplication, provenance and CRM linkage.
Stage | Website assets | Useful business evidence
Discover | Helpful articles, social content, video, real location pages, earned references. | Qualified reach and eligible visits, not fabricated exposure histories.
Evaluate | Service detail, comparisons, demonstrations, case studies, calculators, FAQs and pricing. | Meaningful engagement, customer questions and decision-task completion.
Convert | Relevant landing page, accessible form, booking, checkout or explicit chat capture. | Durably accepted lead, verified booking or payment.
Follow up | Human response, permitted nurture, proposals, reminders and support. | Response time, qualification, attendance, win/loss reasons.
Retain and advocate | Onboarding, resources, service updates, support and honest review/referral requests. | Retention, repeat purchase, satisfaction and fulfilled customer obligations.
Node family | Supported examples | Essential boundary
Trigger | Accepted lead, explicit subscription, qualified stage, booking change, verified purchase, approved segment entry. | Trusted backend event or verified provider receipt; not an arbitrary browser claim.
Condition | Interest, locale, assigned team, consented channel, business stage, permitted segment. | Typed allowlisted fields; no raw SQL, arbitrary code or inferred sensitive traits.
Delay | Relative delay, business-hours wait, wait for reply, wait until a date. | Durable timer with expiry and timezone/DST handling.
Action | Assign lead, create task, send permitted template, request approved integration action, notify staff. | Recheck authorization, suppression, budgets and current business state.
Exit | Customer converted, opted out, replied, disqualified, workflow expired or manually stopped. | Cancel eligible pending actions and record the reason.
Channel | Required implementation | Do not claim
Email | Thread/message mapping, reply handling, delivery feedback, attachment controls and unsubscribe separation. | Every tracked open is a human read.
WhatsApp/SMS | Approved account/template setup, permitted recipient/channel purpose, verified callbacks, opt-out and delivery states. | A website consent toggle authorizes every message category.
Telegram | Explicit bot interaction, chat/account mapping, verified webhook or controlled polling, safe contact capture. | A bot can discover every visitor's phone number or private profile.
Voice | Provider call IDs, initiated/ringing/answered/ended states where supported, routing, missed-call workflow. | A tel: click proves a call connected or produced a qualified lead.
Web chat | Chapter 37 context projection, durable messages, lead transaction, handoff and model fallback. | A provider webhook IP is the website visitor's IP.
Planning area | Required operating decision
Search intent | Theme, relevant search demand, brand/non-brand policy, appropriate destination, exclusions and negative-keyword review.
Retail | Approved products, Merchant Center/feed health, current price/availability, shipping/returns, catalogue identifiers and transaction tracking.
Cross-channel automated campaigns | Business goal, asset groups, eligible URLs, feeds, exclusions, budget, brand controls and reporting limitations.
Video/display/discovery | Audience purpose, placement/format readiness, accessible creative, landing experience, brand safety and conversion meaning.
Lead-generation assets | Native form eligibility, privacy destination, fields, approved lead delivery, duplicate policy and CRM service level.
Level | Allowed scope | Additional safeguards
Reporting | Read campaign/account performance and diagnostics. | Read-only credentials, pagination/backfill, freshness, quotas and aggregate-data boundaries.
Measurement/audience delivery | Send approved conversions or eligible audience changes. | Destination field map, policy gate, deduplication, receipts, deletion/suppression and audit.
Campaign management | Create/edit/pause campaigns, budgets, creatives and targeting where supported. | Explicit spending authority, reviewed changes, bounded budgets, preflight, remote-state confirmation and rollback plan.
Capability | End-to-end requirements
Product discovery | Category/search/filter/sort, accessible variants, comparison or wishlists when selected, truthful availability and related products.
Cart/checkout | Server-priced lines, discounts, taxes/fees, shipping options, guest/account policy, validation, supported payment and retry-safe confirmation.
Inventory/fulfillment | Stock reservation, concurrency, oversell policy, warehouse/provider status, shipment tracking and partial fulfillment.
Returns/refunds | Eligibility, request, approval, receipt where required, partial/full refund, stock/access changes, provider reconciliation and customer communication.
Subscriptions | Trial/activation, renewal, failed payment, grace period, changes/proration, cancellation, entitlements and reconciliation.
Promotions | Valid dates, inventory/budget caps, eligibility, stacking, redemption limits, terms and abuse controls.
View | Required decisions and context
Executive | Valid outcomes, cost, realized value, qualification and response times, risks, freshness and unknown coverage.
Acquisition | Source/campaign/landing-page performance, spend, conversion quality, attribution assumptions and lag.
Content/SEO | Search visibility, indexing, useful engagement, content-assisted outcomes, quality backlog and review dates.
CRM/support | Assignment, response SLA, stages, lost/spam reasons, handoff and channel delivery.
Experiments | Assignment/exposure/outcome counts, uncertainty, guardrails, invalid/inconclusive status and decisions.
Operations/privacy | Collection eligibility, rejected fields, queue lag, connector health, rights tasks, audit integrity and recovery.
Site/audience context | Required decision before activation
Children or school students | Age-appropriate experience, guardian/consent requirements where applicable, restricted profiling/ads, limited public profiles and careful contact capture.
Health, care or counseling | Separation of public marketing from protected intake/records, tracking exclusions, provider contracts, escalation and qualified content review.
Finance, insurance, credit or legal services | Claim/licensing review, sensitive-input minimization, secure applications, eligible advertising and fair decision controls.
Employment, housing or other restricted ads | Actual platform category/targeting restrictions, lawful intake and transparent business criteria.
Global commerce/subscriptions | Taxes/fees, cancellation/consumer notices, cross-border processing and regional payment/marketing rules.
Community or public submissions | Moderation, reporting, takedown, rights, safety and access boundaries.
Failure | Required behavior
CMS read outage | Serve a tested last-known-good public snapshot when safe; no draft exposure.
CRM/email/ad API outage | Accept valid leads durably, queue eligible external effects and show delay.
Analytics/tag failure | Core navigation, forms and checkout continue without optional tracking.
AI/chat provider outage | Deterministic FAQ/form or human-contact fallback; no false lead confirmation.
Geo provider/permission failure | Unknown or manual location selection; no invented coordinates.
Queue/worker restart | Reconstruct eligible work from durable state with idempotent execution.
Audit exporter failure | Preserve local transactional evidence, alert on lag; stop high-risk actions when required guarantees fail.
Payment uncertainty | Reconcile trusted provider state; do not charge again or grant access from guesswork.
Module | Required business lifecycle and controls | Acceptance example
Retail and digital products | Product/variant, discovery, stock/access, cart, payment, fulfillment, returns, refunds, support and feeds. | Concurrent purchase, delayed payment and partial refund remain consistent.
Services and appointments | Service areas/resources, availability, buffers, holds, booking, reminders, rescheduling, cancellation and staff override. | Two users cannot reserve the last available capacity twice.
Events and webinars | Event revisions, capacity/waitlist, registration, payment if used, reminders, attendance evidence, recordings/rights and cancellation. | Canceled event stops reminders and handles refunds/access correctly.
Courses and memberships | Programme/enrollment, payment/entitlement, lessons, progress, assessments, certificates where implemented, support and expiry rules. | A refunded/revoked enrollment loses only the access required by policy.
Student/guardian experience | Age-appropriate onboarding, verified relationships where necessary, private records, consent, communications and limited profiling. | One guardian cannot access another student's records by changing an ID.
Subscriptions/SaaS | Trial, plan, usage/quotas, renewal, dunning, upgrades/downgrades, cancellation, invoices and entitlements. | A late billing webhook cannot restore access after an overriding verified cancellation without a defined rule.
Module | Required business lifecycle and controls | Acceptance example
Customer/support portal | Authentication, profile, case/order/document access, notifications, preferences, support and rights requests. | Object-level access applies to APIs, exports and signed downloads.
B2B sales/quotes | Organization roles, catalogues, negotiated prices, quote versions, approval, validity, acceptance and sales handoff. | Accepting a quote preserves its agreed price despite later CMS changes.
Partner/franchise multisite | Scoped staff, approved shared content, local overrides, domains, branch data, reporting and offboarding. | A branch can edit permitted local data without changing the global legal policy.
Directory/listings | Submission, verification, owner claim, categories, search/map, correction, expiry/publication and moderation. | An unverified claimant cannot take over a listing or see private contact data.
Marketplace | Seller onboarding, listings, availability, order flow, fees, provider-led payouts, disputes, reviews and fraud review. | Refund/dispute affects seller settlement and buyer access consistently.
Referral/affiliate/loyalty | Enrollment, referral evidence, attribution rule, reward eligibility, caps, fraud checks, payout/credit and reversals. | Self-referral or a refunded sale cannot create an unearned irreversible reward.
Careers/applications | Real jobs, accessible applications, private files, stage workflows, interview communication, retention and closed-job discovery handling. | Closing a job changes public availability without silently deleting retained applications.
Module | Required business lifecycle and controls | Acceptance example
News/magazine/resource publisher | Editorial roles, evidence/corrections, feeds, archives, search, media rights, subscription/paywall where used. | Draft/private content never leaks through RSS, search or chatbot indexes.
Publisher advertising/sponsorship | Ad inventory, approved provider, consent, performance/layout budgets, sponsored labels, unsuitable-content review and revenue reporting. | Ads cannot obscure controls, leak private context or shift critical content outside the approved budget.
Forum/comments/community | Member roles, post/report/moderation, appeals, spam controls, safe links/files, suspension and rights handling. | A removal/report action is attributable and cannot be used to expose private complainant details.
Reviews/testimonials | Authentic source, permissions, disclosure, fair moderation, correction and dispute workflow. | A fake/unauthorized review cannot enter structured data or campaign proof.
Podcast/video/media library | Episode/video metadata, accessible transcript/captions, licensing, player, feeds, downloads and playback events. | Revoked media is removed from public distribution without destroying retained lawful source records automatically.
Donations/fundraising | Real beneficiary, disclosures, amount/frequency, verified payment, receipt, refund/cancellation and accounting. | Recurring-donation cancellation stops future eligible charges and preserves receipt history.
Capability | Public/owner frontend | Backend and durable records

Commerce/feeds | Product editor, feed diagnostics, checkout/orders/returns when activated. | Catalogue/Price/Inventory/Order/Feed services; product, transaction, ledger and feed snapshots.
Governance | Asset register, privacy requests, audit, monitoring and release evidence. | PolicyService, AuditService, RightsOrchestrator, HealthService; decisions, ledgers and evidence references.
Use case | Representative route | Required contract
Save draft | PATCH /v1/admin/content/{id}/draft | Expected version, schema validation, acknowledged revision; conflict response.
Preview import | POST /v1/admin/imports/preview | Protected source file, mapping, dry-run manifest and errors; no side effects.
Publish release | POST /v1/admin/releases/{id}/publish | Pinned approved manifest, permissions, audit and invalidation jobs.
Simulate automation | POST /v1/admin/workflows/{id}/simulate | Synthetic fixtures, virtual clock, no production sends.
Publish/stop workflow | POST /v1/admin/workflows/{id}/{action} | Versioned approval, bounded effects, reason and run policy.
Explain score | GET /v1/admin/leads/{id}/score | Authorized safe contributions and rule version, not hidden sensitive features.
Preview segment | POST /v1/admin/segments/{id}/preview | Bounded typed query, permission filter, count/freshness and exclusions.
Reconcile audience | POST /v1/admin/audiences/{id}/sync | Current eligibility, approved destination, idempotent batch and safe receipts.
Unsubscribe | POST /v1/preferences/unsubscribe | Appropriate opaque proof; idempotent suppression, no forced login.
Request campaign change | POST /v1/admin/ad-changes | Explicit write scope, budget authority, preflight and remote-state tracking.
Export report | POST /v1/admin/reports/{id}/exports | Permission-filtered snapshot, expiry, safe formatting and audit.
Gate | Required proof | Blocking failures
Content and UX | Real content/claims, responsive/keyboard flows, working destinations and owner tasks. | Broken primary journeys, inaccessible essential actions, fabricated claims.
SEO and paid readiness | Route/index policy, rendered crawl, approved goals/accounts, test destinations and diagnostics. | Accidental production blocking, invalid destinations, known misleading conversion goals.
Data and privacy | Field maps, choices, suppression, sensitive-data negative tests and rights workflow. | Unauthorized collection/disclosure or bypassed withdrawal.
Security and integrity | Scoped access, secret/upload/API tests, durable transactions and protected audit. | Data leakage, unauthorized changes or unaudited destructive action.
Reliability and operations | Load results, real restore, alerts, runbooks and named support. | Lost acknowledged leads, untested recovery or unowned critical alerts.
Integrations | Real permissions, validated receipts, retry/correction and reconciliation. | Mock-only claims, unverified consequential writes or provider-policy blockers.
Phase | Exit deliverables
Discover and approve | Site brief, activation matrix, business/data decisions, sitemap, account ownership and cost assumptions.
Design foundations | Design system, content/event/policy schemas, auth, database, audit and deployment skeleton with tests.
Content and owner operations | CMS safety, collections, imports, releases, blogs/media, SEO controls and owner task evidence.
Business and measurement | Durable forms/chat, attribution, experiments, CRM and trusted conversion feedback.
Growth operations | Automation, communication, segments/audiences, feeds/reporting and selected campaign-management adapters.
